D. AIIMS PATNA 
 
2.7 STATUS OF CONSTRUCTION WORK 
 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
2.7.1 It is a proven fact that delay in any construction activity leads to unnecessary 
additional costs and time extensions. Inflation, abrupt ending of contracts, arbitration cases, 
disputes etc further increases the cost of any delayed project. Systemic analysis at the 
inception of any project and regular monitoring can easily control all these factors  and 
minimize the chances of delay. Sadly, the Ministry and the Institute remained ignorant of both 
time and cost deadlines for the completion of the project as envisaged for AIIMS Patna. 
 (Para 5.10 of the Report) 
 
Action Taken 
 
2.7.2 In continuation of t he position explained in para 5.9, it is clarified that there has been 
no cost escalation as the project is within the original Cost estimates of approved DPR. The 
total Tender cost under four packages in this project was Rs.554.28 Cr., against which 
Rs.554.47 cr. has been incurred  and the project is almost completed which is well within the 
deviation limit. 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
2.7.3 The Committee observes with anguish that AIIMS Patna has failed to realize the 
implications of this sluggish rate of construction activities and is of the view that the reasons 
cited by AIIMS Patna for the construction delays are inexcusable and could have been easily 
avoided had the Institute been alert and committed for the Project. Moreover, the argument of 
AIIMS P atna that professional expertise in constructing hospital building is missing in the 
country is not tenable and bereft of fact and logic. 
(Para 5.12 of the Report) 

103 
 
 
2.7.4 The Committee is dismayed to note that no project management tool/strategy has been 
employed for construction of an AIIMS like institution that is intended to be the beacon of 
light for the tertiary healthcare sector in the State. A PERT or Program Evaluation Review 
Technique Chart is one such project management tool that helps in better a nalysis of the 
resources and the timeline of any project. In the absence of any evaluation technique, the 
Committee is really sceptical about the completion of the construction work under the 
remaining packages. The Committee recommends that a special project monitoring system for 
AIIMS Patna must be devised to track the progress of construction activities undertaken by 
the Institute so as to ensure better project management. The Committee strongly recommends 
that the work under Package II, III and IV must be completed on time with no further delays. 
 (Para 5.13 of the Report) 
Action Taken 
 
2.7.5 The observations/ recommendations of the Committee have been noted for future 
compliance.  All Project Packages are completed. 
 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
2.7.6 The Committee is at pains to take into account the improper estimation of quantities of 
items pointed out by CAG in its report. Surprisingly, the deviation is upto 173% in case of 
AIIMS Patna which clearly indicates that the requirement of different items  had been under-
assessed and unrealistic assessment of the site conditions were made. The green building 
norms and the Energy Conservation Building Code (ECBC) should have been implemented 
before the beginning of the construction activities. The Committee is highly distressed at the 
inadequate technical estimation made before the construction of AIIMS Patna. 
(Para 5.16 of the Report) 
 
2.7.7 The Committee, therefore, recommends that the Ministry should make a detailed 
technical and realistic assessment befor e setting up of other new AIIMS under PMSSY by 
carrying out proper field and site surveys and the errors while constructing AIIMS Patna 
should not be repeated. 
 (Para 5.17 of the Report) 
Action Taken 
 
2.7.8 It is submitted that there is no overall deviatio n in the project. The up to date payment 
made in PKG -1 is Rs.159.46 crore in final bill against tender cost of Rs. 160.69 crore. The 
final bill has not been paid so far. In PKG-II, the up to date payment made is Rs.306.03 crore , 
including escalation cost o f Rs.25 crore (approximately) against the tender cost of Rs.284.70 
crore. There is no overall deviation in package -1 & a minor deviation in package -due to 
escalation of Rs.25.00 crore. PKG-III-total cost incurred Rs.42.23 crore as against tender cost 
of Rs.42.60 cr. which well within the permissible limit and there is no deviation.  PKG-IV-
Total cost incurred Rs.46.74 crore as against tender cost of Rs.66.29 crore. The work is 
almost completed and there is no deviation. 

104 
 
 
2.7.9 The deviation of 158 -173% repor ted by CAG in its report is regarding certain 
individual items of work and not overall deviation. The deviation in individual items is due to 
less quantity taken in the estimate against the G.F.C. drawing issued by DOPR for 
construction of the buildings.  However, there are also savings in work quantity in number of 
items which is either not required or less quantity is required as per GFC drawing. It is also 
mentioned here that the contract agreement itself has provision of deviation limit of 30% or 
100% depending upon the item in superstructure/ substructure. 
 
2.7.10 It is also mentioned here that presently the construction is being taken up in the EPC 
mode wherein extensive problem of large deviation/escalation would get systematically 
obviated. Further, t he system of assigning the construction work to executing agencies on 
turnkey basis with empowerment to handle all contractual matters at their end has been 
introduced. The executing agencies have been mandated to deploy and use Project 
Management Software  to bring about improved project management at their end. The 
Ministry has also developed an online dashboard to facilitate a closer and more effective 
monitoring. The functional AIIMS have been empowered with greater delegation of powers 
in construction, procurement upto Rs.5 crore and also in administrative matters. The 
procedure for procurement of medical equipment has been simplified and made more efficient 
with greater delegation and empowerment of the Procurement Support Agency (PSA) which 
is entruste d with the work of procurement of medical equipment.  All new AllMS  are now 
being designed keeping in view the "GRIHA" norms for green buildings.  The status of 
different packages is provided as under:- 
 
Pkg-II 100% Completed 
Pkg-III 100% Completed 
Pkg-IV 100% Completed 
 
E. AIIMS RAIPUR 
 
2.8 PROCUREMENT OF EQUIPMENT 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
2.8.1 The Committee is of the further view that quality of medical equipment needs to be 
looked into because defective equipment result in frequent break down of health care services. 
Given the importance of medical equipment in diagnosis as well as treatment and the high 
price involved in purchase of several hi -tech equipment, it is imperative that the equipment 
are put to their best use for optimum utilization of re sources. Non-installation of equipment is 
indicative of lackadaisical approach of the Institute in letting them remain idle which is mere 
wastage of resources at the cost of taxpayers‘ money. The Committee, accordingly, 
recommends the Institute to take str ict action against the manufacturers for delayed delivery 
or delivery of defective equipment. The Committee, further, recommends that Institute should 
either exchange the defective equipment with new equipment or scrap the contract with the 
manufacturers by seeking adequate legal redressal. The Committee recommends for an action 

105 
 
taken status report on the specific observations of the CAG in context of procurement of  
medical equipment and recommends the Ministry to take strict action against the errant 
officials. 
 (Para 6.29 of the Report) 
Action Taken 
 
2.8.2 Due care is taken to ensure that there is no defective equipment delivered/ supplied by 
vendor.  In case of breakdown of any equipment, the warranty or the CMC clause takes care 
of the interest of patien t care. The Institute ensures strict action if any defective equipment is 
supplied or scrap the contract with the manufacturer by seeking adequate legal redressal.  
Some of the equipment as mentioned in the CAG report could not be installed as necessary 
civil infrastructure was not available at that time. The progress regarding installation of these 
equipment is monitored very closely at Institute level as well as in MoHFW. The delay due to 
non-availability of site and the reasons thereof have already been brought out against the reply 
of Point No 6.12.  Some defective equipment supplied through PSA, has been notified to M/s 
HLL so as to take necessary action against respective vendors as per the tender terms and 
conditions. However, the equipment as mentioned  in the CAG have since been installed and 
put in use. 
2.8.3 It is informed that AIIMS Raipur is an institute established under the PMSSY scheme 
of the Ministry of Health and Family Welfare (MoHFW), where patients receive treatment. 
The institute purchases medical equipment, ensuring adequate precautions are taken during 
the procurement process. Additionally, repair and maintenance of the equipment are 
performed in accordance with the tender conditions and contract. If any vendor fails to meet 
these conditions, the institute takes strict action. 
2.9 MEDICAL EDUCATION 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
2.9.1 The Committee feels that clinical training is a vital part in a medical education for 
becoming a successful doctor. The Committee is, however, apprehensive ab out the quality of 
the clinical training imparted at the Institute to the undergraduate and postgraduate students 
due to shortage of faculty at the Institute as many key Departments are being handled by a 
single faculty. The Committee wonders how an Instit ute is accomplishing the objective of 
imparting clinical training to the medical students with limited number of faculty. The 
Committee also notes that seats under many PG courses are lying vacant which raises a 
question mark about the quality of clinical training facilities available at the Institute. Poor 
clinical training hampers the professional pursuit of doctors which amounts to risking the 
lives of the patients. The Committee, therefore, recommends to the Institute to ensure 
adequate faculty at the I nstitute for imparting quality medical education and clinical training 
to the students aspiring to be well-trained doctors. 
 (Para 6.36 of the Report) 
 
 

106 
 
Action Taken 
 
2.9.2 So far as teaching of MBBS and BSc Nursing is concerned, it is to inform that AIIMS  
Raipur is having sufficient Faculty members and other required infrastructure from the 
beginning of these courses as Faculty members of Pre -clinical and Para -clinical and Broad 
Specialty departments were already available. The teaching and training of the se students are 
carried out without any compromise on quality and adequacy. Eight batches of MBBS and 
B.Sc. (Hons.) Nursing have successfully completed their course. A good performance of 
passed out students of AIIMS Raipur by way of securing seats in AIIM S PG and NEET 
examinations reflects and supports this fact.  AIIMS Raipur has also started Postgraduate 
courses (MD/MS/MDS) in 28 specialities and M.Sc. Nursing in 4 subjects. Super speciality 
courses (DM/MCh) have also started in 23 specialities. 
2.10 STATUS OF OPERATION THEATRE (OTS) AND INTENSIVE CARE UNIT 
(ICUS) 
 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
2.10.1 The Committee notes with concern that only 26 beds out of total sanctioned strength 
of 100 beds are functional at the Institute. ICUs are used to provide sp ecialized rehabilitative 
care to patients as they start to recover from their critical illness or injury. The Committee 
believes that bed scarcity in ICU may result in refusal of admission to initial patients, thereby, 
depriving them the benefit of ICU services. The Committee observes that there are as many as 
35 ventilators for 26 ICU beds, indicating that 9 ventilators are lying unused in the ICUs. The 
Committee feels that the value of the equipment starts depreciating immediately after they are 
procured and non-functioning of the equipment results in loss of their value. The Committee, 
therefore, recommends to the Institute to increase the number of beds so that critically ill 
patients are not refused the service of ICUs due to scarcity of beds and focus on optimum 
utilization of resources available. 
 (Para 6.41 of the Report) 
Action Taken 
 
2.10.2 50 ICU beds are functional. AIIMS Raipur efficiently uses its available resources and 
equipment to provide care to the patients. However, patients are referred o nly for step down 
hospital care after diagnosis and essential treatment and stabilisation is done, so that more 
critical cases requiring immediate care may be accommodated.

2.12 STATUS OF CONSTRUCTION WORK 
 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
2.12.1 The Committee is of the view that underutilization of the funds during years 2011 -
2017 is the main reason behind the delay in completion of the project and cost overruns. The 
Committee expresses its concern over delay and cost overruns in different projects of various 
packages. Time and cost over runs are undesirable in projects under construction. The delays 
in construction project have delayed delivery of health servi ces. The Committee observes that 
delay in completion of the project was on account of poor planning, unrealistic scheduling of 
the dates, lack of communication between the contracting agency and the authorities 
concerned at the Institute and poor monitoring. 
 (Para 7.14 of the Report) 
Action Taken 
 
2.12.2 It is submitted that the payment to various agencies by Ministry / AIIMS 
Bhubaneswar was made as per actual completion of work at site. However, the delay in 
completion of project has not delayed the deliv ery of health services which was also 
interrelated to the joining of faculty, recruitment of other staff, installation of equipment‘s etc. 
In February 2014, the hospital was inaugurated and since then, the Hospital services have 
been started in the handed over areas in a phase-wise manner. 
2.13 STATUS OF OPERATION THEATRES AND WARDS/BEDS 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
2.13.1 The Committee is surprised to note the wide difference in the cost per bed in these two 
Institutes that have the same functional capac ity and the same number of beds. The 
Committee takes a serious note of this high cost incurred by AIIMS, Bhubaneswar especially 
when the other AIIMS like Institutions, providing the same tertiary health care facilities, have 
submitted relatively less cost. The Committee, therefore, recommends the Institute to limit its 

108 
 
redundant expenses. The Committee demands that the Institute should submit a status note on 
how the cost per bed was calculated and the reasons behind this high cost so that cost per bed 
may be calculated for other AIIMS also on the same parameters. 
 (Para 7.49 of the Report) 
 
Action Taken 
 
2.13.2 The Institute calculated the cost per bed at the time of furnishing information taking 
into consideration all recurring/non -recurring expenditure di vided by number of functional 
beds which was 560 at that point of time. The variation has occurred due to dividing 560 
functional beds with the total expenditure whereas AIIMS, Raipur might have calculated by 
dividing the total number of sanctioned beds i. e. 960. The numbers of functional beds have 
been taken as a lot of more expenditure will be incurred to make functional the remaining 
beds. Further, the recurring expenditure of AIIMS, Bhubaneswar may be higher in 
comparison to AIIMS, Raipur as the Hospita l services has started here earlier and the patient 
load is higher in comparison with AIIMS, Raipur.  The basis on which the Committee has 
determined that the cost per bed is significantly high remains unclear. It is important to note 
that AIIMS Bhubaneswar  is currently operating with 1,019 functional beds and 351 
temporary beds, bringing the total functional capacity to 1,370 beds.  If the cost per bed is 
recalculated based on this total capacity, the overall cost per bed may come significantly 
lower.

2.14 STUDY VISIT OF THE COMMITTEE 
 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
2.14.1 The Committee notes that the shortage of specialists is a major problem in all the 
AIIMS like Institutions and Government Medical Co lleges. The Committee again emphasizes 
the need to develop special packages and incentives for attracting the specialists to serve in 
this part of the region. 
  (Para 8.66 of the Report) 
Action Taken

the Government Medical Colleges falls in the domain of the State Governments.  This issue 
was taken up with the States in the Quarterly Review Meeting (QRM) with the States held on 
19-20th September 2019. Innovative in centives and special packages developed by some of 
the States like H.P. and Rajasthan were shared with the States so that the States may consider 
and adopt suitable packages.   
 
 
 
 

109 
 
2.15 SELECTION OF PROJECT CONSULTANTS/CONTRACTORS 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
2.15.1 The Committee is of the view that the careless selection of the Project Consultants did 
more harm than good and caused unwarranted delays in the construction activity. The 
Ministry failed to make a comprehensive assessment of selection of proj ect-
consultants/contractors before the execution of the scheme. The CAG has also raised concerns 
regarding the award of consultancy work to PSUs on nomination basis in its Report. The 
Committee also notes that M/s HSCC and M/s HLL were appointed as consult ants on 
nomination basis under the garb of Rule 176 of General Financial Rules (GFR) 2005. This 
rule is always read with the section 1.5.3 that states the selection by direct negotiations/ 
nomination is permissible in terms of Rule 176 of General Financial  Rules, 2005 only under 
exceptional circumstances and such selection may normally be restricted to a financial ceiling 
of Rs ten lakhs. The Committee notes that the cases did not fall under any of the special 
circumstances stipulated in the section 1.5.3. 
(Para 9.9 of the report) 
Action Taken 
 
2.15.2 In addition to the above para the following pertinent points are mentioned below:  
 
The Project Consultants selected for six AIIMS are as under: 
 
Sl. AIIMS Project Consultant 
1. Bhopal M/s Enarch 
2. Bhubaneswar M/s Hospitech 
3. Jodhpur M/s Hospitech 
4. Patna M/s Enarch 
5. Raipur M/s Mukesh Associates 
6. Rishikesh  M/s SMEC 
 
2.15.3 HLL and HSCC were selected as Project Consultants after following due procedure as 
per extant rules, regulations and guideline s. It may be observed that all these companies are 
reputed ones in their respective fields. However, their abandonment of project at crucial time 
did create problems in continuation of works. From perusal of the para, it is noted that the 
Hon‘ble Committee is referring to reading of Rule 176 of GFR 2005 in consonance with Rule 
1.5.3. of Manual of Policies and Procedure of Employment of Consultants prepared by 
Ministry of Finance, Department of Expenditure in August, 2006. The attention of Hon‘ble 
Committee is being sought with the last para of the preface issued by the then Secretary, 
Department of Expenditure along with the Manual wherein it has been clearly stated that the 
manual is to be taken as generic guideline and to be interpreted broadly.  Moreover,  Section 
1.5.3 (b) explicitly permits appointment on nomination basis is done in situations where 
timely completion is of utmost importance. In view of the above the appointment of PSU, on 
nomination basis does not violate the Rule 176 along with Section 1 .5.3 of the Manual of 
Policies and Procedure of Employment of Consultants is followed. 

110

RECOMMENDATIONS/OBSERVATIONS IN RESPECT OF WHICH REPLIES OF 
THE GOVERNMENT HAVE NOT BEEN ACCEPTED BY THE COMMITTEE 
 
A. AIIMS BHOPAL 
 
3.1 STATUS OF FACULTY/NON FACULTY STRENGTH 
 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.1.1 The Committee is dissatisfied with the vacancy positions in different Departments in 
AIIMS, Bhopal, where out of 305 sanctioned faculty posts, 164 posts are lying vacant. The 
Committee is apprehensive that for an AIIMS like Institution, such a gap in workforce would 
adversely affect the functional capacity of the Institute. The Committee calls upon the 
Institute to accelerate its administrative procedure for filling up the vacant posts within a time 
bound manner. The Ministry also needs to closely monitor the efforts made by the Institute in 
this regard. The Committee, also, recommends that the vacant posts should be filled on 
regular basis within a specified period of time. Measures must be taken for fi lling up the 
vacant posts on permanent basis to ensure continuity and smooth functioning of the Institute. 
(Para 2.27 of the report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.1.2 Several policy interventions have been introduced by the Ministry to improve 
availability of candidates  for various faculty positions.   In addition, the Ministry has also 
taken some policy initiatives for bringing about improved availability of trained HR at 
National Level at large.   
 
3.1.3 Measures to improve faculty strength in the new AIIMS were also d iscussed in the 
meeting of the Central Institute Body (CIB) held on 13.08.2018, 10.12.2018, and 24.01.2019.  
CIB advised that the regular recruitment will be done at Institute level while combined 
contractual recruitment will be done by AIIMS, New Delhi thr ough walk in -interview twice 
in a year for selecting students passing from premium medical institutes/INIs, in addition to 
the recruitment done by individual AIIMS.  It was also decided in the CIB to allow 
temporarily, the new AIIMS to fill up the senior le vel posts by selection of candidates at one 
level below in case the post could not be filled up due to no -availability of suitable 
candidates. Measures to improve faculty strength in the new AIIMS were further deliberated 
in the 4 thmeeting of the Central I nstitute Body (CIB) on 27.07.2019 and various steps 
suggested to strengthen the faculty position have been mentioned. 
 
3.1.4 Further, the updated input is as follows: Out of 12 Nursing Faculty sanctioned for 
AIIMS, Bhopal, 10 faculty positions have already  been filled. The post of Professor -cum-
Principal, Nursing College, AIIMS, Bhopal has already been advertised. Subsequent to the 
pending court cases, the result has not been declared. Further, it is also submitted that out of 
305 sanctioned posts of facult y, as on date 232 posts have already been filled up and the 
process for further filling up of the vacant posts has already been initiated.  

111 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.1.5 The Committee notes the progress made by filling up of 232 posts out of 305 
sanctioned faculty posts in AIIMS Bhopal, but the Committee expresses concern  that 73 
vacancies still persist and that some appointments (e.g. N ursing Principal) are delayed 
due to  procedural hurdles. In order to secure full, sustained staffing, the Committee 
recommends that AIIMS Bhopal, in coordination with the Ministry  should further 
undertake followings actions for strengthening faculty and non - faculty of AIMS 
Bhopal: (a) set a deadline for completing all remaining regular recruitments and 
declaring results of p ending cases; (b) convert existing contractual and one -level-below 
appointments into permanent posts as soon as eligibility is met; (c) institute a j oint 
quarterly review mechanism to monitor progress against recruitments targets; and (d) 
actively partner with premier medical institutes to broaden the candidate pool, thereby 
ensuring uninterrupted teaching, research and patient-care functions. 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.1.6 The Committee expresses its concern that out of total sanctioned strength of 3 776 non 
faculty posts in AIIMS, Bhopal, only 792 posts have been filled up, including 424 posts that 
have been actually outsourced. In Committee's opinion non -faculty staff is the backbone of 
any medical institution in terms of its successful and smooth fu nctioning. With such a wide 
gap in the in -position strength against the sanctioned non faculty posts, it would have been 
extremely difficult to manage the day to day functional equilibrium of various Departments of 
the Institute in an integrated manner. Th e Committee, therefore, recommends for expediting 
the appointment process of non -faculty staff on a regular basis so that all the Departments 
have adequate man power to cater to all aspects of patient care. The Committee is of the 
considered opinion that a  centralized recruitment process for filling up of non -faculty posts 
would address the entire non faculty shortage issue and also ensure a transparent recruitment 
process. The Committee strongly recommends the Ministry to initiate this process on an 
immediate basis. 
(Para 2.28 of the report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.1.7 The observation / recommendation of the Hon‘ble Committee have been noted for 
future guidance.  In pursuance of the observation of the Hon‘ble Parliamentary Standing 
Committee, the Central Institute Body (CIB) in its 3rd meeting held on 24.01.2019 advised all 
AIIMS to conduct recruitments for Non -faculty (Technical) cadres on centralized basis by 
applying the following measures:  
 AIIMS Delhi will conduct centralised recruitment for all AIIMS in case of  Nursing 
Cadres. 
 For rest of technical cadres (19), the six new AIIMS will do the centralized 
recruitment by suitably dividing the cadres amongst themselves. 
 
3.1.8 Following is the current status of the recruitment: Out of total 510 sanctioned posts 
of Se nior Nursing Officer (Group B), As on date, 72 Sr. Nursing Officers are in position 

112 
 
against the DR Quota of 127 Posts and the recruitment process for remaining 55 posts vacant 
will be initiate . Total 383 Nursing Officers have been promoted to the post of S r. Nursing 
Officers against 75% quota.  Out of Total sanctioned 1326 posts of Nursing Officers, as on 
date, 1190 Nursing Officer is in position. Further 85 posts out of the remaining vacant posts 
of Nursing Officer have been proposed to be surrendered so that new posts may be created as 
per the Standard Staffing Pattern. 
 
Direct Recruitment for Group 'B' (285 Posts) :- As on date, 194 employees are in 
position in Group -B posts.  Recruitment process against the posts earmarked for 
Direct Recruitment has been i nitiated. Various Group ‗A‘ & ‗B‘ posts on deputation 
basis are being advertised and recruitment is under process.  
 
Recruitment of Faculty positions on Direct Recruitment basis (305 posts):  As on date 
total 232 faculty posts are filled and the recruitment  of remaining 73 posts is under 
process.  
 
Recruitment of Tutor/Clinical Instructor (33 posts): The proposal for surrender of 
these posts is lying with Ministry under SSP. 
  
3.1.9 Out of 12 Nursing Faculty sanctioned for AIIMS, Bhopal, 10 faculty positions  have 
already been filled.  The post of Professor -cum-Principal, Nursing College, AIIMS, Bhopal 
has already been advertised. Subsequent to the pending court cases, the result has not been 
declared. Recruitment for the post 10 Nos. Of Assistant Professor on  contractual basis in 
various Departments at  AIIMS Bhopal is under process. The advertisement has been 
published. Out of total 630 posts, as on date, 360 are filled and the recruitment of remaining 
vacant posts is under process. Total 192 posts have been advertised through CRE. 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.1.10 The Committee observe lackadaisical approach towards filling up of non-faculty 
posts at AIIMS Bhopal, particularly in the nursing cadre . Accordingly, the Committee 
remains deeply concerned towards the persistent large number of vacancies and the 
over-reliance of the institute on outsourcing. Since the 3776 sanctioned non-faculty posts 
are not filled up through regular recruitment, core institutional functions are being 
compromised. The Committee, therefore, reiterates its recommendation for the Ministry 
to operationalize a robust, time-bound recruitment mechanism for all technical and non-
technical non -faculty posts, ensuring uniformity and transparency across AIIMS. The 
Committee further recommends that posts proposed to be surrendered be reviewed with 
caution to avoid under -staffing in critical areas, and that all direct recruitment and 
deputation-based appointments currently under process be concluded, at the earliest. 
 
 
 
 

113 
 
3.2 SPECIALITY/SUPER SPECIALITY DEPARTMENTS 
 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.2.1 The Committee is deeply anguished to take into account with the present status of the 
faculty at the Specialty and Super Departments at the Institute. Only 17 posts have been filled 
up against 100 sancti oned posts in the Super Specialist Departments and 83 posts are lying 
vacant. Not only that, many Departments such as Cardiology, Cardiothoracic and Vascular 
Surgery, Psychiatry, Nephrology, Surgical Oncology, Endocrinology & Metabolic Diseases, 
Pediatric Surgery and Nuclear Medicine are being run by a single faculty. The Committee is 
surprised to observe the sorry state of functioning of AIIMS, Bhopal and concludes that with 
such huge vacancy, the Institute cannot achieve its objectives of providing tertiary level health 
care services to the people. The Committee believes that there is an urgent need to address the 
deficiency in the manpower at the Specialist and Super Specialist level. The Committee, 
therefore, strongly recommends to the Institute to fill up the vacant posts at the earliest. The 
Committee also recommends that AIIMS Bhopal should explore innovative means like 
special packages/incentives to attract Specialist/Super Specialists from premier institutes in its 
different Departments to ensure better functioning of AIIMS Bhopal. 
(Para 2.33 of the report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.2.2 The observation / recommendation of the Hon‘ble Committee has been noted for 
future guidance.  From the side of the Ministry several policy interventions have been 
introduced to improve availability of candidates for various faculty positions.   In addition, the 
Ministry has also taken some policy initiatives for bringing about improved availability of 
trained HR at National Level at large. Measures to improve faculty strength in the new AIIMS 
were also discussed in the meeting of the Central Institute Body (CIB) held on 13.08.2018, 
10.12.2018, and 24.01.2019. CIB advised that the regular recruitment will be done at Institute 
level while combined contractual recruitment will be don e by AIIMS, New Delhi through 
walk in -interview twice in a year for selecting students passing from premium medical 
institutes/INIs, in addition to the recruitment done by individual AIIMS.  It was also decided 
in the CIB to allow temporarily, the new AIIM S to fill up the senior level posts by selection 
of candidates at one level below in case the post could not be filled up due to no -availability 
of suitable candidates. 
 
 
3.2.3 The details of department wise posts are enclosed. Out of total 510 sanctioned posts of 
Senior Nursing Officer (Group B), As on date, 72 Sr. Nursing Officers are in position against 
the DR Quota of 127 Posts and the recruitment process for remaining 55 posts vacant will be 
initiated. Total 383 Nursing Officers have been promoted to t he post of Sr. Nursing Officers 
against 75% quota. Out of Total sanctioned 1326 posts of Nursing Officers, as on date, 1190 
Nursing Officer is in position. Further 85 posts out of the remaining vacant posts of Nursing 
Officer have been proposed to be surrendered so that new posts may be created as per the 
Standard Staffing Pattern. 
 

114 
 
Direct Recruitment for Group 'B' (285 Posts) :- As on date, 194 employees are in 
position in Group -B posts.   Recruitment process against the posts earmarked for 
Direct Recruitment has been initiated.  Various Group ‗A‘ & ‗B‘ posts on deputation 
basis are being advertised and recruitment is under process.  
  
Recruitment of Faculty positions on Direct Recruitment basis (305 posts):  As on date 
total 232 faculty posts are filled and th e recruitment of  remaining 73 posts is under 
process.  
  
Recruitment of Tutor/Clinical Instructor (33 posts):  The proposal for surrender of 
these posts is lying with Ministry under SSP.  
  
3.2.4 Out of 12 Nursing Faculty sanctioned for AIIMS, Bhopal, 10 fa culty positions have 
already been filled.   The post of Professor -cum-Principal, Nursing College, AIIMS, Bhopal 
has already been advertised. Subsequent to the pending court cases, the result has not been 
declared. Recruitment for the post 10 Nos. Of Assista nt Professor on contractual basis in 
various Departments at AIIMS Bhopal is under process. The advertisement has been 
published.  Out of total 630 posts, as on date, 360 are filled and the recruitment of remaining 
vacant posts is under process. Total 192 p osts have been advertised through CRE.  Senior 
Resident – 134 vac ant out of 377 sanctioned posts. Junior Resident – NIL out of 421 
sanctioned posts. 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.2.5 The Committee acknowledges the Ministry‟s ongoing recruitment efforts and 
policy interventions aimed at addressing faculty shortages across AIIMS institutions, 
including AIIMS Bhopal. However, the Committee remains concerned that despite these 
measures, critical gaps persist in the Specialty and Super Specialty Departments, many 
of which continue to function with only one faculty member. The Committee, therefore, 
recommends that AIIMS Bhopal, in close coordination with the Ministry, implement a 
targeted recruitment plan specifically for Super Specialty Departments, with defined 
timelines and deliverables. The Committee also urges that the previously suggested 
measures such as offering special incentives, permitting lateral entry of experienced 
specialists from leading institutes be operationalized without delay.  
 
3.3 MEDICAL EDUCATION 
 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.3.1 The Committee has observed that the admissions in MBBS and B.Sc Nursing Courses 
are being done with an intake of 100 and 60 students respectively at AIIMS, Bhopal. The 
number of admissions in MD/MS courses in different disciplines has also increased. Out of 33 
sanctioned seats for January, 2018 session, 24 admissions have already been done. The 
Committee was given to understand by the Ministry that two seats in Departments of 
Microbiology and one seat in Immunohemato logy and Blood Transfusion were sanctioned in 
January, 2018. However, utmost to the surprise of the Committee, none of the seats in these 

115 
 
two Departments have been filled up yet. Further, in Anatomy and Paediatrics Departments, 
three seats have been sancti oned for each Department and only one seat in each Department 
has been filled up. The Committee, while expressing its serious co ncern, strongly, 
recommends the Ministry to evaluate the reasons for non -filling up of the seats and undertake 
corrective measur es to fill up the vacant seats strictly in a time bound manner so that the 
intended benefit to the beneficiaries be made available and the scarce resources, that have 
immense opportunity costs, are used in a judicious manner. The Committee, further, 
recommends to the Institute to expedite the process of starting new courses within a stipulated 
time so that students do not suffer due to non -availability of any medical Department in the 
Institute. 
(Para 2.35 of the report) 
Action Taken

is as below:

Anaesthesiology (2 sea ts in Anaesthesiology & 1 seat in Pathology). 3 PDCC 
candidates have joined the Institute. 
 Super specialty DM course in Neurology and Pulmonary Medicine started in the 
Institute in January 2019 with 2 seats in Neurology and 2 seats in Pulmonary Medicine 
were advertised. 3 candidates have joined so far. 
 Super specialty courses in M.Ch. in Burns & Plastic Surgery and in Neurosurgery 
started in January 2019 with 2 seats in Burns & Plastic and 2 seats in Neurosurgery. 
All 4 students have joined. PhD program was  started in AIIMS Bhopal in January 
2019 and 17 candidates have joined.

remaining vacant have to be explored through a coordinated exercise conducted at all India 
level. This is not a phenomena specific for AIIMS Bhopal.

Further Recommendation 
 
3.3.5 The Committee notes  significant progress in increasing student intake across 
various courses at AIIMS Bhopal, including MBBS, B.Sc Nursing, MD/MS, and the 
successful initiation of numerous new PDCC, DM, M.Ch, and PhD programs. This 
demonstrates a proactive approach to expanding educational and healthcare capacities. 
However, the continued vacancy in certain super-specialty departments like Cardiology, 
Nephrology, and  Surgical Oncology remains a concern for the Committee. While the 
Ministry attributes this to an all -India phenomenon, it is crucial to further analyses and 
make detailed assessment by the concerned authority and take appropriate step for 
fulfilling up vac ant post in various department s. Moreover, if any specific factors at 
AIIMS Bhopal contribute to these vacancies, in order to develop targeted strategies in 
coordination with AIIMS Delhi, AIIMS Bhopal must address these gaps, ensuring 
timely recruitment of sanctioned seats and optimal utilization of resources, particularly 
in high-demand and specialized departments. 
 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.3.6 The Committee is of the strong view that for a medical or nursing college, it is 
imperative to have more exposure of medical cases for students to attain clinical skills in their 
respective fields. The Committee is highly apprehensive as to how the Institute will 
accomplish the objective to impart training to the PG students, MD/MS, PhD students with 
inadequate faculty and limited OPD. The Committee is also skeptical of the clinical training 
of the first batch of MBBS& Nursing students who have completed their internship this year. 
Since the Institute had huge number of vacancies in various departments, it is obvious that 
students would not have been given adequate clinical training as part of their curriculum. This 
is not a good indicator for AIIMS, Bhopal for meeting the expectation of high medical 
education from an institute of national importance. The Commi ttee has reasons to apprehend 
that inadequate training of medical and nursing students will only produce partially skilled 
and half trained doctors in the country that would, in fact, become a liability rather than assets 
of the country. Therefore, in orde r to ensure the prospective future of aspiring doctors and 
nurses, the Institute must strive towards providing all the requisite resources for the students 
to help them to hone their skills adequately during their academic years so that they acquire 
desired level of knowledge during their stay in AIIMS. 
(Para 2.40 of the report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.3.7 The observation / recommendation of the Hon‘ble Committee have been noted for 
future guidance. The faculty who joined in the year 2012 (MBBS 1 st Prof. Subjects) when the 

117 
 
clinical posting does not start in 1 st year of MBBS, took efforts to provide quality education. 
Departments and beds in hospital gradually increased and faculty in the year 2013 and 
subsequently, joined to ensure that education of MBBS students is not compromised. 
 PDCC, DM, M.Ch, Ph. D courses have started at AIIMS Bhopal. 
 MD/MS seats have been increasing since January 2017 at AIIMS Bhopal. 
 
3.3.8 It is submitted that number of beds and clinical material have increased substantially 
along with increase in faculty strength, which increased the clinical exposure and learning to 
the students of AIIMS, Bhopal at par with other Institute of National Importance. 
 
i. In the year of 2017 total number of faculties was 53 and as on date (12/02/25) 249 
faculties are working at AIIMS Bhopal  
ii. In the year of 2017 number of MD/MS courses were running in 03 departments and as 
on date (12/02/25) MD/MS courses running in 25 departments 
iii. MDS Course is running in two departments 
 
 
3.3.9 The institute adopts a cutting -edge c urriculum that integrates theoretical knowledge 
with extensive clinical exposure, ensuring that students receive a well -rounded medical 
education. The faculty comprises experienced and qualified professionals dedicated to 
mentoring and guiding students. In  the National Institutional Ranking Framework (NIRF) 
2024, AIIMS Bhopal was ranked 31 st among medical colleges in India. This recognition 
reflects the institute's dedication to maintaining high standards in medical education and 
research. 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.3.10 The Committee appreciates  positive steps taken to address the  Committee‟s 
concerns regarding inadequate clinical exposure and faculty shortages, notably through 
a substantial increase in faculty numbers (from 53 in 2017 to 249 as of 12/02/25 ), 
expansion of MD/MS courses to 25 departments, and the commencement of PDCC, DM, 
M.Ch, and PhD programs. The improved National Institutional Ranking Framework 
(NIRF) ranking to 31st in 2024 further supports the claim of enhanced educational 
quality. Whil e these are commendable achievements demonstrating a commitment to 
providing a "well-rounded medical education," the Committee further recommends that 
the Ministry should provide specific metrics, such as patient -to-student ratios, bed 
occupancy rates, and  a breakdown of surgical cases or specialized procedures students 
are exposed to across various departments. Such data would offer concrete evidence of 
adequate clinical material and validate the claim of "extensive clinical exposure," 
ensuring that the qu alitative improvements translate into demonstrably high levels of 
practical skills for all medical and nursing graduates. 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.3.11 The Committee, however, appreciates the efforts made by the Institute in expanding 
their medical  education services. The Committee feels that paramedical education and 
training should also be included in the medical education services at AIIMS, Bhopal as the 

118 
 
available requisite infrastructure can help produce a competent technical workforce in 
different clinical fields. The Committee, therefore, recommends to the Ministry to 
contemplate about introducing the paramedical education and training courses at AIIMS, 
Bhopal on an urgent basis to address the problem of shortage of technical manpower in 
clinical fields and optimize the utilization of infrastructure of the hospital set up. 
(Para 2.41 of the report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.3.12 In this regard, Central Institute Body (CIB) has been constituted by Ministry of Health 
& Family Welfare to look into the matter s of common concerned of all AIIMS. This body is 
in process of formulating recommendations in this regard. However, the Institute is also 
actively pursuing commencement of Para Medical Training/ Courses to address the problems 
of shortage of technical manp ower in clinical fields and to optimise the utilisation of 
infrastructure of the Hospital setup.

Further Recommendation 
 
3.3.14 The Committee commends AIIMS Bhopal for initiating paramedical (Allied 
Health Sciences) courses and expanding nursing education programmes, including t he 
launch of B.Sc., M.Sc., and Ph.D. level courses. This marks a significant step toward 
addressing the shortage of trained technical manpower and optimizing the use of 
available infrastructure. However, the Committee recommends that the Ministry and 
the I nstitute should further strengthen these efforts by expanding the range of Allied 
Health Science  disciplines offered, increasing seat capacity based on projected 
healthcare needs, and ensuring timely faculty recruitment for these programmes. The 
Committee also recommends the establishment of a dedicated Allied Health Sciences 
faculty or school within AIIMS Bhopal for better academic oversight, curriculum 
development, and integration with clinical services. A long -term roadmap for Allied 
Health Science educa tion expansion should be taken up on fast track mode to produce 
timely and  adequately competent technical work force in various clinical fields. Such 
steps would make the hospital infrastructure capable of providing world class 
healthcare besides opening o pportunity for employment -generation through expansion 
of Medical field. 
3.4 OPERATION THEATRES 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.4.1 The Committee is concerned to note that the waiting time in surgical procedures varies 
from one week to six months due to l imited availability of operation theatres at present. The 
Committee is of the view that AIIMS, Bhopal was established to provide immediate medical 
care to the nearby population who rush to AIIMS, Delhi or other adjoining facilities for 
specialized care. Th erefore, long waiting time at AIIMS, Bhopal for the patients requiring 
surgeries would defeat the very purpose of having an AIIMS like Institution in the State. It 
would further increase the out of pocket expenditure of those who cannot afford high medical  

120 
 
expenses. The Committee, therefore, recommends to the Institute to expedite the construction 
of OTs and make them fully functional. The Committee would also like to be apprised of the 
number of surgeries being conducted (both major and minor) per day per OT to ensure 
optimum utilization of the existing functional OTs. 
(Para 2.43 of the report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.4.2 The details of Surgeries conducted (both major and minor) by AIIMS, Bhopal is as 
under: 
 
Month/Year Major Surgery Minor Surgery Total Surgery 
2019 4624 9471 14095 
2020 2674 3003 5677 
2021 Nov 4823 3941 8764 
Total 12121 16415 28536 
 
All the 27 Major OTs including 24 Modular OTs and 8 Minor OTs are fully functional at 
AIIMS Bhopal. Please be apprised that the waiting time is not because of non -
operationalization of Operation Theatres but due to the significantly higher number of patients 
coming to AIIMS Bhopal. 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.4.3 The Committee acknowledges the Ministry‟s clarification that all 27 Major OTs 
(including 24 Modular OTs) a nd 8 Minor OTs at AIIMS Bhopal are now fully 
functional, and appreciates the substantial volume of surgeries being conducted 
annually. However, the Committee is worried that despite the operational readiness of 
OTs, long waiting periods for surgical proced ures persist due to high patient load. The 
Committee, therefore, recommends that AIIMS Bhopal undertake a comprehensive 
audit of OT utilization, manpower adequacy, and scheduling efficiency to identify 
systemic bottlenecks to overcome the issue of long wai ting time for surgeries at AIIMS 
Bhopal. In this regard, the Institute should consider extending OT hours, recruiting 
additional surgical staff, and streamlining pre -operative processes to im prove output . 
The Committee also urges the Ministry to explore th e feasibility of establishing satellite 
surgical centres or partnerships with nearby government hospitals to offload elective 
surgeries and reduce waiting times. 
3.5 STATUS OF INTENSIVE CARE UNITS AND TRAUMA/EMERGENCY 
FACILITIES 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.5.1 The Committee expresses its deep concern to view that for an institution like AIIMS, 
Bhopal the facilities for intensive care units are inadequate. Though the existing units are 

121 
 
functional with optimal equipment, there is scope for expansion in t erms of separate ICU for 
different Departments like Cardiology, Medicine, etc. The Institute has partially started 24x7 
Trauma and Emergency services. However, there are just two faculty for Trauma and 
Emergency center which is inadequate to provide dedica ted trauma/emergency facilities and 
to deal with mass casualties. A lot more needs to be done by AIIMS, Bhopal as a tertiary level 
medical care hospital to cater to the victims of increasing road accidents and other trauma 
cases. The Committee would like to emphasize that the Institute should have a well developed 
infrastructure and a better equipped set up with state of art medical facilities to deal with 
trauma cases. The Committee, therefore, recommends that the Institute should work towards 
enhancing it s spheres/perception of healthcare services to stand out as the most efficient 
healthcare provider in Madhya Pradesh. The Institute needs to increase ICUs and trauma 
facilities on a priority basis so that it becomes the best referral centre in the entire S tate for 
Trauma/Emergency Care. 
(Para 2.45 of the report) 
 
Action Taken 
 
3.5.2 The observation / recommendation of the Hon‘ble Committee has been noted for 
future guidance.Trauma and Emergency services are fully functional at AIIMS, Bhopal and 
working 24x7. Total 137 bedded COVID & Non Covid ICU is functional at AIIMS, Bhopal.  
As on today, there are 12 Faculty Members in the Trauma and Emergency and there are 78 
beds including 20 ventilator supported beds and Trauma and Emergency is functional 
24x7x365 days with 3 OTs dedicated to Trauma and Emergency. 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.5.3 The Committee notes the  significant progress in enhancing trauma and 
emergency services at AIIMS Bhopal, particularly with the increase to 12 faculty 
members and 78 functional be ds, includi ng 20 ventilator -supported beds.  The overall 
Intensive Care Unit ( ICU) bed count has increased to 137. A breakdown of the se beds 
into specialized units like Trauma, Cardiology, ENT, Neurology, Urology, etc.,  address 
the targeted functionality a nd the specific need for specialized critical care. This 
specialization optimizes patient care by providing tailored environments and expertise 
for complex medical conditions. The Committee further recommends that the separate 
ICUs should be expanded to ot her remaining specialisations also and their numbers in 
Urology and Trauma should be adequately increased for taking specialised critical 
healthcare.  
 
3.6 DIALYSIS FACILITIES 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.6.1 The Committee expresses its dissatisfaction  that there are only two dialysis machines 
at the hospital. The Committee also notes huge shortfall in manpower resources especially in 
the Nephrology Department. The Committee, therefore, recommends that the Institute should 

122 
 
strive to increase the number of dialysis machines as well as the requisite manpower to 
upgrade their Nephrology Department for effective health care service delivery. 
(Para 2.49 of the report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.6.2 AIIMS Bhopal is making all sincere efforts to improve the overall situat ion in the 
Nephrology department. As desired by the Committee, Faculty posts have been advertised in 
the Department of Nephrology vide Advertisement No. Admin/AIIMS/ Bhopal/2019/2917 
dated 21-01-2019 to meet  the requirement. As on today, there are 11 dialy sis machines fully 
functional in departments of Nephrology, Paediatrics and ICU. 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.6.3 The Committee appreciates the efforts made by AIIMS Bhopal to strengthen its 
Nephrology services, including the increase in the number of functi onal dialysis 
machines from two to eleven across Nephrology, Paediatrics, and ICU. However, the 
Committee emphasizes that enhancing equipment capacity must be matched with 
adequate and specialized human resources to ensure quality service delivery. In its 
reply, the Ministry stated that the faculty posts have been advertised in Department of 
Nephrology in 2019, however, the status of recruitment through that advertisement and 
the present status of vacant posts should have been informed to the Committee. The  
Committee further recommends that the Institute should expedite all p ending 
recruitment of qualified nephrologists, dialysis technicians, and nursing staff dedicated 
to renal care. It also urges the Ministry to support the development of a dedicated Renal  
Care Unit with integrated diagnostic, therapeutic, and training facilities.  
3.7 MERGER OF BHOPAL MEMORIAL HOSPITAL & RESEARCH CENTRE 
(BMHRC) AND AIIMS, BHOPAL 
 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.7.1 The Committee welcomes the positive response of the Minis try w.r.t. the merger of 
BMHRC with AIIMS Bhopal. The Committee also appreciates the concurrence of Director, 
BMHRC and the Department of Health Research to this merger proposed in public interest. 
The Committee is of the considered view that at present th ere is duplication of resources and 
services being offered in various medical fields leading to non -optimal utilization of high cost 
equipment and medical services. The BMHRC caters to a substantial population of gas 
tragedy victims and their dependents, i t may continue to do the same subsequent to the 
merger. BMHRC is well equipped to train paramedical workforce which can be produced by 
the hospital easily if the requisite faculty is made available. The Committee feels that merger 
of both hospitals would h elp in attracting and retaining medical faculty. The Committee feels 
that merger of BMHRC with AIIMS, Bhopal would increase scope of teaching and training 
avenues for doctors and students. The Committee, therefore, lends its full support to the 
merger of b oth Institutes for the benefit of the society. The Committee, therefore, 
recommends that Department of Health & Family Welfare should work towards chalking out 

123 
 
necessary course of action for bringing BMHRC under its purview by merging it with AIIMS 
Bhopal. While doing so, the Department of Health & Family Welfare can look into utilizing 
the huge set up of BMHRC, Bhopal for putting in place a dedicated resource centre for both 
medical faculty and paramedical staff in order to have paramedical/clinical techni cians 
workforce in various fields. The synergy arising out of the merger of BMHRC with AIIMS, 
Bhopal would result in optimal utilization of the existing infrastructure and resources. The 
Committee, therefore, strongly recommends that administrative hurdles towards the merger of 
both the institutes should be removed as early as possible. 
(Para 2.60 of the report) 
 
3.7.2 The Committee, further, recommends that in order to protect the interest of doctors of 
BMHRC, the recruitment rules which are different from  AIIMS (which happens to be an 
autonomous Institution governed by a separate Act) be retained in the transitory phase till 
complete merger is done so that the promotional avenues of the BMHRC doctors are 
protected. No fresh recruitment should be done under  BMHRC rules and the cadre of serving 
doctors be treated as a dying cadre for smooth transition of the merger process. The 
Committee recommends for time bound action in this matter and would like to be apprised of 
the progress made within three months of presentation of this Report. 
(Para 2.61 of the report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.7.3 It was decided to constitute a Core Group under the Chairmanship of Director, AIIMS, 
Raipur and Director, AIIMS Bhopal in connection with the proposal of merger of Bhopal 
Memorial Hospital and Research Centre (BMHRC), Bhopal with AIIMS, Bhopal. The Core 
Group has submitted its report on the 1 st October, 2018. PMSSY has sent the report to 
Department of Health Research with the request to consult all the stakeholders including 
employees/doctors association and confirm acceptability of the recommendations made in the 
Report. Further, action will be taken in the matter on acceptance of the recommendation by all 
stakeholders. The recommendation of the DRPSC in the para was shared with DHR f or 
necessary action.  However, DHR has decided not to merge BHMRC with AIIMS Bhopal. 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.7.4 The Committee recapitulates the positive response of the Ministry w.r.t. the 
merger of BMHRC with AIIMS Bhopal before the Committee during it s discussion on 
the subject. The Committee, therefore, reiterates its strong conviction that the merger of 
BMHRC with AIIMS Bhopal is in larger public interest and would ensure optimum 
utilization of resources, better faculty retention, and expanded traini ng and teaching 
opportunities, particularly while continuing to cater to the needs of gas tragedy victims. 
While noting that the Department of Health Research has decided against the merger, 
the Committee urges the Department of Health and Family Welfare  to revisit this 
decision in consultation with all stakeholders, keeping in view the long -term benefits for 
patients, medical faculty, and students. The Committee, therefore, recommends the 

124 
 
Department of Health and Family Welfare  to devise  a concrete roadmap  and time -
bound plan for resolving administrative hurdles and ensuring that the merger, along 
with protection of service conditions of existing BMHRC doctors during the transition, 
in the interest of public health and medical education. 
 
B. AIIMS RISHIKESH 
3.8 STATUS OF FACULTY/NON FACULTY STRENGTH 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.8.1 The Committee observes that faculty position in AIIMS, Rishikesh has improved, 
however the Committee strongly feels that the present strength of faculty is insufficient for a 
premier tertiary care institute. This has adversely impacted the quality of medical services and 
standards of education being imparted by the Institute. The Committee, thus, recommends to 
the Institute to expedite the process of recruitment and take immediate  steps to avoid 
unnecessary administrative delays in faculty recruitment. 
(Para 3.34 of the report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.8.2 The observation/recommendations of the Hon‘ble Committee have been noted for 
future guidance. Measures to improve faculty strength in th e new AIIMS were also discussed 
in the meeting of the Central Institute Body (CIB) held on 13.08.2018, 10.12.2028, 
24.01.2019. CIB advised that the regular recruitment will be done at Institute level while 
combined contractual recruitment will be done by A IIMS, New Delhi through walk -in 
interview twice a year for selecting students passing from premium medical institutes/INIs, in 
addition to the recruitment done by individual AIIMS.  It was also decided in the CIBs to 
allow temporarily, the new AIIMS to fill  up the senior level posts by selection of candidates 
at one level below in case the post could not be filled up due to non -availability of suitable 
candidates. To ensure more number of candidates appearing for the interview, CIB also 
advised in the meetin g held on 10/12/2018 that the institute may convene the Standing 
Selection Committee meeting at the mentor Institute or any city near Institute. 
 
3.8.3 From the side of the Ministry several policy interventions have been introduced to 
improve availability of candidates for various faculty positions. In addition, the Ministry has 
also taken some policy initiatives for bringing about improved availability of trained HR at 
national level at large. Institute is continuously advertising vacant posts of faculty i n various 
departments. However, recently two advertisement were advertised by the Institute and was 
cancelled due to revision and implementation of new reservation roster is approved by the 
Competent Authority new advertisement of vacant posts will be publ ished. As on date total 
sanctioned posts of faculties are 305 against which 214 are working. Advertisement for 
recruitment of balance post is being done time and again. 
 
 

125 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.8.4 The Committee acknowledges the efforts made by AIIMS Ri shikesh and the 
Ministry to enhance faculty recruitment, including the steps initiated through the 
Central Institute Body (CIB) and the ongoing policy interventions. However, the 
Committee expresses the  concern that despite repeated advertisements, approxi mately 
one-third of the sanctioned faculty posts remain vacant, and recent recruitments were 
delayed due to the revision of the reservation roster. The Committee, therefore, 
recommends that AIIMS Rishikesh must urgently finalize and operationalize the revised 
reservation roster and ensure that the pending faculty recruitment advertisement is 
issued without further delay. To avoid recurring procedural setbacks, the Committee 
further advises instituting a streamlined and time -bound recruitment protocol. Regul ar 
biannual recruitment cycles, as envisaged by CIB, must be strictly adhered to, and the 
progress should be reviewed quarterly by the Ministry. Further, the Committee would 
like to have a status report on efforts taken by  AIIMS Rishikesh and the Ministry 
regarding the operationalization of revised reservation roster and latest recruitment 
advertisement issued towards filling up of sanctioned posts of faculties. 
 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.8.5 The Committee underlines the need to augment the Specialty  and Super Specialty 
workforce at the Institute. The Committee observes that in 16 Departments the posts for 
Professor are lying vacant. Further, four Departments i.e. Physical Medicine & Rehabilitation, 
Transfusion Medicine, Gastroenterology and Biochemis try are being handled by a single 
faculty. The Committee would like the Institute to seriously introspect the issues of faculty 
and staff shortage and take necessary actions on a priority basis. The Committee feels that 
having an experienced workforce is a bsolutely necessary not only for providing best medical 
services to the patients but also for enhancing the practical expertise of the resident doctors. 
The Committee is of the considered view that adequate incentives should be offered at Super 
Specialty l evel to attract faculty at senior level from premier institutes like AIIMS, 
Delhi/JIPMER/PGI etc. for strengthening the healthcare services in the Institute. 
(Para 3.37 of the report) 
 
3.8.6 The Committee also notes that while on the one hand the Institute  has announced its 
plans for expansion, it has not been able to recruit even the sanctioned strength. The 
Committee strongly recommends that additional posts beyond the initially projected posts 
should be contemplated only when the existing sanctioned facu lty is recruited to the extent of 
almost 100% and there is optimum utilization of the existing infrastructure Any addition in 
faculty/Non faculty posts should be done only after ensuring that there is substantial 
enhancement in patient load. 
(Para 3.38 of the report) 
 
 
 

126 
 
Action Taken 
 
3.8.7 The observation/ recommendations of the Hon‘ble Committee has been noted for 
future guidance. However, all 18 speciality Departments and all 17 Super Speciality 
Departments are functional at AIIMS Rishikesh.  Transfusion M edicine and Biochemistry 
have more than one faculty now. All efforts are being made to fill up all vacant posts. 
 
Departments  Sanctioned  Filled up  Vacant 
PMR 4 2 2
