Noted and co ntinuous efforts are being made to fill up the vacant post by advertising 
periodically. 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.8.8 The Committee takes note of the Ministry‟s and AIIMS Rishikesh‟s ongoing 
efforts to strengthen the Specialty and Super Specialty Departme nts, including progress 
in departments such as Gastroenterology, Transfusion Medicine, and Biochemistry. 
However, the Committee is concerned that critical vacancies still persist in key 
departments such as Physical Medicine & Rehabilitation (PMR) and Bioch emistry, and 
that faculty recruitment overall continues to lag despite repeated advertisements. The 
Committee, therefore, reiterates its recommendation that AIIMS Rishikesh urgently 
prioritize the filling up of all senior faculty positions , especially Prof essors, through 
time-bound recruitment drives, while exploring targeted strategies such as inter -
institutional deputation, lateral entry of experienced specialists, and incentivized 
appointments from leading institutes like AIIMS Delhi, JIPMER, and PGIMER.  
Further, the Com mittee emphasizes that any plan  for institutional expansion must be 
accompanied by demonstrable improvements in faculty strength, infrastructure 
utilization, and patient load.  
3.9 MEDICAL EDUCATION 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.9.1 The Committee observes that there are new courses being offered at the Institute viz. 
PhD, MSc Nursing, DM/M.Ch and there has been a increase in the number of seats in existing 
courses as also the number of courses being offered at the Institute. The Committe e 
recommends that enhancement of the PG seats at the Institute should be done only after 
ensuring that AIIMS, Rishikesh meets at least the minimum benchmarks as per MCI norms 
applicable to other medical college throughout the country. The Committee does no t accept 
the argument of the Institute that it is governed by the AIIMS Act and hence the minimum 
bench mark as per MCI norms are not applicable to it. This argument puts a question mark 
over the quality of learning and clinical experience given to graduat es passing out of regional 

127 
 
AIIMS. The Committee is of considered opinion that graduates and post graduate passing out 
of AIIMS, Rishikesh should be equal to other medical graduates passing out of premier 
Institutes of the country. The Committee is of the c onsidered opinion that autonomy as per 
AIIMS Act was given only with respect to AIIMS, Delhi which has always exceeded the MCI 
benchmarks in every single aspect. However, the Committee is extremely concerned that the 
reference to the AIIMS Act and conseque nt autonomy available to regional AIIMS by virtue 
of this Act is being audaciously misused to dilute the medical education standards in a very 
blatant manner with the justification that the Institute meets US/UK standards. The 
Committee, therefore, recommends the Ministry to ensure that autonomy must not be used to 
dilute educational standards under any circumstances. The Committee is distressed at the 
medical education standards of the new AIIMS and is of the opinion that vacant PG seats in 
these Institutes are a reflection of lower standards of medical education. 
(Para 3.46 of the report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.9.2 The observation / recommendation of the Hon‘ble Committee has been noted for 
future guidance. The Institute have been sensitized in this regard.  The o bservation 
/recommendation of the Hon‘ble Committee have been noted for future guidance & we are 
trying to implement all suggestions. 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.9.3 The Ministry's response, stating that the Committee's observations have been 
"noted for fut ure guidance" and that the Institute has been "sensitized" and is "trying 
to implement all suggestions," lacks specific details or a concrete action plan to address 
the Committee's serious concerns regarding the potential dilution of medical education 
standards at AIIMS Rishikesh. The Committee's core apprehension centres on the 
perceived misinterpretation and misuse of AIIMS  Act autonomy to justify not adhering 
to MCI /NMC benchmarks, thereby raising questions about the quality of learning and 
clinical exp erience. Therefore, the Committee strongly recommends that the Ministry 
provide a detailed, time -bound action plan outlining how AIIMS Rishikesh will be 
brought into full compliance with MCI /NMC norms for faculty -student ratios, 
infrastructure, and clinica l exposure, particularly for postgraduate seats, without 
compromising the autonomy under the AIIMS Act , but rather enhancing, t he quality of 
medical education. 
3.10 OPERATION THEATERS (OTS) AND TRAUMA & EMERGENCY CENTER 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.10.1 The Committee is dismayed at the sorry state of affairs with regard to patients getting 
surgical procedures done after long waiting time. The Committee observes that at present only 
13 operation theatres are functional at AIIMS, Rishikesh due to which th ere is a long waiting 
period for the surgical procedures to be done at the Institute. The Committee observes that in 
Urology, Neurosurgery, and Burns & Plastic Departments, there is two months waiting time 

128 
 
and in general surgery, there is 45 days waiting t ime for the procedures to be done besides six 
months for ENT. The Committee feels that such instances of long waiting time due to non -
availability of operation theatres defies the very purpose of setting up of AIIMS like 
institution as patients have to fac e hardships. The Committee finds the reasons for long 
waiting time at AIIMS, Rishikesh are inadequate infrastructure and constraints even after six 
years of its operationalization. The Committee recommends for optimum utilization of the 
existing OT infrastructure. The Committee also recommends for a comparative analysis with 
AIIMS Delhi regarding the number of major/minor surgeries per OT per day. Despite these 
lacunae the Institute is aiming for setting up of advanced centres at exorbitant costs without 
getting the basics right. The Committee recommends that optimum functional status of the 
originally conceived infrastructure be achieved and resources be utilized for achieving this 
objective instead of just focusing on rapid expansion. 
(Para 3.56 of the report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.10.2 The observation/recommendation of the Hon‘ble Committee has been noted for future 
guidance. There was some delay in construction work related to setting up of Modular OTs. 
However, as on date 54 OTs including 25 Major OTs, 29 Mino r OT/procedure rooms are 
fully functional. A comparative study with AIIMS Delhi to evaluate utilization of OTs will be 
carried out. OTs were non -functional temporarily during COVID -19 pandemic, but now all 
OTs are fully functional, which will help in reducing waiting time for patient‘s surgery. 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.10.3 The Committee appreciates the progress made by AIIMS Rishikesh in 
operationalizing a total of 54 Operation Theatres, including 25 Major and 29 Minor 
OTs, which marks a significant impr ovement in surgical infrastructure. However, the 
Committee emphasizes that full functionality must translate into tangible reductions in 
patient waiting times across all surgical departments. The Committee,  therefore, 
reiterates that AIIMS Rishikesh conduc t and submit, without delay, a detailed 
comparative analysis with AIIMS Delhi on OT utilization efficiency, including the 
number of surgeries (major and minor) performed per OT per day.  The Committee  
further recommends that a performance audit be undertake n to assess scheduling 
practices, manpower deployment, and procedural turnaround times to ensure optimum 
utilization of available OT infrastructure. The Committee reiterates that a ny future 
expansion plans must be substantiated by due justification evident with supporting data 
showing improved usage of existing facilities and reduced surgical backlogs.  
 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.10.4 The Committee notes that AIIMS, Rishikesh is not prepared to manage huge number 
of casualties in case of any disaster  due to non -establishment of trauma center. The 
Committee, therefore, recommends that AIIMS, Rishikesh management must expedite the 

129 
 
construction work for establishment of Trauma center for treatment of casualties in case of 
disaster, as Uttarakhand is prone to disasters. 
(Para 3.57 of the report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.10.5 The obse rvation / recommendation of the  Committee has been noted for future 
guidance. It is submitted that the Trauma Building is fully functional with the capacity of 50 
beds. Helipad is also made functional in AIIMS Rishikesh, which is helping in managing and 
receiving patients for any disaster, in cooperation with the State Government. Heli Emergency 
Medical Services (HEMS) has been started for transporting critical patients to AIIMS 
Rishikesh.  
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.10.6 The Ministry's response details significant progress in addressing the 
Committee's concerns regarding disaster preparedness at AIIMS Rishikesh, with the 
Trauma Building now fully functional with 50 beds, the helipad oper ational, and the 
commencement of Heli Emergency Medical Services (HEMS). This proactive approach 
to establishing critical infrastructure and services for trauma and disaster management 
is commendable, especially given Uttarakhand's susceptibility to natura l disasters. The 
Committee, however, recommends that the Ministry of Health and Family Welfare, in 
tandem with Ministry of Home Affairs, must now focus on conducting regular, large -
scale multi -agency disaster drills and simulations involving local and stat e emergency 
services, as well as other relevant stakeholders. The Committee also desires that the 
Ministry should make introspection to specifically test the hospital's capacity in 
managing "huge numbers of casualties" in recent disaster in the states and accordingly 
refine coordination protocols to further  enhance preparedness and response capabilities 
for future contingencies. 
 
C. AIIMS JODHPUR 
3.11 Procurement of Medical Equipment 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.11.1 The Committee expresses its concern tha t 32 types of equipment were procured on a 
single bid basis at AIIMS Jodhpur. Such situation leads to minimizing the options for 
procurement of equipment for the Institute. In case of single bid, the cost of equipment is 
likely to be higher than multiple b ids. The Committee is of the view that multiple bids is the 
only way for procurement of economical and best equipment for the Institute. The Committee 
cautions that the Institute has not followed the General Financial rules for the procurement of 
equipment. As per General Financial Rules, procurement from a single source may be 
resorted to only when there is a single manufacturer, in case of emergency and for 
standardization of machinery or spare parts to be compatible to the existing sets of equipment. 

130 
 
In the event where more than one bid is received, it means that there is more than one supplier 
of the required equipment. The Committee, thus, recommends to the Ministry to comply with 
the GFRs issued by the Government of India and go for procurement of equi pment on 
multiple bid bases so as to achieve competitive advantage. The Committee recommends for a 
probe and analysis for all cases of single bid/ proprietary certificate procurements and a status 
report be submitted to the Committee within three months of the presentation of the Report. 
(Para 4.26 of the report) 
 
Action Taken 
 
3.11.2 The observations / recommendation of the Hon‘ble Committee has been noted for 
future guidance. It is stated by AIIMS, Jodhpur that the process of E -tendering and publishing 
all tenders on CPPP portal & website is being followed w.e.f. November, 2016. Prior to this 
also multiple tendering process has been followed for purchase of medical equipment. This 
approach has been adopted to encourage wider participation for all vendors a nd getting more 
competitive bids and to ensure public trust and transparency in procurement system. 
However, procurement on single bid basis has been made in few cases where multiple bids 
were not received in spite of tendering for multiple times. It is to  mention here that single bid 
has been resorted to only when there is a single manufacturer, in case of emergency and for 
standardization of machinery or spare parts to be compatible to the existing sets of equipment 
under Rule 166 of GFR. Moreover, techni cal committee has also certified in accordance with 
Rule GFR 173(xx) that the specifications prepared are not specific to any brand/ make and 
encourage larger/ multivendor participation in order to get competitive prices and rate 
reasonability has been asc ertain by the committee based on the copies of Purchase Order of 
other Government institution in most of the cases. 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.11.3 The Committee takes note of the steps undertaken by AIIMS Jodhpur to ensure 
transparency in procurement, inc luding the use of e -tendering and publication of 
tenders on the CPPP portal, as well as adherence to relevant provisions of the General 
Financial Rules (GFRs) in cases where single bid procurement was undertaken. 
However, the Committee is of the view that instances of procurement through single 
bids should have been avoided and the institute must adhere to mu ltiple tendering 
attempts. The C ommittee feels the need for more proactive vendor outreach and 
competitive specification design to reduce cost of procu rement of components. The 
Committee, therefore, recommends that AIIMS Jodhpur should also develop a 
structured vendor engagement strategy, including pre -bid meetings, to increase 
participation. Moreover, the Committee desires that all single bid and propri etary 
procurements should be independently reviewed on a half -yearly basis by an internal 
audit committee, and the summary of such reviews, including justification and cost 
comparisons with peer institutions, should be submitted to the Ministry. A 
comprehensive status report on the findings of the earlier probe into single bid 

131 
 
procurements, as previously recommended, should also be submitted for consideration 
of Committee. 
3.12 Status of Faculty and Non Faculty Strength 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.12.1 The Committee is constrained to observe that out of 305 sanctioned faculty posts, 160 
posts are lying vacant. Again, 2059 posts are vacant in non -faculty staff that amounts to 
approximately 55 percent of the total sanctioned non faculty posts. The Committ ee finds the 
vacancy level at Professor/Additional professor level quite alarming. The Committee is of the 
view that deficient number of teaching faculty at the Institute adds woes to basic health 
infrastructure at the Institute besides diluting the standa rd of medical education. Such a huge 
number of vacancies of the faculty/staff would definitely have an adverse impact on the 
functioning of the Institute. Vacancy of one category of health personnel is restricted not only 
to a particular Department but als o affects the overall functioning of a healthcare Institute. 
The Committee, thus, recommends to the Ministry to make all out efforts to expedite the 
process of filling up of the posts in a time bound manner in order to impart the medical 
education at the desired level and to ensure that tertiary level health care services are provided 
to the patients. The Committee also recommends to the Ministry to offer better pay structure 
and incentives to attract Specialist/Super Specialist doctors from premier Institu tes like 
AIIMS/JIPMER/PGI, etc. to join the Institute. The Committee is constrained to observe 
almost negligible presence of faculty from these similar premier Government Institutes and 
would also like to be apprised of the number of applicants who had app lied from these 
Medical Institutes but were not found suitable, along with the reasons for rejection. 
(Para 4.35 of the report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.12.2 The process of strengthening the faculty in the new AIIMS is being deliberated upon 
by the Ministry and Ins titutes on regular basis and the overall situation will improve in near 
future. Measures to improve faculty strength in the new AIIMS was also discussed in the 
meeting of the Central Institute Body (CIB) held on 13.08.2018, 10.12.2018 and 24.01.2019. 
CIB a dvised that the regular recruitment will be done at Institute level while combined 
contractual recruitment will be done by AIIMS, New Delhi through walk in -interview twice 
in a year for selecting students passing from premium medical institutes/INIs, in ad dition to 
the recruitment done by individual AIIMS. It was also decided in the CIB to allow 
temporarily, the new AIIMS to fill up the senior level posts by selection of candidates at one 
level below in case the post could not be filled up due to no -availability of suitable 
candidates. 
 
As regards vacant non -faculty posts at the institute, it is informed that Institute is making all 
its efforts to fill up the vacancy and regular recruitment are being done to fill up the vacant 
posts. As regards, number of ap plicants who had applied from premier institute like 
AIIMS/JIPMER/PGI, but were not found suitable, it is informed that institute has not 

132 
 
maintained data in such format. However, the information is being extracted and will be 
provided separately. The updated position of faculty and non-faculty posts is as below –

Further Recommendation 
 
3.12.3 The Committee acknowledges the measures taken by the Ministry and AIIMS 
Jodhpur to address faculty and non -faculty vacancies, including regular recruitment 
drives and the introduction of combined contractual recruitment through AIIMS New 
Delhi for candidates from premier medical institutions. While these initiatives are steps 
in the right direction, the Committee, however, is worried that 89 faculty posts and 807 
non-faculty posts still remain vacant, particularly at the senior levels whose leader ship 
and guidance is critical to institutional performance and academic quality. The 
Committee, therefore, reiterates that immediate, time -bound recruitment targets must 
be set with quarterly progress monitoring. Further, the policy of appointing candidate s 

133 
 
under contract & one level below the sanctioned post should be only a temporary 
stopgap measure/management, and not a substitute for attracting high -calibre talent. 
The Committee, in this regard, recommends the creation of a centralized digital 
recruitment dashboard across all AIIMS to track applications, selections, rejections 
(with reasons), and origin of candidates (including those from AIIMS 
Delhi/JIPMER/PGI). This would not only enhance transparency but also help refine 
strategies to attract top tale nt. The Committee expects the pending data on applicants 
from premier institutes viz. AIIMS/ JIPMER/PGI but were not found suitable along 
with reasons for their rejection to be submitted in Action Taken Notes of present Report 
to the Committee for consideration. 
3.13 Super Speciality Services 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.13.1 The Committee desires the Ministry to submit a status note on the cost -benefit 
analysis for establishment of Advanced Cardiac Center, Neuroscience Centre, Advanced Eye 
Centre, Cent re for Oncology, Reserved block and Organ Transplant Service Centre. The 
Committee, in this connection, recommends that Advanced Centers be established only after 
AIIMS, Jodhpur achieves the desired level of faculty and manpower needed to run these 
Super-Specialties Departments and attains the daily OPD of at least 50% of AIIMS, Delhi. 
The Committee also recommends that all the decisions regarding setting up of advance 
centers should be referred to the CIB. 
(Para 4.39 of the report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.13.2 The observation / recommendation of the committee have been noted for future 
guidelines. As also advised the matter for establishment of the various advance centres will be 
placed before the CIB for consideration / decision. As a policy, the attempt / focus w ill be on 
operationalization of remaining super -speciality Departments.  Reference in this regard has 
also been made to AIIMS Delhi vide Ministry letter dated 23.03.2019.  The Governing Body 
(GB) in its 8 th Meeting has given in principle approval regarding t he establishment of various 
centres at AIIMS Jodhpur viz. – 
 Centre for skill development of rural health  
 Allied healthcare professional training centre 
 Establishment of De-addiction centre 
 Comprehensive cancer care centre 
 Centre for rare diseases 
 OMICS centre 
 Centre for infectious disease 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.13.3 The Committee acknowledges that the Ministry and AIIMS Jodhpur have taken 
action in compliance of  Committee‟s recommendations for establishment of advanced 

134 
 
centres which was later on  referred to the Central Institute Body (CIB) for arriving at 
concrete decision . While the in -principle approval by the Governing Body for new 
centres such as the Comprehensive Cancer Care Centre, Centre for Rare Diseases, and 
others is appreciated, the Committee  re-emphasizes that such expansions should not 
dilute the core objective of achieving full operationalization of existing super -specialty 
departments and optimal faculty strength. The Committee, however, recommends that a 
detailed cost-benefit analysis and  feasibility report for each proposed centre, including 
staffing, infrastructure, and projected patient load, be submitted to the CIB prior to 
establishment of advance centre at AIIMS Jodhpur. The Committee reiterates that the  
establishment of these centre s must be contingent on AIIMS Jodhpur filling up critical 
vacancies.  
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.13.4 The Committee observes that out of 285 sanctioned posts in 37 functional Super 
Specialties Departments at the AIIMS Jodhpur, only 142 Seats have bee n filled up which 
constitutes only 50% of the sanctioned seats. The Committee is appalled to note that the 
Departments of Cardiology, Cardiothoracic Surgery, Endocrinology &Metabolism, General 
Medicine, Nephrology, Neurology, Surgical Oncology and Radio Th erapy are functioning 
with single person manning these Departments. Further, in the Neonatology Department, 
there are only two faculty and one of them is working on contractual basis. These 
Departments cater to cases of heart, kidney, and neurological dise ase. The Committee is of 
the view that Super Specialty Departments offer specialized diagnosis and treatment protocol 
dealing with a particular disease as it is not possible for a general physician to deal with 
patients suffering from all kinds of diseases. The gap in the actual position and the sanctioned 
strength of Super Specialists will adversely affect the working efficiency of the Institute as 
patients would be denied the services of the institute due to lack of Specialty/Super Specialty 
Departments. The Committee, therefore, recommends that the Institute must fill up the vacant 
faculty posts at the Professor /HOD level so as to provide best services to the patients visiting 
the Institute. 
(Para 4.41 of the report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.13.5 The observation / recommendation of the co mmittee are noted for guidance. From the 
side of the Ministry several policy interventions have been introduced to improve availability 
of candidates for various faculty positions.   In addition, the Ministry has also taken some 
policy initiatives for bringing about improved availability of trained HR at National Level at 
large. Recruitment advertisements for faculty members are being advertised on regular basis. 
Currently the rolling advertisements no. Admn/Faculty/01 -2024- AIIMS.JDH and 
Admn/Faculty/03-2024- AIIMS.JDH for 82 post of faculty members and 04 post of faculty 
members respectively in various departments of AIIMS Jodhpur are under process for which 
the interviews is likely to be conducted shortly. Faculty Position at AIIMS, Jodhpur as on 31 st 
January 2025 (Without APS)

Further Recommendation 
 
3.13.6 The Commi ttee acknowledges the efforts made by the Ministry and AIIMS 
Jodhpur to address faculty shortages through rolling advertisements and policy 
initiatives aimed at enhancing the availability of qualified candidates. However, despite 
these measures, a signific ant number of posts, i.e., nearly 30% of the total sanctioned 
faculty positions remain vacant, with several critical super -specialty departments still 
being manned by inadequate number of faculty member. The Committee understands 
that such situation severely hampers the delivery of quality healthcare and compromises 
academic and clinical training standards. The Committee, therefore, reiterates its 
recommendation that urgent and targeted steps be taken to fill up the vacant faculty 
positions, particularly at  the Professor and Head of Department levels in the super -
specialties.

Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.14.1 The Committee is surprised at the long waiting period for basic diagnostic tests and 
demands the institute to su bmit the waiting period for all the diagnostic tests in AIIMS 
Jodhpur specifically MRI. The Committee is saddened to note that despite limited functional 
OPD to AIIMS standards there is a waiting time for surgeries and even ENT procedure. If 
AIIMS, Jodhpur  has to enhance its perception, there has to be minimal waiting time for 
surgeries and other procedures. 
(Para 4.51 of the report) 
Action Taken

exponentially increasing at AIIMS Jodhpur. The waiting time for MRI is an effect of heavy 
patient load. Rega rding waiting time for surgical cases, we expect that the waiting time will 
be curtailed as soon as the 33 planned modular operation theatres are commissioned.  Details 
of waiting period for all diagnostic tests in AIIMS, Jodhpur is given below.

Further Recommendation 
 
3.14.3 The Committee appreciates the efforts made by AIIMS Jodhpur in enhancing 
diagnostic and surgical capacities, including 24x7 ope ration of the MRI unit, 
procurement of additional USG scanners, and the commissioning of 30 modular OTs. 
However, the Committee remains seriously concerned about the persistently long 
waiting period, up to six months , for MRI in OPD patients and up to thre e months for 
elective surgeries in several departments such as ENT, Paediatric Surgery, and Plastic 
Surgery. Such delays in treatment of patients compromise timely patient care. The 
Committee, therefore, strongly recommends that the Ministry take immediate  steps to 
address infrastructural and human resource bottlenecks, particularly by expediting  the 
procurement and commissioning of additional MRI machines,  USG and CT Scanner  
increasing recruitment of radiographer technicians, and ensuring optimal utilizati on of 
the new modular OTs.

3.15 BURNS AND PLASTIC SURGERY DEPARTMENT 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.15.1 The Committee is of the view that given the high incidences of burn injuries in the 
State of Rajasthan and adjoining areas, a separate Burn Unit at  the Institute is the need of the 
hour. This will ensure that timely and adequate treatment to burn patients in terms of reducing 
their mortality, complications and rehabilitation. The Committee, thus, strongly recommends 
that the Institute must start a se parate Burns Unit in IPD with sufficient manpower and 
necessary equipment to improve the care and facilities for such patients at the earliest. The 
Committee, in this connection, recommends that ambulance services equipped with advanced 
features for transportation of patient from accident site to the burns unit of the Institute should 
be made available. 
(Para 4.63 of the report) 
Action Taken 
3.15.2 The observation/ recommendation of the committee have been noted for future 
guidance. Regarding the observatio n about starting of Burn unit, AIIMS Jodhpur is in the 
process of setting up a Burn and Plastic Service in the Institute. State of the art advanced 
ambulances is available in the Institute round the clock and are being utilized for patient care. 
The institute is in the process of augmenting the services.  One floor in the under construction 
trauma building is designated for state of art burn unit.  Currently construction work is under 
full swing. 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.15.3 The Committee notes the availa bility of state -of-the-art ambulance service and 
that a floor, in the new trauma building under construction , has been designated 
towards establishing a dedicat ed Burns Unit at AIIMS Jodhpur. The response of the 
Ministry, however, lacks specific timelines for the completion and operationalization of 
the Burns Unit, including the recruitment of specialized manpower and procurement of 
all necessary equip ment. Therefore, the Committee recommends that the Ministry 
should provide a detailed project completion timeline for the "state of the art burn unit" 
within the trauma building, clearly outlining key milestones such as construction 
completion, equipment procurement, and the specific plan for recruiting and training 
sufficient specialized manpower (plastic surg eons, burn nurses, physiotherapists, etc.) 
dedicated to the unit.  
3.16 AYUSH FACILITIES 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.16.1 The Committee observes that only Ayurveda and Homoeopathic OPD services are 
functional at the AIIMS, Jodhpur. The Committee consi ders that there is a need to integrate 
the Ayurveda, Yoga, Naturopathy, Unani, Siddha, and Homeopathy (AYUSH) system of 
indigenous medicine to meet the shortage of health care professionals and strengthen the 

145 
 
delivery system of the health care service through an alternate system of Indian medicine. The 
Committee, therefore, recommends to the Ministry to start other AYUSH services at the 
Institute. The Committee also recommends to the Ministry to ensure the accessibility and 
availability and choice of AYUSH services to all patients depending in these specific needs. 
The Committee recommends to the institute to chalk out strategy for strengthening AYUSH 
services and to support this system with adequate infrastructure and manpower in a time 
bound manner. 
(Para 4.66 of the report) 
 
3.16.2 The Committee is constrained to observe that out of seven sanctioned posts for 
AYUSH faculty, only two faculties are presently working at AIIMS, Jodhpur and that too on 
contract basis. The Committee observes that posts of Senior  Medical Officer AYUSH and 
Yoga instructor are lying vacant. The Committee, thus, recommends the Ministry to fill up the 
posts for AYUSH doctors on regular basis at the earliest. 
(Para 4.67 of the report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.16.3 The observations / recommendat ion of the Hon‘ble Committee has been noted for 
future guidance. The Ministry has decided to integrate the AYUSH in the tertiary care system 
in new AIIMS by augmenting the strength of AYUSH professionals and by also creating 
scientific posts in order to un dertake academic Ph.D. programme and finding solutions to 
India‘s public health challenge. M/o AYUSH had advised that each of the new and upcoming 
AIIMS should have augmented faculty / doctor strength and higher focus in one particular 
traditional system of medicine which may be having popularity in the particular region. Each 
such augmented medicine system will have additional 16 posts, consisting of posts of 
Professor, Additional Professor, Associate Professor, Assistant Professor, SR/JR posts. This 
will be over and above the already sanctioned five Medical Officer posts, one each in 
Ayurveda, Yoga and Naturopathy, Unani, Siddha and Homeo pathy. The above structure of 
Ayush Department has been included in the Standard Staffing Pattern approved by CIB. The 
proposal for creation of posts is being taken up with Department of Expenditure accordingly.  
The Institute has taken action to fill up these posts. 
 
3.16.4 AYUSH OPD services have been started in the institute and a separate AYUSH Block 
is functional. Curre ntly out of seven sanctioned post, six are working at AIIMS Jodhpur on 
regular basis. 1 post of Ayurveda Medical Officer has been advertised.  Recruitment Process 
for One Senior Medical Officer AYUSH and Five Posts of Medical Officer AYUSH 
(Ayurveda, Yoga, Unani, Siddha  & Homeopathy) has been completed. Joined in August 
2022. 1 Medical officer (Ayurveda) resigned on 30.11.2023, same post has been 
advertisement on 19.10.2024 and requirement is under processing. 1 Yoga  Instructor has also 
joined on regular basis w.e.f. 17.08.2021. 
 
 
 
 

146 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.16.5 The Committee acknowledges the positive steps taken by AIIMS Jodhpur and 
the Ministry towards strengthening AYUSH services, including the establishment of a 
dedicated AYUSH block, initiation of OPD s ervices, recruitment of AYUSH medical 
officers, and alignment with the Standard Staffing Pattern ap proved by the CIB. T he 
Committee, nevertheless, emphasizes  that the resignation of a key medical officer and 
the continued gaps in faculty recruitment unders core the need for robust retention and 
timely hiring mechanisms. The Committee, therefore, recommends that the Ministry 
expedite the creation and operationalization of the additional 16 AYUSH posts as 
proposed and  ensure timely recruitment against all sanc tioned vacancies, and allocate 
adequate budgetary support for infrastructure, training, and research under AYUSH 
disciplines.  
 
D. AIIMS PATNA 
3.17 PROCUREMENT OF MEDICAL EQUIPMENT 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.17.1 The Committee is dismayed to note that 16 items of equipment (8.33%) costing Rs. 56 
crore remain undelivered while 34 items of equipment (19.54 %) costing Rs. 34 crore have 
been delivered but not installed. Consignee Receipt Certificate (CRC) has not been issued for 
20 items of equipment (11.49%) costing Rs. 3 crore. The Committee is shocked over the state 
of affairs in this institute of National importance where such grave underutilization of 
resources has become a common phenomenon. The Committee cannot fathom that how can 
the Institute turn a blind eye to the number of medical equipment that are lying uninstalled in 
the hospitals for such long periods. 
(Para 5.27 of the report) 
 
3.17.2 The Committee is not at all convinced by the reasons given by AIIMS Patna for the 
delayed supplies and non -installation of the medical equipment. Delays due to the non -
availability of space or complete power etc. could have been easily addressed by the Institute. 
The Committee believes that the institute had plenty of time at its disposal to obtain site 
approvals and the site should have been prepared well in advance. This undue delay in 
installation also highlights the failure of the Procurement Support Agency in ensuring timely 
supply and installation of the medical equipment in the Institute. The Committee is al so 
highly concerned regarding the role of the Procurement Support Agency that should have 
analysed the area and power requirement for the medical equipment and communicated the 
same to the Institute. The Committee holds the Institute and the Procurement Su pport Agency 
responsible for the delay in operationalization of these equipment and also warns that this lax 
attitude of the institute has proved to be the biggest roadblock in its path to establish itself as a 
centre of excellence in medical education. 
(Para 5.28 of the report) 

147 
 
Action Taken 
 
3.17.3 The observation/recommendation of the Hon'ble Committee has been noted for future 
guidance. In pursuance of the observations of the Committee, the delivery status of the 16 
Items of equipment has been checked up and the position is as under: 
 
(i) Out of 16 items, only four items were not delivered due to some issues between HITES 
(PSA) and the Vendor. PSA is being directed to sort out the issues and get the equipment 
delivered and installed. 
 
(ii) All the deliver ed equipment was installed except two equipment s delivered but not 
installed by the vendor l.e. High Speed Steam Glass Sterilizer & Lyophilizer. 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.17.4 The Committee takes note of the updated status indicating progress in the 
delivery and installation of most of the previously undelivered and uninstalled medical 
equipment at AIIMS Patna. The Committee, however, expresses serious concerns over 
the fact that some critical equipment remains undelivered or uninstalled due to 
unresolved issues between the Procurement Support Agency (HITES) and vendors 
reflects persistent systemic inefficiencies. The Committee, therefore, recommends that 
the Ministry should monitor and co -ordinate in a time -bound manner for resolution of 
issues pertaining to procurement and installation delays, indicating defined 
accountability for both the Institute and the PSA. A periodic review system should be 
institutionalized, involving all stakeholders, to ensure proactive site readiness, 
infrastructure support, and timely vendor coordination. The Ministry should consider 
developing a centralized dashboard for real -time tracking of equipment procurement 
and installation across all AIIMS. 
 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.17.5 The Committee, therefore strongly recommen ds that the institute should be proactive 
in taking suomoto cognizance of the delay in delivery of the equipment and ensure that 
medical equipment are delivered on time. The Institute should also make sure that 
unnecessary delay in installation of equipmen t does not take place due to shortage of skilled 
manpower or lack in infrastructure. The Committee also recommends that all the medical 
equipment should be accepted only after a technical inspection by a technical Inspector and 
should receive routine maint enance and calibration. The Institute should also have a 
contingency plan in case of breakdown of equipment so that the diagnostic services are not 
adversely effected. 
(Para 5.29 of the report) 
 
 

148 
 
Action Taken 
 
3.17.6 The observation/recommendation of the H on'ble Committee has been noted for future 
guidance. Efforts are made by the Institute and the Ministry to impress upon the Procurement 
Support Agency i.e. HITES for early delivery and installation of equipment. However, 
whenever there is delay in supply o f equipment, penalty is imposed as per tender clause by 
the PSA. Moreover, skilled manpower is provided for installation of equipment. Also for 
equipment purchased and installed, there is a warranty clause for 5 years warranty and in 
addition all equipment  are covered with Comprehensive Maintenance Agreement for further 
period of five years. 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.17.7 The Committee note s the efforts being made to impress upon HITES for early 
delivery and installation, along with the imp osition of penal ties for delays at AIIMS 
Patna. Furthermore, the provision of skilled manpower for installation and the coverage 
of equipment under 5 -year warranty and subsequent 5 -year Comprehensive 
Maintenance Agreements are positive steps towards ensuring operation fun ctionality 
and proficiency. However, the reply does not explicitly address the Committee‟s 
recommendation for a technical inspection by a technical inspector before acceptance of 
equipment, nor does it details a comprehensive contingency plan in case of eq uipment 
breakdown to prevent adverse effects on diagnostic services. Therefore, the Committee 
reiterates that the Ministry, in addition to existing measures, establish a mandatory 
protocol for independent technical inspection and certification of all medic al equipment 
prior to acceptance to ensure quality and functionality at the time of delivery. The 
Committee reiterates that a detailed contingency plan for critical equipment 
breakdowns should be developed at AIIMS Patna outlining alternative arrangements to 
minimize disruption to patient diagnostic and treatment services. 
 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.17.8 AIIMS Patna also submitted that US FDA and CE certification was put in the 
specification due to unavailability of a similar Indian Model. The Commit tee is shocked by 
the false claims of AIIMS Patna that Indian model for basic instruments such as hand drill, 
mouth gags, rib, hooks, chisels etc. are not available in the market. The Committee is of the 
opinion that items such as Navigation system, Surgic al Helmet and Accessories, Surgical 
consumable items and other instruments are easily available with the Indian manufacturers 
but AIIMS Patna, by insisting on US FDA and CE certification even for such basic 
instruments has clearly shown contempt for the Indian manufacturing sector. 
(Para 5.35 of the report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.17.9 With reference to the Committee's observation, it is mentioned here that the Ministry 
has issued guidelines for adopting the BIS standards also as an option to bidders, wherever 

149 
 
available, vide Ministry OM dated 20.02.2018 and since then procurement is being made 
accordingly. All the AIIMS have been advised to comply with the instructions. AIIMS, Patna 
is also following "Make in India" policy and insists on Bureau of Indian Standard ( BIS) 
certification. 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.17.10 In the light of the Ministry‟s response and the adoption of BIS standards 
in procurement practices following the OM dated 20.02.2018, the Committee 
acknowledges the corrective steps taken to promote indi genous manufacturing. The 
Committee, nevertheless,  recommends that the Ministry ensure strict compliance with 
the BIS-inclusive procurement policy across all AIIM S and mandate that procurement 
committees give due preference to quality -certified Indian manufacturers, especially for 
basic instruments. A formal mechanism should be instituted for periodic audit of tender 
specifications and vendor selection processes to prevent unwarranted bias toward 
foreign certifications.  
 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.17.11 On one side where the Government is taking measures to reduce the country‘s 
dependency on imported medical devices, it is unfortunate to find an institute of national 
importance openly following a mandatory US FDA certification policy for purchasing 
medical equipment. The Committee is distressed to note that medical devices like 
defibrillator, ultrasound machine, neonatal ventilator, surgical instruments etc  are being 
purchased with US FDA certification even though there are many other manufacturers wit h 
CE certification. The Committee strongly recommends to AIIMS Patna to follow a buy 
"Make in India" policy and insist on Bureau of Indian Standard (BIS) certification. 
(Para 5.36 of the report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.17.12 With reference to the observation/recom mendation of the Hon'ble Committee, 
it is submitted that the Ministry has already initiated action in this regard. The Secretary, 
Ministry of Health and Family Welfare vide letter dated 7 th May, 2018 to Director General, 
Bureau of Indian Standards requesti ng for formulation of specifications for medical 
equipment being procured to promote 'Make in India' policy of the Government of India.  
Bureau of Indian Standards has already started creation of BIS standards for Medical 
Equipment and Devices. As per infor mation available with the Ministry, a standardization list 
for around 1000 medical devices has almost been completed. 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.17.13 In view of the action taken by the Ministry and the ongoing efforts by the 
Bureau of Indian Standards (BI S) to formulate specifications for approximately 1000 
medical devices, the Committee appreciates the alignment with the "Make in India" 
initiative. However, to ensure that these efforts translate into real -time procurement 

150 
 
reform, the Committee recommends that AIIMS Patna and all other similar institutions 
strictly discontinue the practice of mandating US FDA certification where BIS or CE 
standards are available. Additionally, the Committee recommends monitoring of 
procurement patterns across AIIMS institutions to ensure preference for indigenous and 
BIS-certified products. 
3.18 STATUS OF FACULTY/NON-FACULTY STRENGTH 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.18.1 The Committee notes that 122 posts have been filled up against the sanctioned 
strength of 305 for Profess ors/Additional Professor/Associate Professor/ Assistant Professor 
whereas 183 Posts are lying vacant in different Departments of AIIMS Patna. None of the 
posts have been filled up in Trauma and Emergency, Nuclear Medicine, Nephrology, Hospital 
Administration and Endocrinology and Metabolism Departments. The Committee observes 
with concern that important Departments such as Cardiology, Medical Oncology and 
Haematology, Neonatology, Neurology, Radiotherapy, Surgical Oncology, Urology are 
functioning with only  one doctor in AIIMS Patna. The Committee also notes with serious 
concern that AIIMS Patna has the lowest faculty strength among all the new AIIMS 
established under Phase 1 of PMSSY. This casts aspersion on the functioning of AIIMS, 
Patna with regard to Human Resource Management as well. 
(Para 5.39 of the report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.18.2 Matter of Record. At present, 224 seats of various faculty posts against total 305 seats 
are filled up. Only one department, Endocrinology & Metabolism is not functional at AII MS 
Patna due to non-availability of suitable candidates for the post. 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.18.3 The Committee acknowledges the progress made in improving faculty 
recruitment at AIIMS Patna with 224 out of 305 faculty posts now filled up. However, 
the Committee expresses deep concern that critical departments such as Endocrinology 
& Metabolism continue to remain non -functional due to the lack of suitable candidates, 
and many other departments don‟t have adequate number of faculty. The Committee, 
therefore, recommends that the Ministry take urgent and targeted measures to address 
faculty shortages through proactive recruitment drives, relaxation of procedural delays, 
and provision of additional incentives, especially for super -specialty and 
underrepresented departments. 
 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.18.4 The Committee is disturbed by the acute shortage of Professors/Additional 
Professor/Associate Professor/ Assistant Professor in AIIMS Patna and other AIIMS like 
Institutes. The Committee during its study visit to AIIMS Patna also discussed the shortage of 

151 
 
facility with the Director and representatives of the Ministry. The Committee recommends 
that AIIMS Patna should make immediate efforts to implement all the initiatives for 
recruitment of faculty. The  Committee notes that provision has been made to engage faculty 
from other institutes and Government medical colleges on deputation but the Committee 
would also like to emphasize that engaging faculty from the GMCs should not result in 
vacancies in the nearby GMCs. 
(Para 5.41 of the report) 
 
Action Taken 
 
3.18.5 The process of strengthening the faculty in the new AIIMS is being deliberated upon 
by the Institutes & the Ministry on regular basis and the overall situation will improve in near 
future. In additi on, the Ministry has also taken some policy Initiatives for bringing about 
improved availability of trained HR at National Level at large. Measures to improve faculty 
strength in the new AllMS  were also discussed in the meeting of the Central Institute Bod y 
(CIB) held on 13.08.2018 and 10.12.2018 and 24.01.2019. CIB advised that the regular 
recruitment will be done at Institute level while combined contractual recruitment will be 
done by AIIMS, New Delhi through walk in -interview twice in a year for selecti ng students 
passing from premium medical institutes/INIs, in addition to the recruitment done by 
Individual AIIMS. It was also decided in the CIB to allow temporarily, the new AIIMS to fill 
up the senior level posts by selection of candidates at one level below in case the post could 
not be filled up due to non-availability of suitable candidates. 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.18.6 The Committee notes the efforts to streamline and decentralize faculty 
recruitment processes and the policy decision to allow comb ined contractual 
recruitment through AIIMS, New Delhi. However, given the continuing faculty 
shortage, especially in critical specialties, the Committee recommends that the Ministry 
and AIIMS Patna urgently operationalize bi -annual walk-in interviews as pl anned and 
closely monitor the success rate of such recruitments. The Committee understands that 
while temporary appointments at one level below may provide only interim relief, 
therefore, the Committee emphasizes the need to maintain academic and clinical 
standards on permanent basis.  
 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.18.7 The Committee understands that special packages/incentives or higher salary may not 
always be a driving factor to serve in this part of the country, therefore, the Institute has to 
explore alternative means such as paid research collaboration, high end technology, better 
working hours, security, enough leisure time etc. to attract qualified doctors to the Institute. 
 (Para 5.42 of the Report) 
 
 

152 
 
Action Taken 
 
3.18.8 The observations/recommendation of the Hon'ble Committee has been noted for future 
guidance. 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.18.9 Given the persistent challenges in attracting and retaining qualified doctors in 
these regions, the Committee urges the Ministry to prepare a structured fr amework of 
non-monetary incentives also such as opportunities for research collaboration, provision 
of advanced medical technology, assured work -life balance, and safety measures. The 
Committee further recommends that pilot initiatives in this regard be un dertaken at 
select Institutes with regular monitoring and feedback, so that effective models can be 
scaled up across all Institutes to ensure sustainable retention of skilled medical 
professionals. 
 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.18.10 The Committee unde rstands that the shortage of doctors and specialist is a 
macro level problem considering the poor doctor: patient ratio in the country but the 
Committee fails to understand the reasons for huge number of vacancy in the non -faculty 
positions at AIIMS Patna.  The Committee strongly recommends that the Institute should 
expedite the recruitment process for the non-faculty posts. 
(Para 5.44 of the report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.18.11 On the basis of the Parliamentary Standing Committee observations, the 
Central Institut e Body (CIB) in its 3 rd meeting held on 24.01.2019 advised all AIIMS to 
conduct recruitment for Non -faculty posts on centralized basis by applying following 
measures: 
 The recruitment of Nursing Officers in all AIIMS is being conducted by AIIMS New 
Delhi th rough NORCET (Nursing Officer Recruitment Common Eligibility Test) 
twice in a year as of now and allocating the provisionally selected candidates to the 
concerned participating institute. 
 For the rest of Technical and Non -Technical posts of Non -faculty Gro up- B & C, 
recruitment is being by AIIMS New Delhi through Common Recruitment 
Examination (CRE) in an year an allocating the provisionally selected candidates to 
concerned participating institutes. 
3.18.12 The Non-Faculty position as on 31.01.2025 is as under: 
Sanctioned Filled Up Vacant 
3884 2539 1345 
 
 246 vacancies of Nursing Officers has been advertised by AIIMS New Delhi through 
NORCET- 07 and 222 provisionally selected candidates were allocated to AIIMS 
Patna, which is under process. 

153 
 
 
3.18.13 307 vac ancies of various Non -Faculty posts of Group - B & C has been 
advertised by AIIMS New Delhi through Common Recruitment Examination (CRE)  - 2024 
on behalf of AIIMS Patna and the process is ongoing. 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.18.14 The Committee acknowledges a administrative shift towards centralized 
recruitment for non -faculty posts at all AIIMS, including AIIMS Patna, through 
NORCET for Nursing Officers and the Common Recruitment Examination (CRE) for 
other Group B & C posts. Such streamlined approach aims t o expedite the recruitment 
process, as evidenced by the recent advertising of 246 Nursing Officer vacancies and 307 
other non -faculty posts for AIIMS Patna. However, despite these efforts, a significant 
number of vacancies of non-faculty posts (1345 out of  3884 sanctioned posts as of 
31.01.2025) still persist. Therefore, the Committee further recommends the Ministry to 
take necessary steps for expediting recruitment process by the centralized recruitment 
process, particularly focusing on the conversion rate  of advertised vacancies to fill up 
positions and shortening the average time taken for a complete recruitment cycle. The 
Ministry should identify bottlenecks or systemic challenges within the centralized 
process that might still contribute to the high vac ancy rates and develop targeted 
interventions, such as increasing the frequency of examinations for posts with persistent 
shortages or implementing fast -track recruitment for critical positions to ensure high -
quality patient care and education. 
 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.18.15 The Committee also feels that AIIMS Patna should not ignore the importance 
of nurses in the health care system and recommends expediting the recruitment of 500 staff 
nurses for which AIIMS Patna has already issued advertisement . The institute should also 
speed up its efforts to start different medical and para -medical courses to fill the existing gaps 
in their medical services. 
(Para 5.45 of the report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.18.16 The matter was discussed in the 3 rd Meeting of CIB hel d on 24.01.2019 with 
the purpose to streamline the recruitment process of non-faculty (Technical) by new AIIMS to 
attain speedier and better outcome.  It was inter-alia decided that, AIIMS New Delhi shall 
conduct recruitment for Nursing Officers for all AII MS through NORCET and CRE and 
vacant positions have advertised and recruitment is under process.  Present Strength of 
Nursing Officers is 786 against Sanctioned strength of 1326 seats.  Present Strength of Senior 
Nursing Officer is 413 against Sanctioned str ength of 510 seats.  Presently 246 seats of 
Nursing Officer advertised vide NORCET - 07 by AIIMS New Delhi.  Presently 39 seats of 
Senior Nursing Officer advertised vide CRE- 2024 by AIIMS New Delhi. 
 
 

154 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.18.17 The Committee observes th e steps taken towards addressing the issue of 
nursing staff shortage at AIIMS Patna by centralizing recruitment through NORCET 
and CRE via AIIMS New Delhi. The current strengths of 786 Nursing Officers against 
1326 sanctioned and 413 Senior Nursing Officer s against 510 sanctioned indicate 
ongoing efforts, further supported by the recent advertisements for 246 Nursing Officer 
and 39 Senior Nursing Officer positions. However, a substantial vacancy of 540 Nursing 
Officers and 97 Senior Nursing Officers still e xists. The Committee, therefore, 
recommends that the Ministry, in collaboration with AIIMS New Delhi, should explore 
the feasibility of increasing the frequency of NORCET and CRE examinations, or 
streamlining the selection and allocation process, to more r apidly fill up the persistent 
vacancies in nursing cadres. The Committee also reiterates that the Ministry should 
chalk out a streamlined strategy and timeline for the initiation of "different medical and 
para-medical courses " to address underlying gaps in  medical services and ensure a 
sustainable supply of skilled healthcare professionals. 
 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.18.18 The Committee feels that this huge shortage against faculty and non -faculty 
posts in AIIMS Patna has an adverse effect on the fun ctioning of many important 
Departments in the Institute impacting the overall quality of medical service provided to the 
patients. Therefore, the Committee recommends a common centralised recruitment policy for 
filling up the vacancies in all the AIIMS. Th is will not only lower the cost of recruitment 
process but also offer uniform and fair selection criteria for all the AIIMS. 
 (Para 5.46 of the Report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.18.19 The observations/recommendation of the Hon'ble Committee has been noted  
for future guidance. 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.18.20 The Committee expresses deep concern on the response of the Ministry in 
regard to the Committee‟s recommendation on huge shortage against faculty and non -
faculty posts in AIIMS Patna. The Committee, therefore, rec ommends AIIMS Patna 
that concrete roadmap is imperative at place to address the acute shortage of faculty 
and non -faculty in AIIMS Patna and other new AIIMS. The Committee further 
recommends the Ministry to expedite formulation and implementation of a cent ralized 
recruitment policy for all AIIMS, ensuring uniformity, transparency, and cost -
effectiveness in the selection process. A clear timeline be laid down for operationalizing 
this policy.

3.19 SPECIALITY AND SUPER-SPECIALITY DEPARTMENTS 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.19.1 The Committee believes that Speciality and Super Speciality Departments are 
fundamental for an Institute like AIIMS Patna that is envisaged as a flag bearer in providing 
tertiary health care and medical education. The Committee howeve r is anguished to note that 
important Departments like Endocrinology, Nephrology, Urology, and Nuclear Medicine are 
not functional till date in the Institute. Considering the fact that around 2.2 Lakh new patients 
suffering from End Stage Renal Disease (ES RD) are reported every year which results in an 
additional demand of 3.4 crore dialysis every year  the status of crucial Department like 
Nephrology being non-functional in AIIMS Patna is a matter of grave concern. 
(Para 5.50 of the report) 
3.19.2 The Insti tute needs to be considerate towards the expenses borne by poor patients 
when they are referred to other hospitals because of non -availability of the medical facility in 
AIIMS Patna. The Committee, thus, recommends to the Institute to start the Specialty a nd 
Super Speciality Departments as early as possible in the best interest of the people of the 
State. 
(Para 5.51 of the report)

the Department of Endoc rinology and Metabolism. Vide Rolling Advertisement 2023/1

filling the vacant seats of the department of Endocrinology & Metabolism. However, it is 
pertinent to mention that, only 2 applications for the said department have been received so 
far. Some posts of Additional Professor and Associate Professor have been downgraded as per 
recommendation of the CIB & GB. Rolling Advertisements for the vacant posts is regularly 
advertised and efforts are being made to shortlist suitable candidates. Present Faculty Strength

infrastructure and faculty are available. However, the Committee is concerned over the

specialist output. The Committee, therefore, recommends that the Ministry, in 
coordination with AIIMS Delhi, expl ore options for decentralised or supplementary 
counselling rounds specifically for AIIMS Patna to ensure optimal seat utilisation. The 
Committee further recommends that AIIMS Patna should submit a roadmap for 
gradually introducing PG courses in other departments that are currently not teaching

development of high standards of medical ed ucation in AIIMS Patna. The Committee, 
therefore, strongly recommends creation of a Human Resource Development Cell in AIIMS 
Patna that would conduct regular training for the doctors, nurses and the staff. The Institute 
must also conduct special training for ensuring palliative care to the patients. 
(Para 5.56 of the report)

research papers and attend different conferences. Department of Medical Education have been 
formed and is regularly undertaking induction & Training programmes. Further, foundation 
courses are being conducted for UG & PG students. 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.20.6 While the Committee notes the positive steps taken by AIIMS Patna in 
organizing CMEs, workshops, and training programs through the Department of 
Medical Education, however, a dedicated and structured Human Resource Development 
(HRD) Cell is yet to be formally established , as recommended by the Committee . The 
Committee, therefore,  reiterates its earlier recommendation and urges the Institute to 
institutionalize a full -fledged HRD Cell with defined roles, budgetary provisions, and 
training schedules to ensure systematic capacity building for all categories of staff 
including doctors, nurses, and paramedics.  
 
 
 
 

160 
 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.20.7 The Committee is sceptical about the standard of clinical training provided as the 
Institute has shortage of faculty in almost every Department. The Committee is anguished to 
find that there are insufficient number of Operation Theatres and laboratories as the 
construction work is yet to be completed. The Committee strongly emphasizes that the 
assessment of the budding doctors should be based on real clinical cases rather th an on rote 
learning. Ideally, each hospital should have senior consultants to advise the resident doctors 
on treatment or supervise the key procedure and surgeries but the shortage of trained 
specialists in AIIMS Patna may produce graduates and post gradua tes with no practical 
medical skills. Therefore, the Committee lays more impetus on imparting practical, technical 
and skill based knowledge to the doctors. The Committee is of the view that AIIMS Patna 
should, therefore, focus on producing better trained doctors who later become competent 
enough to work in a clinical setting and possess sharp clinical and medical acumen. 
(Para 5.58 of the report) 
 
Action Taken 
 
3.20.8 At Present there are 28 OTs, functional at AIIMS Patna and 17 Minor OTs. All three 
labs Microbiology, Pathology & Biochemistry are functional and all tests are done in -house. 
A new MBBS Course has been implemented and also approved by SAC which is competency 
based Medical Education & training Programme with a foundation course, early Clinical 
exposure, elective posting for diverse learning experience with research & community project. 
This will enhance practical, technical & Skill of the students. 
 
3.20.9 The observation/ recommendation of the Hon‘ble Committee has been noted for future 
guidance. The faculty strength of the Institute has been increased in the last few years. 
Further, all departments at AIIMS Patna are functional except the Department of 
Endocrinology & Metabolism. The vacant seats are regularly being advertised through 
Rolling Advertisement. 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.20.10 The Committee acknowledges the progress made by AIIMS Patna in 
terms of increasing the number of functional Operation Theatres, laboratories, and the 
implementation of the competency-based MBBS curriculum designed to enhance clinical 
and technical skills. However, the Committee remains to be apprised of the actual 
faculty strength against the sanct ioned strength, department -wise.  T he Committee , 
therefore, underscores the critical importance of adequate faculty  in-position to 
effectively deliver hands-on training and mentorship, particularly in specialized clinical 
procedures. Furthermore, t he Committee would like to know the reason for not yet 
operationalization of the Department of Endocrinology and Metabolism  and desire that 
all requisite initiative must be taken to commence that Department without further 
delay. The Committee further recommends that AIIMS Patna should strengthen clinical 
mentorship by ensuring the presence of senior consultants in all departm ents and 

161 
 
integrate simulation-based learning alongside real patient exposure. The Institute must 
also prioritize the completion of other requisite infrastructure to facilitate uninterrupted 
clinical training, thereby , ensuring that graduates are not only a cademically proficient 
but also clinically adept and job-ready. 
3.21 TRAUMA AND EMERGENCY SERVICES 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.21.1 The Committee takes it to exception that all the faculty positions in the Trauma and 
Emergency Care Centre are vacant. Considering this fact, AIIMS Patna's claim that it has a 
functional trauma/emergency centre is absolutely farce. The Committee reprimands the 
Institute for making such false claims and misleading the Committee. It is unfortunate that 
despite the required infrastructure for Trauma and Emergency centre, it is not fully functional 
due to the shortage of skilled manpower. The Committee strongly recommends that 
recruitment to various posts is carried out and trauma and Emergency Centre is made 
functional in the real sense of the term with adequate manpower so that critical patients are 
given urgent and prompt treatment and care. The Committee takes serious note of the manner 
in which AIIMS Patna has tried to evade Parliamentary scrutiny. 
(Para 5.63 of the report) 
 
Action Taken 
 
3.21.2 Trauma department is fully functional in AIIMS Patna.  Currently 6 Faculty (4 
Surgeons and 2 Anaesthetists) are working for the Department of Trauma Surgery & Critical 
Care. The remaining seats of Trauma & Emergency department shifte d to newly created 
Department the Emergency Medicine.  Currently 5 Faculty are working in Emergency 
Medicine. 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.21.3 The Committee notes the recent efforts made by AIIMS Patna to operationalize 
the Trauma and Emergency Care Centre b y deploying faculty in both the Trauma 
Surgery & Critical Care and the newly created Emergency Medicine departments. 
While this marks a positive shift from the earlier situation, the Committee desires that 
faculty must be  supported by  dedicated emergency p rotocols and round -the-clock 
availability of skilled personnel and adequate support staff. Therefore, the Committee 
further recommends that AIIMS Patna must ensure seamless coordination between the 
Trauma and Emergency Medicine department s for  strengthening emergency response 
systems. 
 
 
 
 
 
 

162 
 
3.22 AYUSH 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.22.1 The Committee believes that in a country like India with acute shortage of medical 
professionals, AYUSH through its traditional methods of treatment can go a long way in 
providing basic health care facilities to the citizens. National AYUSH Mission was launched 
in 2014 with the same vision, to provide cost effective and equitable AYUSH health care 
throughout the country by improving access to AYUSH services. AIIMS Patna bei ng the 
foremost institute of medical care should understand the importance of medical pluralism and 
make every effort to strengthen the AYUSH system. The Committee, therefore, strongly 
recommends that other branches of AYUSH should be operationalized at AI IMS Patna 
without delay and recruitment to the sanctioned posts must be carried out immediately. 
(Para 5.72 of the report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.22.2 The observation / recommendation of the Hon‘ble Committee has been noted for 
future guidance. Advertisement for the vacant posts was issued and the Vacant positions have 
been filled up in the year of 2020 and 2021. 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.22.3 The Committee notes  that vacant positions we re filled in 2020 and 2021. The  
action so taken addresses the immediate conce rn of Committee about staffing, h owever, 
the response does not provide specific details on current functional status or patient 
uptake in these departments. To fully realize the potential of medical pluralism and 
strengthen AYUSH as envisioned by the Commi ttee, a mere statement of recruitment is 
insufficient. Therefore, the Committee further recommends that the Ministry provide a 
detailed update on the current operational status of all five major AYUSH branches 
(Ayurveda, Yoga & Naturopathy, Unani, Siddha, and Homoeopathy) at AIIMS Patna, 
including the daily patient footfall and the range of services offered in each  department. 
Furthermore, the Ministry should outline a strategic plan for promoting and integrating 
these AYUSH services with conventional medic ine at AIIMS Patna, aiming to establish 
it as a model institution for medical pluralism and comprehensive healthcare delivery. 
 
E. AIIMS RAIPUR 
3.23 STATUS OF CONSTRUCTION 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.23.1 The Committee is anguished at the slow pace of th e construction activities at the 
AIIMS, Raipur as none of the four Packages have been completed even after delays ranging 
from 70 to 90 months. Surprisingly, the main reasons for delay under all the Packages were 
abandonment of work by the construction age ncies and lack of co -ordination among various 

163 
 
agencies. The delay in the completion of the construction has resulted in cost escalation of Rs. 
58.18 crore which is expected to increase further if the remaining work is not completed 
within the revised date of completion. The Committee is of the view that though various 
reasons have been identified by the Institute, corrective measures should have been taken 
much earlier in order to rectify the mistakes to avoid time and cost overruns. Such inordinate 
delays have resulted in indefinite postponement of delivery of health care services to the 
people. The Committee, therefore, advocates strict monitoring by the Ministry so that the 
project work is completed within the revised deadlines without any further delay. The 
Committee recommends for submission of a detailed enquiry report  along with action 
taken against the errant project consultants and contractors. 
 
 (Para 6.12 of the Report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.23.2 Except for Package I the Final certificate for all the balance packages has been issued. 
1. Package II  :    06-01-2023. 
2. Package III :    07-05-2021. 
         3.   Package IV :     20-02-2023. 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.23.3 The Committee notes the Ministry‟s submission that certificates have now been 
issued for completion of construction activities at AIIMS Raipur under Packages II, III, 
and IV, while construction activities under Package I remains pending. The Committee, 
however, emphasizes that accountability for delays spanning more than 70 mo nths in 
some cases and the resultant cost escalation must not be overlooked. The Committee, 
therefore, reiterates its recommendation for a detailed enquiry report identifying lapses 
on the part of project consultants, contractors, and monitoring agencies, along with 
specific action taken against those found responsible. Further, the Committee 
recommends that lessons from these delays be institutionalized in the Ministry‟s 
monitoring framework to ensure that future projects under PMSSY or otherwise are 
completed strictly within timelines and without avoidable cost overruns. 
3.24 STATUS OF FACULTY AND NON FACULTY STRENGTH 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.24.1 The Committee has been reiterating that shortage of qualified medical professional is 
one of the key challenges that new A IIMS are presently facing. The Committee notes that 
huge number of posts in both faculty and non-faculty posts are lying vacant at AIIMS, Raipur. 
Out of total sanctioned posts of 4089, 2636 posts are lying vacant that constitutes more than 
64 percent of the total sanctioned posts. The Committee, further, observes that majority of the 
vacancies are at the Professor/Additional Professor level which indicates that the Institute is 
suffering from dearth of experienced personnel. The Committee believes that heal th care is 

164 
 
one of the most labour -intensive sectors where human resources are crucial. Merely 
developing infrastructure and increasing capital expenditure would not help, instead it is 
adequate manpower at the health care Institutes that ensures efficient and prompt delivery of 
health care services to the patients. The Committee, therefore, would like the Institute to make 
concerted efforts and accord equal priority to fill up the vacant posts. 
(Para 6.19 of the report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.24.2 AIIMS Raipur has been continuously pursuing to fill up the vacant posts of Professor, 
Additional Professor, Associate Professor and Assistant Professor on permanent basis, 
deputation basis as well as on contractual basis at regular intervals. Despite many attempts, 
some p osts are still remaining vacant due to no application/less application/no suitable 
candidate found/non -joined after selection to the posts. There is no such problem in Pre -
clinical and Para-clinical departments. Many non-faculty posts have been filled on p ermanent 
basis. 
 
1. Continuous sincere efforts are being made to fill up vacant faculty posts in speciality  and 
super-speciality departments. Process for filling up 169 faculty posts was  completed in 2022. 
Joining of the selected candidates was completed in April 2023. 
2. Advertisement for recruitment to 116 post was published on 12.05.2023. The  same was 
cancelled due to some administrative reasons. 
3. Recently, AIIMS Raipur has successfully completed the rolling advertisement for 
recruitment to fill  up 129  vacant faculty posts with selection of 34 faculties for various 
departments.  
4. Second phase of existing rolling advertisement is ready for receiving online application for 
the remaining 91 vacant posts of the advertisement. 
5. Rolling advertisement give s eligible candidates opportunity to apply for the post  
throughout the year subject to availability of vacancies. 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.24.3 The Committee acknowledges the  efforts made by AIIMS Raipur to fill up the 
vacant faculty and non -faculty posi tions through various recruitment methods, 
including permanent, deputation, and contractual basis, as well as the implementation of 
a rolling advertisement system. However, the submission of ministry  for remaining 
vacancies at various levels due to "no app lication/less application/no suitable candidate 
found/non-joined after selection" points out deeper systemic issues beyond mere 
recruitment efforts. The Committee , therefore, recommends that the Ministry, while 
continuing with the current recruitment drive s, commission a comprehensive study to 
identify the root causes for the persistent lack of suitable candidates and joining issues, 
particularly at the Professor/Additional Professor levels in specialty and super -specialty 
departments at AIIMS Raipur. This study should explore factors such as competitive 
compensation packages, infrastructure and research facilities, work -life balance, 
academic environment, and geographical location. Based on these findings, the Ministry 
should develop and implement targeted strategies, which could include enhanced 

165 
 
incentives, improved institutional support for research and academic pursuits, or 
specialized outreach programs, to attract and retain experienced medical professionals 
to AIIMS Raipur. 
 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.24.4 The Committee is perturbed to observe that majority of the non -faculty posts are 
engaged either on contract or have been outsourced. The Committee believes that such adhoc 
arrangements for deployment of medical professionals on contractual basis results in 
disturbing issues of inconsistency in carrying on daily task that ultimately affects the overall 
functioning of the Institute. It also affects the motivational level of the staff due to prevailing 
uncertainties about job security and stability a nd thereby, lack of sense of belongingness 
towards the organization. Hence, the Committee calls upon the Institute to strive for 
employing staff on permanent basis for ensuring continued and better delivery of services. 
(Para 6.21 of the report) 
 
Action Taken 
 
3.24.5 AIIMS Raipur is continuously filling the Non-Faculty posts by advertising recruitment 
notices and many candidates have joined in various department/sections.3224 DR posts are of 
non-faculty positions (Out of which 798  Resident doctors,1481 Nurs ing staff and 945 
technical and nontechnical posts are sanctioned for DR . The recruitment for medical 
professionals is always done on regular basis first through direct recruitment as per prevailing 
recruitment rules. The unfilled posts are attempted to fi ll up on contractual basis to run the 
services and strengthen department. Amid the unfilled posts are advertised.  SRs and JRs are 
recruited as per the Residency Scheme throughout the year. In Nursing Cadre 1627 employees 
are working on permanent basis (DR and Promotional posts). Technical and Non -Technical 
Group ‗B‘ employees are working on permanent as well as on contractual basis.  Employees 
of Group ‗C‘ employees are working on outsourced basis as well as permanent basis. 
 
Group A

Group B
