(iii) 209 candidates joined through Direct Recruitment in the Year 2024 
 
Group C 
(i) 169 candidates joined through Direct Recruitment in the year 2024. 
(ii) 103 posts of 'Group C' sent to AIIMS Delhi to fill up by CRE-2024. 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.24.6 The Committee notes that the M inistry acknowledges the Committee 's concern 
regarding the high reliance on contractual and outsourced non -faculty staff at AIIMS 
Raipur. A significant number of candidates have joined through direct recruitment in 

166 
 
2024 across Group A, B, and C, and that 1627 nursing staff are already on p ermanent 
roles. The Ministry explicitly states that attempt s are made to fill up  unfilled posts on 
contractual basis to run the services and strengthen department," indicating that 
contractual arrangements remain a necessity due to persistent vacancies. Wh ile 
acknowledging the operational imperative, nevertheless the Committee is of the view 
that the Ministry should develop a comprehensive, time-bound strategy to systematically 
reduce the reliance on contractual and outsourced non -faculty staff at AIIMS Rai pur 
and ultimately to do away with such methods of ad -hoc recruitment. Rather the strategy 
should be more focussed on frequent direct recruitment drives for all non-faculty cadres 
and addressing the underlying reasons for permanent positions remaining vaca nt, such 
as potentially uncompetitive salaries or benefits for specific roles, to ensure long -term 
job security and foster a sense of belongingness among the workforce, thereby 
enhancing the overall stability , quality and professional proficiency  of health care 
services. 
3.25 SPECIALTY AND SUPER SPECIALTY SERVICES 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.25.1 The Committee finds that the status of vacancies in 18 Specialty and 12 Super 
Specialty Departments is equally appalling. Under Specialty Departments, out of a  total of 
146 sanctioned posts, 68 posts are lying vacant. While in Super -Specialty Departments, out of 
81 sanctioned posts, 60 posts are vacant. The Committee expresses its concern that 16 
Departments are suffering with vacancies of more than 50% of sanct ioned posts. The 
Departments i.e. Radiotherapy, Physical Medicine & Rehabilitation, Physical Medicine & 
Rehabilitation, CTVS, Endocrinology & Metabolic Diseases and Neonatology are being 
handled by a single faculty. Such huge vacancy will hamper the specia lized services provided 
by the Institute. The main purpose of having Super Specialty services at the medical Institute 
is to save the patients of the State from the compulsion of going out for treatment to other 
metro cities but that very purpose is defeat ed due to the shortage of faculty. The Committee, 
therefore, strongly recommends to the Institute to fill up the vacant posts at the earliest so as 
to provide best medical services to the patients who are visiting hospital with the hope best of 
tertiary level health care services at lower cost. The Committee desires the Ministry to take 
necessary action to get the vacant posts filled up without delay. 
(Para 6.23 of the report) 
 
Action Taken 
 
3.25.2 Observation / Recommendation of the Committee has been note d for compliance.  
AIIMS Raipur is continuously endeavouring to fill up Faculty posts. From the side of the 
Ministry several policy interventions have been introduced to improve availability of 
candidates for various faculty positions:  In addition, the Min istry has also taken some policy 
initiatives for bringing about improved availability of trained HR at National Level at large.   
 
 
 

167 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.25.3 The Committee notes that the Ministry, in its response to the critical faculty 
vacancies, particularly, in specialty and super -specialty departments at AIIMS Raipur, 
largely reiterates the ongoing efforts viz. including continuous recruitment drives, 
contractual appointments, and the implementation of a rolling advertisement system. 
While the com pletion of recruitment for 169 faculty posts and the ongoing process for 
129 more (with 34 selections) are positive indicators of effort, nevertheless, the persistent 
high vacancy rates, especially in departments being run by a single faculty, pin-point 
that current strategies  deployed by AIIMS management  are not fully addressing the 
underlying challenges of attracting and retaining specialized medical professionals. The 
Committee, therefore, further recommends that besides normal recruitment efforts, the 
Ministry, in conjunction with AIIMS Raipur  management must undertake a targeted, 
department-specific analysis for 16 departments with over 50% vacancies, particularly 
those operated by a single faculty.  
 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.25.4 The Committee is distressed to observe that crucial Super Specialty Departments like 
Surgical Gastroenterology, Nephrology, Medical Oncology/Hematology, Surgical Oncology 
and Gastroenterology are not functional at AIIMS, Raipur. The Committee believes that there 
are man y diseases which require specialty diagnosis and treatment necessitating the 
availability of Specialty and Super Specialty Departments. The Committee, therefore, 
recommends to the Institute to make these Departments functional without any delay so that 
tertiary healthcare services are made available for the patients from Chhattisgarh and 
neighbouring States. 
(Para 6.24 of the report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.25.5 AIIMS Raipur has floated advertisement for recruitment of 183 posts for Faculty 
members. Preference was given to the Super -Specialty departments for conducting the 
interview in the 1 stand2ndphases. The 1 stphase of faculty interviews against above said 
notification was conducted, wherein a faculty member as Assistant Professor in the 
Department of Nephrology (Super-specialty) was selected and subsequently joined the 
Institute. The interviews for the Departments of Surgical Gastroenterology and Surgical 
Oncology (Super -Specialty) have been conducted in the 2nd phase. Result of2nd phase of 
faculty interview has been declared. AIIMS Raipur has also included Ministry‘s initiatives for 
faculty to enhance its services through Visiting Faculty Scheme, retired faculty on contractual 
and deputation basis. AIIMS Raipur is actively pursuing filling -up of all the vacant po sts of 
faculty members in the Super -Specialty as well as Specialty departments for providing the 
best services to patients from Chhattisgarh and neighbouring States.  The department of 
Nephrology, Surgical Gastroenterology, Nephrology, Medical Oncology & Haematology   are 
functional at the Institute now. Currently Surgical Oncology is not functional, however, the 
cases of Onco surgeries are being done by the surgical departments.  AIIMS Raipur is 

168 
 
repeatedly publishing the recruitment of faculty posts for   Su rgical Oncology but No 
candidate applied/selected candidates did not show interest to join/found suitable  in the 
interview. 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.25.6 The Committee acknowledges the  efforts made by AIIMS Raipur to 
operationalize crucial Super Specialt y Departments, with the result that Department of 
Nephrology, Surgical Gastroenterology, Medical Oncology & Haematology are now 
functional. The ongoing recruitment drives, including the preference given to Super -
Specialty departments in interviews and init iatives like the Visiting Faculty Scheme, 
demonstrate a commitment to adequate staffing. However, the reply highlights the 
persistent challenge of making Surgical Oncology fully functional due to a lack of 
suitable applicants or can didates joining. The Com mittee further recommends that the 
Ministry must direct  AIIMS Raipur  management to  conduct a targeted analysis to 
understand the specific deterrents for super -specialists in Surgical Oncology. The 
Ministry should develop a tailored, aggressive recruitment strategy for Surgical 
Oncology, which could include offering enhanced incentives, dedicated research grants, 
specialized training opportunities, to ensure that this vital department becomes fully 
functional with adequate specialized faculty without further  delay, thereby ensuring 
comprehensive cancer care. 
3.26 PROCUREMENT OF EQUIPMENT 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.26.1 The Committee deprecates the Institute for poor management of crucial equipment 
worth crores. Procurement process involves commissioning , installation, training, warranty, 
after sales services like post -warranty maintenance and assured availability of spares. The 
Committee is of the view that delay in the installation of procured equipment may jeopardize 
after sales services for warranty p eriod may lapse as per the contract. The Committee would 
like the Institute to install the equipment as soon as these equipment are delivered. Further, 
the equipment must be purchased only when the suitable infrastructure and manpower for 
their installation and operation is available. 
(Para 6.27 of the report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.26.2 So far as purchases done by the Institute are concerned, there are no such cases as 
mentioned in this point, where in equipment were purchased and infrastructure and manpower 
was not available. The Institute purchases equipment only when suitable infrastructure and 
manpower for their operation and installation are available and take immediate action to 
install the equipment as soon as it is received.  As per the tender clauses of AI IMS Raipur, the 
warranty will commence only after the installation and commissioning of the particular 
equipment. In addition to this, there is always a clause of Liquidated Damages as prescribed 
under GFR to supplier in case the equipment is supplied afte r expiry of prescribed delivery 
period as mentioned in purchase order to protect the interest of AIIMS Raipur.  So far as cases 

169 
 
relating to equipment purchased through M/s. HLL are concerned, few of the equipment 
could not be installed where the designated area of the concerned departments was not handed 
over timely by the project contractors. The reasons of construction delay have already been 
brought out in the point no. 6.12. 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.26.3 The Committee has been informed that AIIMS Raipu r generally adheres to best 
practices by purchasing equipment only when infrastructure and manpower are 
available, and by ensuring warranty periods commence post -installation, with 
provisions for liquidated damages. This is a positive step towards prudent equipment 
management.  However, the submission that "few of the equipment could not be 
installed where the designated area of the departments concerned was not handed over 
timely by the project contractors" underscores a critical gap in coordination betwee n 
procurement and infrastructure development. The Committee , therefore,  further 
recommends that the Ministry implement a more robust, inter -departmental 
coordination mechanism between the procurement wing and the project/construction 
department at AIIMS Ra ipur. Such mechanism should include mandatory, pre -
procurement verification of infrastructure readiness and a staggered procurement 
schedule explicitly linked to the confirmed handover dates of designated departmental 
areas, thereby preventing future insta nces where costly equipment remains uninstalled 
due to construction delays. 
 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.26.4 The Committee feels that delay in the delivery or installation of the equipment at the 
Institute is certainly not a healthy trend. It deprive s patients of diagnostic/therapeutic benefits 
through the usage of such equipment. The delay in the supply of the equipment is the fault on 
the part of the PSA/supplier/manufacturer and the Institute must take suitable action against 
the errant supplier. The Committee is of the view that procurement of equipment is a key area 
and therefore the Institute must resolve these issues by making the PSA accountable for any 
delay and lapse. 
(Para 6.28 of the report) 
 
Action Taken 
 
3.26.5 So far as the procurement c ases pertaining to PSA, i.e. M/s HLL, adequate Liquidated 
damage have been imposed on delayed supply of the equipment whenever there is 
delay/defect on the part of PSA/ Supplier/ Manufacturer. The Final Acceptance Certificates 
against such equipment issued  by the Institute clearly mentions the date of installation and 
commissioning. The PSA, in turn, imposes suitable penalty against errant suppliers as the 
conditions of the tender.  However, the concern raised by Hon‘ble Committee is well taken 
and the Institute will take up suitable action/measures against the PSA. 
 
 

170 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.26.6 The Committee has been apprised that the Committee‟s concern over delays in 
equipment delivery and installation has been addressed by imposing liquidated damages 
on errant suppliers and that AIIMS Raipur generally ensures infrastructure and 
manpower are available before procurement. The Committee , however, recommends 
that the Ministry, be side imposing penalties must  establish a more integrated and 
proactive system t o ensure seamless coordination between procurement, infrastructure 
development, and departmental readiness at AIIMS Raipur. This system should 
mandate a pre -procurement checklist and real -time monitoring of project completion 
milestones, enabling a "just-in-time" equipment delivery and installation strategy.  
 
3.27 MEDICAL EDUCATION 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.27.1 The Committee was given to understand that out of the 30 total PG seats, only 22 seats 
were filled in July, 2018. The seats in six Departme nts i.e. Anatomy, Biochemistry, Forensic 
Medicine & Toxicology, Microbiology, Pharmacology and Physiology remained vacant. The 
Committee expects the Institute to assess the reasons for the vacant seats and take remedial 
action so that all seats get filled up from the next session onwards. 
(Para 6.34 of the Report) 
 
Action Taken 
3.27.2 As the first two rounds of counselling are conducted online, non -filling of seats is 
probably due to the candidates not exercising their choice for these departments, which ar e all 
Pre-clinical/Para-clinical departments. Reasons for candidates not opting for these seats can 
be multiple ranging from availability of seats in Clinical Departments (through NEET or State 
quotas), which provide more job opportunities including in Non -Academic Institutions and 
also to do clinical practice in society. While for non -clinical and para clinical courses, the job 
opportunities are mainly in Academic Institutions.  The choice of seats by students is beyond 
the purview/control of the Institute.  However, it may please be noted that vacant seats of the 
previous sessions(s) are included in the subsequent sessions to the extent permitted as per 
availability of teachers in the department. 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.27.3 The Committee acknowledges the Ministry‟s explanation that the persistent 
vacancies in pre -clinical and para -clinical departments are largely due to candidates‟ 
preference for clinical specialties offering wider job opportunities. The Committee , 
while noting the submission of the Minist ry, emphasizes that sustained underutilization 
of sanctioned PG seats defeats the very objective of expanding medical education 
capacity. The Committee, therefore, recommends that the Ministry, in consultation with 
the National Medical Commission (NMC), ex plore remedial measures such as offering 
structured career pathways, research incentives, fellowships, or enhanced academic and 
institutional opportunities in pre -clinical and para -clinical disciplines to make them 
more attractive to aspirants. The Committ ee, therefore, recommends the Ministry for 

171 
 
periodic assessment of demand trends across disciplines and corrective action so that 
sanctioned seats are optimally utilized and do not remain vacant year after year. 
3.28 AYUSH 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.28.1 The Committee observes that out of 5 AYUSH services, only Ayurveda services are 
functional at the Institute at the present. The Committee underlines the need for starting other 
branches of AYUSH system at the Institute so that the patients avail the fa cilities and services 
of an alternate system of medicine. Patients would be benefitted immensely as AYUSH 
medicines are not only cost effective but also widely used as a standalone option or as adjunct 
with bio medicine in the diseases entailing long durat ion of treatment. The Committee feels 
that the relevance of AYUSH has become more prominent due to this paradigm shift in the 
health sector. The Committee, therefore, recommends to the Institute to start other Ayush 
services as well as IPD services at the Institute without any further delay. 
(Para 6.46 of the report) 
 
3.28.2 The Committee notes that out of 7 sanctioned posts, only one post has been filled up 
yet. One Senior Medical Officer (Ayurveda) is working on contractual basis since 1st 
September 2014. The Committee believes that the Institute needs to strengthen its in AYUSH 
wing and provide cost effective & equitable AYUSH health care services throughout the 
country by improving access to the services as envisaged under National Ayush Mission. To 
ensure the optimal development and propagation of AYUSH systems of health care, there is 
the need to have adequate faculty in the AYUSH Department of the Institute. The Committee, 
thus, recommends to the Institute to fill up the vacant posts in the AYUSH Depar tment on 
regular basis without further delay. 
(Para 6.47 of the report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.28.3 The Institute is aware of the immense benefits that the patients can get by AYUSH 
Health Care Services and also supports the same whole heartedly. The department of AYUSH 
(Ayurveda, Yoga, Siddha and Homeopathy) is functioning with the permanent Medical 
Officers.OPD and IPD services are available.  Post for Medical Officer Unani Medicine is 
presently vacant due to resignation of the Medical Officer Unani Medicine, and  has been 
advertised. The recruitment process for the post of Senior Medical Officer -1 (Ayurveda) has 
been completed, and the position has been filled on a permanent basis following due 
procedure. 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.28.4 The Committee appreciates t he progress made by AIIMS Patna in expanding 
AYUSH services, including the initiation of OPD and IPD services and the appointment 
of permanent Medical Officers in various AYUSH disciplines. However, the Committee 
notes with concern that Unani services rema in non -operational due to a vacancy, and 
the Department overall still lacks the full sanctioned strength needed for optimal 

172 
 
functioning. Given the increasing demand for holistic and cost -effective healthcare 
solutions, the Committee reiterates the importan ce of ensuring all five AYUSH systems 
are fully operational. Therefore, the Committee further recommends that AIIMS 
Raipur expedite the recruitment process for the vacant Unani Medical Officer post and 
ensure that all sanctioned posts under AYUSH are filled on a regular basis.  
 
F. AIIMS BHUBANESWAR 
3.29 BUDGETARY ALLOCATION 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.29.1 The Committee expresses concern over the constant underutilization of funds in 
AIIMS, Bhubaneswar from the year 2011 to 2017. There has been an abysma l increase in the 
percentage utilization of funds from 2016-17 onwards, however, it remained below 75 percent 
of the total available funds. The Committee feels that improper planning and lack of 
monitoring mechanism in the initial years led to under -utilization of Grants in Aid under the 
head CCA that adversely affected the construction and other developmental activities at the 
Institute and resulted in cost overruns. This is indicative of financial mismanagement within 
the Institute. The underutilization o f available funds will only result in an inordinate delay in 
realization of both the short as well as long -term objectives of the Institute. The Committee, 
therefore, is of the view that the funds allocated to the Institute need to be reviewed from time 
to time. An effective strategy ensuring fiscal discipline by continuous and efficacious 
monitoring would help in strengthening the financial management in the Institute. 
 (Para 7.8 of the Report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.29.2 The % utilisation of funds under Capital grants from the period from 2018-19 to 2023-
24 are mentioned as below-

The utilisation of funds under GIA Capital is rapidly utilised during the perio d from 2018-19 
to 2020-21. The unutilised balance for the year 2021-22 is carried forward to the year 2022-23 
and subsequently refunded as per the direction of the Ministry after implementation of TSA.  
Hence, after implementation of TSA, no surplus fund laying with the Institute Accounts. 
 
 

173 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.29.3 The Committee takes note of the significant improvement in fund utilization at 
AIIMS Bhubaneswar in recent years, particularly the near full utilization of capital 
grants in 2018-19, 2019-20, 2020-21, and 2023-24. However, the Committee observes that 
in 2021 -22 and 2022 -23, utilization again dipped to below 75 percent, which points 
towards persisting gaps in financial planning and project execution. While the 
implementation of TSA has addres sed the issue of surplus funds lying idle, the 
Committee recommends that the Ministry ensure a robust mechanism for real -time 
monitoring of fund flow, project progress, and  timely corrective action , and 
institutionalize periodic financial audits and  capacity-building of its financial 
management systems to prevent recurrence of underutilization and consistency in fiscal 
discipline. 
 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.29.4 The Committee finds that funds allocated in the year 2017 -18 remained unaudited. 
The Committee underlines the need for an audit of the allocated funds regularly. Needless to 
say, audit ensures financial reliability and compliance with laws and statutory regulations. 
 (Para 7.9 of the Report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.29.5 The Director General of Audit (C & AG) has conducted Financial Audit regularly of 
the Accounts of AIIMS Bhubaneswar to ensures financial reliability and compliance with 
laws and statutory regulations. SAR for the Financial Year 2018 -19 to 2023 -24, have been 
received from the Audit Auth ority for incorporation in the Annual Report and submission to 
the Ministry of Health & Family Welfare for laying before both the Houses of the Parliament. 
The Audit Report is available on the Institute website. 
Further Recommendation 
3.29.6 The Committee notes with satisfaction that regular financial audits of AIIMS 
Bhubaneswar are now being conducted by the CAG and that the audit reports from 
2018-19 to 2023 -24 have been received and incorporated in the Annual Reports. While 
this is a welcome step towards  ensuring financial accountability, the Committee 
recommends that the Ministry ensure timely completion and disclosure of audits every 
financial year without delay. The Committee also stresses that audit observations, if any, 
must be acted upon promptly wi th a clear compliance mechanism and periodic 
reporting to avoid recurrence of lapses.

3.31 STATUS OF FACULTY AND NON-FACULTY STRENGTH 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.31.1 The Committee is perturbed to note that approximately 40 percent of faculty posts and 
78 percent non-faculty posts are lying vacant at the Institute. The Committee notes that out of 
54 sanctioned posts for Professor, only 17 posts have been filled up and under 2 4 
Departments, posts for Professor are lying vacant. Further, 13 Departments are being run 
either by an Associate or Assistant Professor. The Committee believes that shortage of health 
personnel to this extent is a critical stressor for hospitals. The Comm ittee underlines the need 
for requisite number of health personnel in place for smooth functioning of the Institute. 
(Para 7.33 of the report) 
 
Action Taken 
 
3.31.2 It is submitted that the vacant faculty posts and non-faculty posts have been advertised 
by AIIMS Bhubaneswar on more than one occasion. The institute did not get suitable 
candidates to fill up these vacant posts. The institute again advertised all 37 vacant posts of 
Professors on 28.01.2019. 
 
Faculty  
The recruitment for filling 108 posts of Faculty Advt. was published on 13.11.2020 but due to 
a change in the reservation roster policy of Government of India, the advertisement was 
cancelled and a fresh advertisement of 112 faculty posts was issued on 30.08.2021.  The 
interview for the same has been completed on 08.12.2021 and the result of selected candidates 
will be issued on approval of HR- Sub Committee of GB.  
 
Non-Faculty  
The Recruitment Rule for non -faculty positions of AIIMS, Bhubaneswar is under revision. 
After obtaining the necessary appr oval for such revision all the vacant posts (Direct 
Recruitment) in Gr. B & Gr. C will be filled up through open competition.  Presently, the 
recruitment for the post of Nursing Officer is being carried out centrally by AIIMS, New 
Delhi through a common ope n competitive exam, NORCET and at AIIMS vacancies of such 
post are being filled regularly, as per the choices exercised by the qualified candidates of the 
said examination. In 2020, AIIMS, Bhubaneswar projected 600 vacancies for Nursing Officer 
to be fille d through NORCET -2020 and in turn, 403 Nursing Officers out of 439 who opted 
AIIMS BBSR as their first choice has joined in AIIMS, Bhubaneswar till the first phase of 
candidate allocation. Further, more candidates will join against the said 600 vacancies o n 
completion of the document verification of the 118 candidates allotted in the second phase of 
candidate allocation. 
 
3.31.3 Moreover, for NORCET -2021, AIIMS, Bhubaneswar has projected 296 vacant posts 
of Nursing Officer for which recruitment is under pro cess. Regarding non-faculty Group ‗A‘, 
the recruitment process has already been completed for the post of Antenatal Medical Officer, 

176

completed and the result of the selected candidate will be issued shortly.  As most of the

3.33 STATUS OF OPERATION THEATRES AND WARDS/BEDS 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.33.1 The Committee takes note that out of 25 sanctioned OTs, only 7 are functional at 
present resulting into waiting time of one week to four months for surgical proced ures under 
various Departments. The Committee is of the view that the Institute was established with the 
aim of providing affordable and reliable tertiary healthcare services to the people and 
preventing them from shuttling to other metro cities in search of tertiary level health care 
services. Further, the Committee finds the non -availability of gas pipe -line in OTs 
unacceptable. The Committee recommends to the Institute to make the remaining 18 
sanctioned OTs functional without any delay. The Institute sh ould also ensure that OTs are 
equipped with latest equipment including Medical Gas Pipeline System (MGPS). 
(Para 7.45 of the report) 
Action Taken

3.33.3 The Committee appr eciates the significant progress made by AIIMS 
Bhubaneswar in operationalizing all 25 sanctioned Modular Operation Theatres (MoTs) 
and ensuring the installation of the Medical Gas Pipeline System (MGPS), thereby 
addressing a critical infrastructure gap. Ho wever, the Committee notes that despite the 
sanction of 112 ICU beds in the main hospital, 86 beds in the Trauma Centre, and 36 
beds in the Burn Centre, only a total of 130 ICU beds (112 + 12 trauma centre beds + 06 
burn centre beds) are currently function al and 30 burn centre beds remains to be 
installed. This indicates a considerable underutilization of sanctioned critical care 
infrastructure. The Committee , therefore, further recommends that the Institute 
expedite the process of making all remaining ICU beds functional by addressing any 
outstanding manpower, equipment, or logistical constraints. The Committee also desires 
that the Institute may furnish details of occupancy rate of ICU beds in various 
wards/departments.

3.34 AYUSH FACILITIES 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.34.1 The Committee observes that out of five services under AYUSH, only two services 
i.e. Yoga and Ayurveda OPD services are functional at the AIIMS, Bhubaneswar. The 
Committee is of the considered view that the AYUSH systems offer a n alternative system of 
medicine to the patients and is generally cheaper and affordable with lesser side effects. Gaps 
in India‘s healthcare sector are huge with a large proportion of the population virtually being 
denied access to healthcare facilities. To address these gaps, there is a need to support 
AYUSH systems of medicine and education, particularly in vulnerable and far flung areas. 
The Committee desires the Institute to make earnest efforts to start other AYUSH services as 
well as IPD services at the Institute. The Committee recommends that apprehensions about 
the efficacy and safety of AYUSH systems need to be allayed through undertaking suitable 
desired level of scientific research and development of good AYUSH practices. Further 
Standard Treatment protocols need to be developed also to build public trust in these systems. 
(Para 7.54 of the report) 
 
3.34.2 The Committee is distressed to note that out of six sanctioned posts at the AYUSH 
Department, only one post has been filled up so far and that too on contractual basis. The 
Committee believes that without adequate manpower, the Institute will not be able to achieve 
the objective of strengthening AYUSH services. The Committee, therefore, recommends to 
the Institute to fill up the vacant posts on regular basis without further delay. 
 (Para 7.55 of the report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.34.3 The recommendation of the Hon‘ble Committee has been noted for future guidance.  
The Ministry has decided to integrate the AYUSH in the tertiary care system in new AIIMS 
by augmenting the strength of AYUSH professionals and by also creating scientific posts in 
order to undertake academic Ph.D programme and finding solutions to India‘s public health 
challenge. M/o AYUSH had advised that each of the new and upcoming AIIMS shoul d have 
augmented faculty / doctor strength and higher focus in one particular traditional system of 
medicine which may be having popularity in the particular region. Each such augmented 
medicine system will have additional 16 posts, consisting of posts of Professor, Additional 
Professor, Associate Professor, Assistant Professor, SR/JR posts. This will be over and above 
the already sanctioned five Medical Officer posts, one each in Ayurveda, Yoga and 
Naturopathy, Unani, Siddha and Homeopathy. The above struc ture of AYUSH Department 
has been included in the Standard Staffing Pattern approved by CIB. The proposal for 
creation of posts is being taken up with Department of Expenditure accordingly.  Current 
status of posts is as follows: 
SI.NO. POST SANCTIONED AVAILABLE 
1 SENIOR MEDICAL OFFICER 1 1 
2 MEDICAL OFFICER 5 4 
3 YOGA INSTRUCTOR 1 1 

180 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.34.4 The Committee notes with satisfaction that the Institute has taken steps to fill  up 
the sanctioned posts in the AYUSH Department, with five ou t of six posts now occupied, 
thereby reflecting progress in addressing the earlier manpower shortfall. However, the 
Committee also emphasizes that mere availability of posts is not sufficient unless backed 
by regular appointments, appropriate infrastructur e, and full operationalization of 
services. Therefore, the Committee desires that the remaining vacancy be filled up on a 
priority basis through regular recruitment. Additionally, the Institute should ensure 
that all appointed personnel are provided with necessary facilities and support to deliver 
quality AYUSH services. The Committee also urges the Institute to develop a roadmap 
for expanding AYUSH services beyond OPD to include IPD and academic functions, 
aligning with the Ministry‟s broader vision of int egrated and regionally adapted 
traditional healthcare delivery. 
 
G. Upgradation of Government Medical Colleges/ Institutions  
 
3.35 BUDGETARY ALLOCATION AND FUNDING PATTERN 
 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.35.1 The Committee takes into account the budgetary allocation and funding pattern of 
select 73 GMCs under five phases in PMSSY for which the Ministry of Health and Family 
Welfare has released a total amount of Rs.5239.71 crore. However, the State share is pending 
in about 50% case of GMCs for upgradation w orks under Phase III. The Committee observes 
that delay in release of State share has impacted the upgradation projects adversely. The 
Committee desires the Ministry to urgently take the matter with State Governments concerned 
to release their share at the earliest so that the work of GMCs does not suffer. The Committee, 
in this regard, would like to emphasize that the Ministry/State Governments concerned must 
comply with the fund sharing commitment to maintain the sanctity of project. The Ministry 
needs to put in place a mechanism for release of funds within a stipulated time period from 
both Centre and State Governments. The Committee, therefore, recommends concerted action 
on part of both the Centre and State Governments to make the upgradation project su ccessful. 
The Committee, therefore, recommends that all out efforts need to be taken by the Ministry to 
get all the procedural formalities/ necessary approvals done well in time and the State 
Governments concerned need to be persuaded and advised regularly  in this regard. A 
quarterly status report on the pending position with respect to the State share may also be 
provided to enable the Committee to take up the matter with the concerned State Government 
as most of the delays are due to internal processes of the States. 
 (Para 8.10 of the report) 
 
 
 
 

181 
 
Action Taken 
 
3.35.2 Meetings have been held with State Governments for release of funds pending from 
State‘s share. Consequently, all State Governments, except for Government of Andhra 
Pradesh in respect of Upgradation of GMC at Anantapur and Vijayawada, have fully released 
their share of funds. Government of Andhra Pradesh is being requested to release its share in 
respect of above two projects. Matter will be vigorously pursued with the Government of 
Andhra Pradesh in this regard.    
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.35.3 The Committee acknowledges the progress made by the Ministry in ensuring that 
almost all State Governments, barring Andhra Pradesh, have released their share of 
funds under Phase III of the PMSSY upgr adation projects. However, the continued 
delay by the Government of Andhra Pradesh in releasing its share for the GMCs at 
Anantapur and Vijayawada remains a matter of concern. The Committee, therefore, 
recommends that the Ministry intensify its efforts by engaging at the highest 
administrative and political levels with the Government of Andhra Pradesh to expedite 
the release of the pending State share. Additionally, the Committee reiterates its earlier 
recommendation that a formal mechanism be institutional ized to monitor and report 
fund releases on a quarterly basis, including reasons for delays and follow -up actions 
taken, so as to ensure timely implementation of upgradation works and enable proactive 
intervention wherever needed.

3.36.1 The Committee during the course of its study visit observes that construction activities 
at various Institutes are delayed. The reasons for delays are manifold including (i) n on-
availability of encumbrance free land (ii) delays in getting clearances for excavation (iii) tree 
cutting and other site related conditions (iv) changes made in the scope and quantum of work 
(v) post contract changes in drawings and quantities (vi) dela ys in providing drawings (vii) 
delay in release of mobilization advance and payments to contractor (viii) delay in provision 
of services (ix) delay in work due to shortage of labour (x) delay in finalization/tendering of 
medical equipment (xi) changes in s ite after award of work delay in shifting of existing 
electrical lines (xii) internal disturbances in some States etc. The Committee urges the 
Ministry to flag these issues with the State Government concerned to undertake appropriate 
steps to remove the bo ttlenecks causing delay in the completion of construction activities at 
various GMCs. 
(Para 8.28 of the report)

from eight months to seven years. In other six GMCs approved under phase -II, the 
upgradation of only three GMCs has been completed though with the delay of several years. 
In the remaining three colleges, the work has n ot been completed even after many years. The

initially that were scheduled to be completed in July, 2011.

Further Recommendation 
 
3.36.6 The Committee appreciates the concerted efforts made by the Ministry in 
coordinating with State Governments and relevant stakeholders to address the multiple 
causes of delays in construction activities at various Government Medical Colleges 
(GMCs), resulting in the completion of 71 out of 75 projects. However, the Committee 
expresses concern over the continued delays in the remaining four projects  viz. PMCH, 
GMC (Surat) and GTBH (Delhi) , particularly due to pending State share commitments 
and GMC (Cuttack) due to delayed submission of Detailed Project Reports (DPRs). The 
Committee, therefore, strongly recommends that the Ministry should establish a fixed 
timeline in coordination with the concerned State Governments for resolving the 
outstanding issues in these four projects. A robust escalation mechanism should also be 
instituted fo r projects facing chronic delays, including structured fortnightly reviews 
and submission of status updates to the Committee. Furthermore, resilient follow-up 
action is needed in the case of the Guru Teg Bahadur Hospital project in Delhi, where 
financial commitments are still pending, to prevent further delay in project completion. 
 
3.36.7 The Committee takes note of the reasons cited such as issues related to land 
availability and statutory clearances  as being largely under the purview of the State 
Governments and implementing agencies. The Committee, therefore, recommends that 
for future projects, the Ministry must ensure timely pre-approval protocols, including 
mandatory submission of encumbrance -free land certificates and statutory clearances 
before initiating tendering or construction.  
3.37 PROCUREMENT OF EQUIPMENT 
Recommendations/Observations  
3.37.1 The Committee observes that there is an acute shortage of equipment at the various 
GMCs approved for upgradation under PMSSY. The primary reasons being de lays in 
placement of orders, change in the model of the equipment and lack of follow up by Institutes 
with the nominated PSA after placement of orders. The Committee apprehends that in the 
absence of the equipment, the scheme gets affected and patients are  deprived of upgraded 
services. The CAG has further highlighted a grave concern i.e 75 equipment have been 
supplied by HLL at a cost of Rs. 3.26 crore but are not anywhere in record either with HLL or 
the concerned GMCs. The Committee takes a serious note of this lapse and recommends for 
suitable remedial action to address this criminal wastage of public fund. The Committee 
would also like to be apprised of the action taken by the Ministry for fixing accountability for 
this lapse. 
(Para 8.42 of the report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.37.2 It is submitted that the current position with respect to Installation of Equipment at 39 
sites under Phase-III is as below: 
 

184 
 
Out of total 141 items under Phase III indicated as pendency under this Para, order for 48 
items has already been placed and the remaining are under procurement. 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.37.3 The Ministry's Action Taken Notes, while acknowledging the equipment shortage 
at GMCs under PMSSY, primarily focuses on the current procurement status for Phase 
III, indicating that orders for 48 out of 141 pending items have been placed and the rest 
are under procurement. This response, however, completely omits addressing the 
Committee's serious concern over the wastage of public funds because of the fact that a 
total of 75 numbers of equipment worth Rs. 3.26 crore remained unaccounted for by 
both HLL and the GMCs  concerned, as highlighted by the CAG. It also fails to address 
the underlying issues of delays in order placement, changes in equipment models, and 
lack of follo w-up, or to outline any measures for fixing accountability for these lapses. 
Therefore, the Committee strongly reiterates its recommendation for a comprehensive 
and urgent remedial action plan to recover or account for the 75 missing equipment s as 
identified by the CAG, along with a detailed report on the investigation conducted to fix 
accountability for such lapse in public fund management. Furthermore, the Ministry 
must chalk out a concrete strategy, with strict timelines and clear performance metrics, 
and streamline the entire procurement -to-installation process, establishing robust 
inventory management systems ensuring rigorous oversight a nd follow -up action with 
PSAs and GMCs to prevent future occurrences of such mismanagement. 
 
 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.37.4 The Committee is, therefore, of the considered view that procurement of equipment on 
single bid basis reduces the competition in the market and minimizes the opportunity of 
competitive bidding. The Committee would like the Ministry to adhere to the procedure of the 
multiple bids in order to achieve competitive pricing. 
(Para 8.43 of the report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.37.5 Observations / Recommendations of the Committee have been noted for guidance. 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.37.6 The Committee notes that its recommendation regarding avoidance of single bid 
procurement to encourage competitive pricing has been acknowledged by the Ministry. 
However, in view of the significant financial outlay involved in medical equipment 
procurement and the need for t ransparency and efficiency in public procurement 
processes, the Committee recommends that the Ministry should issue detailed guidelines 
to all implementing agencies and procurement units under its purview, explicitly 
discouraging single bid procurement except in exceptional and justifiable circumstances. 
These exceptions should be subject to prior approval by a higher authority and duly 

185 
 
documented. Additionally, the Committee recommends that the Ministry must establish 
a monitoring mechanism to regularly re view procurement cases, especially those 
finalized on a single bid basis. 
 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.37.7 The Committee observes that the revised policy and procedure formulated for 
procurement of medical equipment has not been followed in letter an d spirit. The Committee 
strongly recommends that there should be minimum pendency and in the procurement process 
and all pending procurement tenders be closed without any further delay. The Ministry should 
also formulate effective monitoring mechanism for timely receipt, installation and proper 
functioning of equipment at GMCs so that the equipment are utilized properly for the purpose 
for which they were procured. A quarterly update on gap analysis, delivery of equipment, 
delays if any, with respect to all GMCs should be submitted to the Committee by the PSA. 
(Para 8.44 of the report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.37.8 Observations / Recommendations of the Committee have been noted for future 
guidance. The progress of procurement of equipment is reviewed periodically alo ng with the 
progress of construction so that better synchronization between procurement activity and 
construction activity can be achieved. 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.37.9 The Committee acknowledges that the Ministry has taken note of its 
recommendations a nd is reviewing the progress of equipment procurement alongside 
construction activities  with respect to all GMCs . The Committee,  in this regard,  
recommends that a centralized digital dashboard be developed for real -time tracking of 
tender issuance, approva l, delivery, installation, and operationalization of equipment 
across all GMCs. The effective monitoring mechanism must ensure that there should be 
no delay in procurement of medical equipments in any GMC. 
 
3.38 ENHANCEMENT OF PG SEATS 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.38.1The Committee notes that as per the joint gap analysis and DPR, the number of PG 
seats would be 666 in 39 GMCs under Phase-III Projects and 355 PG seats in 14 GMCs under 
Phase IV & V Projects. The Committee, however, is of the view that th e enhancement of PG 
seats without having adequate faculty/staff would not be result - yielding. The Committee, 
therefore, recommends to the Ministry to focus on the employment of the faculty along with 
enhancement of the PG seats to the desired level of fac ulty-student ratio. Hence, the 
Ministry/State Governments must strive to fill up the vacant seats within a stipulated time 

186 
 
period and basic other infrastructure for teaching must be in place for facilitating the 
imparting of medical education to extended number of students. 
(Para 8.46 of the report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.38.2 It is submitted that Agreement of the State Govt. has been obtained through MOUs 
entered with each state to the effect that the State Govt. will create and fill up required faculty 
and non -faculty post to operationalize the new super speciality facilities.   However, as 
advised by the Committee, the matter for employment of faculty will be pursued with the 
State Govt. with greater vigour.  In addition, Ministry has also in various forum take up the 
matter of recruitment with the State Government during the course of up -gradation of the 
GMCIs. The need for creating required posts of specialist doctors was again highlighted in 
Quarterly Review Meeting (QRM) held on 19-20th September 2019 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.38.3 The Committee appreciates the efforts in securing commitments from State 
Governments through MOUs for creating and filling up required faculty and non -
faculty posts, and its continued engagement with States through review meetings.  
However, the Committee expects that the Ministry should have furnished the 
development/progress made and outcome achieved of various steps taken, in this regard, 
during September 2019 to May 2025. G iven that enhancement of PG seats without 
proportionate f aculty undermines the quality of medical education, the Committee 
recommends that the Ministry develop a structured timeline with performance 
indicators for faculty recruitment aligned with the increase in PG seats. Further, the 
Ministry should regularly m onitor compliance by States and the status of faculty -
student ratios across GMCs on a quarterly basis.  
 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.38.4 The Committee, further, recommends to the Ministry that the PG seats in GMCs 
should be increased in view of the e xisting infrastructure and adequate faculty. When 
compared to new AIIMS like Institutions, GMCs are better equipped to provide medical 
education and clinical exposure to the PG students. GMCs are in a better position to address 
the regional imbalances in S tates and enhancing their PG seats could increase the outreach of 
tertiary health care in the remote areas of the country. The Committee strongly recommends 
for a substantial enhancement in the PG seats in all the GMCs so as to address this critical gap 
in medical education. The Committee recommends that the Ministry should prepare a 
comprehensive action plan for augmenting PG seats in GMCs which are being upgraded 
under PMSSY. 
(Para 8.47 of the report) 
 
 

187 
 
Action Taken 
 
3.38.5 It is submitted that the Minist ry has work out plan for increase in PG seats in 
consultation with the respective State Government and also taken their consultation through 
MoU with them.  The matter is being pursued with the State Government in this regard.  The 
need for creating additio nal P.G seats in these GMCs was again highlighted in Quarterly 
Review Meeting held on 19 -20th September, 2019 and State Govt. requested for taking 
necessary action in this direction. 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.38.6 The Committee desires that the Ministry s hould have furnished the details of 
outcome of the efforts so made to collaborate with State Governments through MoUs 
and its continued engagement via review meetings to enhance PG seats in GMCs,  
especially during the period September 2019 to May 2025. The Committee, furthermore, 
recommends that a time -bound and target -driven roadmap be finalized and 
implemented for each GMC, detailing the proposed increase in PG seats aligned with 
available infrastructure and faculty strength. The Committee further urges t he Ministry 
to institute a central monitoring cell to track the progress of PG seat augmentation 
across all GMCs  along with status of professional proficiency generated due to 
enhancement of PG seats in all GMCs. The Committee understands that GMCs are the  
gateway for delivery of adequate, accessible and affordable healthcare services at grass 
root level imparting medical education and clinical exposure at doors, and effective 
means of removing regional imbalances and critical medical education gap at sourc e. 
The Ministry, in tandem with State Government, should chalk out „Action Plan‟ to 
achieve the said objectives.   
 
3.39 SUPER SPECIALTY BLOCKS 
 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.39.1 The Committee is, therefore, of the firm view that in the modern age wher e heart 
diseases have become so common, super specialty services without a CATH lab would be 
short of special facility for delivery of adequate service to the suffering heart patient. The 
Committee, therefore, recommends that all the SSB envisaged should i nclude functional 
CATH lab under the project should be made functional without much procrastination. 
  (Para 8.52 of the Report) 
 
Action Taken 
 
3.39.2 The Committees Recommendations have been noted for compliance. The requirement 
of facilities including CA TH Lab has been finalised in the Gap Analysis  Meetings in 
consultation with the respective GMCs, Institutes/State Governments and accordingly 18 Cath 
Labs have been created under Phase III.  The GMCs/ State Govt. have prioritized their 
requirements and decided the facilities to be provided.  As is the limit budget (Phase III – 150 

188 
 
crore each, Phase IV – Rs.200 crore each) all needed facilities could not be covered in the 
projects.  
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.39.3 The Committee takes note of the creation of 18 Cath Labs under Phase III in 
consultation with GMCs, Institutes, and State Governments. However, the Committee 
expresses the concern that due to budgetary limits, not all required Cath Labs could be 
established, which may compromise the availability of critical cardiac care services in 
certain regions. The Committee, therefore, recommends that the Ministry, in 
coordination with the State Governments, should devise a phased plan to ensure that all 
medical colleges and institutions with super -specialty ser vices are equipped with 
functional Cath Labs at the earliest. Additional financial support, either through 
enhanced central allocation, phased funding, Corporate Social Responsibility or 
convergence with other healthcare schemes, should be explored so that  budgetary 
constraints do not hinder the provision of life-saving cardiac care infrastructure. 
3.40 GENERATION OF EMPLOYMENT 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.40.1 The Committee observes with concern that no action has been taken by the Ministry 
and the Sta te Governments concerned to generate employment at the GMCs approved for 
upgradation under PMSSY. The Committee is of the considered view that health care sector 
has tremendous potential for employment generation. The Committee, therefore, strongly 
recommends that the Central and State Government must to chalk out suitable strategy to 
explore various avenues for employment generation in different segments of healthcare 
sector. The Committee, in this regard, believes that higher investment in the health sect or 
would yield greater opportunities for employment generation. The Committee is, however, 
perturbed to observe that many Departments at the various GMCs are not functional due to 
the shortage of the manpower. The Committee, therefore, strongly recommends to the 
Ministry to analyse the manpower requirement of each GMC and advise the State 
Governments to create and fill up adequate number of posts, accordingly. The Committee also 
recommends that vacant posts must be filled up as early as possible so as to pa ve the way for 
smooth delivery of health services to the people. 
(Para 8.55 of the report) 
 
Action Taken 
 
3.40.2 The Recommendations of the Committee have been noted for guidance. The matter of 
creation of posts / filling up of Faculty and Non -faculty position is regularly taken up with 
State Governments in different review meetings and through D.O letters.  The need for 
creating required posts of specialist doctors and paramedics was again highlighted in 
Quarterly Review Meeting held on 19-20th September 2019. 
 

189 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.40.3 The Committee acknowledges the communication with State Governments 
regarding manpower requirements, however, the Committee expresses concern that the 
Ministry has not furnished updated response covering details of progr ess made during 
September 2019 to May 2025 in compliance of recommendation for exploring huge 
employment generation opportunities in all GMCs identified for up -gradation under 
PMSSY. The Committee, therefore, recommends that a structured manpower audit be 
conducted for each GMC upgraded under PMSSY to assess current staffing gaps across 
faculty, non -faculty, healthcare allied and professional services , and support services. 
The Ministry should also set up a robust follow -up mechanism to ensure time -bound 
action by State Governments on the creation and filling of posts , at various levels so as 
to open employment generation opportunities and ensuring that all departments of 
GMCs are made functional.  
3.41 STUDY VISIT OF THE COMMITTEE 
 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.41.1 The Committee also notes that wide regional imbalance exists in the delivery of 
Health Care Services in the State. The remote regions of the State are still out of the reach of 
tertiary health care services as most of such facilities are estab lished in and around Patna. 
There is a severe lack of adequate medical care and facilities in Government Sector in North 
Bihar, especially in the districts of Saharsha, Madhepura, Purnea, Kathiar, Kishanganj and 
Farbishganj, i.e., Koshi region. The Committ ee desires that the Ministry may explore the 
possibility for setting up of AIIMS like institutions in this extremely poor and backward 
region to cater to the health facilities and services to the poor at the economical/lower cost. 
The Committee, therefore, strongly urges upon the Central and the State Governments to take 
into account the need and requirement of health care services in these economically deprived 
regions and work towards decentralization of the health care facilities. 
 
  (Para 8.65 of the Report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.41.2 It is submitted that three Districts in Bihar namely Purnia, Saran (Chhapra) and 
Samastipur have been approved under Phase I of the Centrally Sponsored Scheme namely, 
'Establishment of new medical colleges attached with existing District/Referral Hospitals' at a 
cost of Rs.189 crore each to be shared between Centre and State Government in the ratio of 
60:40. Complete amount of central share amounting to Rs.113.40 crore has been released to 
the State for colleges at Purnia, Samasti pur and Saran. The medical college at Purnia is 
functional from academic session 2023-24. Further, as informed by the State Government, the 
construction of medical colleges at Saran and Samastipur is almost complete.  
 
3.41.3 The State Government approved transfer of 200.02 acre land in Darbhanga Medical 
College & Hospital (DMCH) campus and in March, 2023, alternatively offered a new site 

190 
 
(Measuring 187.44 Acre) situated on EkmiShobhan Bypass, Anchal - Bahadurpur, Mouza -
Balia, Thana No.-120/2 for establishment of AIIMS Darbhanga.  A Central (Technical) Team 
was constituted to assess the feasibility and suitability of the proposed site and it 
recommended that the site is feasible for establishing AIIMS  and suggested that necessary 
geotechnical investigation, liquefaction hazard analysis, Hydrological Study/Investigation and 
Analysis for drainage planning and flood management should be undertaken for ensuring 
structural stability. 
 
3.41.4 On 13.09.2024, the work of conducting the necessary Geotechnical investigat ions, 
Hydrological Study etc. was entrusted by the Ministry to IIT Delhi. On 18.09.2024, M/s 
HSCC (India) Ltd. was appointed as the Executing Agency (EA) for Pre -Investment Works 
and Main Works. Subsequently, an  MoU was signed on 12.11.2024 between MoHFW a nd 
State Government of Bihar, wherein, the State Govt. has committed to provide permanent 
electricity connection of required load from two alternate power sources, 4 -lane road 
connectivity and potable water supply for the Institute and has also agreed to u ndertake land 
filling. The BhoomiPujan on the site was conducted on 13 thNovember, 2024 and the logo of 
the Institute was launched by Hon'ble PM the same day. 
 
3.41.5 In December 2024, Architectural & Design Consultant for the project was finalized by 
M/s HSCC. Topographical Studies including Soil Investigation, Soil testing, Site Survey & 
Contour map work has been completed and the report has been shared with IIT Delhi . 
Hydrological Study has also been completed. The land for the project has been demarcated  by 
the State Government. Masterplan and Drawings and estimation of the Main building is under 
preparation in consultation with IIT Delhi. The Executing Agency has awarded the  work for 
the construction of boundary wall of AIIMS Darbhanga on 16.04.2025. Mob ilization of men 
and material have started at the site. 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.41.6 The Committee takes into account the progress made in strengthening healthcare 
infrastructure in Bihar through the establishment of new medical colleges at Purnia, 
Saran, and Samastipur as well as the initiation of AIIMS Darbhanga.  
3.41.7 The Committee, however, emphasizes that the Koshi/ Simanchal region and other 
underserved districts such as Saharsha, Madhepura, Katihar, Kishanganj,  Araria and 
Farbishganj continue to  remain deprived of adequate tertiary healthcare facilities. The 
Committee, therefore, recommends that the Ministry, in collaboration with the State 
Government, should prepare a comprehensive roadmap to ensure equitable distribution 
of tertiary healthcare services across all backward and flood -prone Koshi/Simanchal 
region of Bihar. The Committee understands that site selection for any AIIMS like 
Institution requires a detailed assessment of the geographical location and the 
demographic distribution. Easy ac cessibility and transportation availability is also the 
other criteria being taken into consideration for setting up of AIIMS like institution . 
Moreover, air connectivity to the place has been one of the major basis for site selection 
for AIIMS. The Committee takes into consideration the poor human development index 

191 
 
in the Simanchal region and poor ratio of doctor against the national average 
necessitates the setting up of AIIMS like institution in the Simanchal region.  The 
proposed expressway from Patna to  Purnia and commencement of air connectivity to 
and fro Purnia has brought the Koshi region on national platform that fulfils the criteria 
of easy accessibility and transportation for setting up of AIIMS like institution. Further, 
being a backward and unde rserved region, Koshi/Simanchal region is the suitable site 
for establishment of AIIMS like institution to bridge the regional  imbalance in 
healthcare access. The Committee, therefore, urges the Government for setting up of 
clear timelines for exploring th e feasibility of setting u p additional tertiary healthcare 
facilities through AIIMS like institution in the Koshi/Simanchal region.  
H. OBSERVATIONS/RECOMMENDATIONS ON COMMON ISSUES 
3.42 EXPANSION PROGRAMME OF AIIMS 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.42.1 The Committee is of the view that AIIMS like Institutes and hospitals should be 
excluded from the NBC guidelines or some amendments in the guidelines can be made so that 
AIIMS like Institutions are allowed to carry out vertical expansion. This will not only ensure 
the optimum utilization of land but also resolve the delays caused due to unavailability of 
land. Vertical expansion will also lead to an organized set up. The OPDs can be operated from 
the top floors and the other critical centres such as trauma an d emergency, ICUs, HDUs, OTs, 
etc. can be functional from the lower floors of the building. The Ministry of Health and 
Family Welfare should take up this proposal with the Ministry of Housing and Urban Affairs 
and study the feasibility and implications of the recommendation so as to facilitate vertical 
expansion in all AIIMS. 
(Para 9.18 of the report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.42.2 Observations / Recommendations of the Committee have been noted for future 
guidance. As regards exclusion from the NBC guidelines or amen dment in the guidelines to 
enable to the AIIMS to carry out the vertical expansion, the matter has already been taken 
with Ministry of Urban Development and NBCC. Further, action shall be taken after 
receiving the recommendation /suggestion of MoUD/MoHUPA.  Incidentally, it may be 
mentioned that FAR for AIIMS Nagpur has been increased and the FAR of Bilaspur AIIMS 
building has also been increased.  A communication has been sent to Secretary, Ministry of 
Housing and Urban Affairs highlighting the observations of the Committee in March, 2019. 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.42.3 The Committee has been informed that the recommendation for excluding 
AIIMS-like institutions from or amending NBC guidelines to allow vertical expansion 
has been "noted for future guidance,"  and the matter has been taken up with the 
Ministry of Urban Development and NBCC. The mention of increased FAR for AIIMS 

192 
 
Nagpur and Bilaspur is a positive sign, suggesting some progress in facilitating vertical 
growth. However, the communication with the Ministry of Housing and Urban Affairs 
dates back to March 2019, and the response lacks concrete timelines or definitive 
outcomes from the action taken so far , particularly regarding a broader policy change 
that would benefit all AIIMS. The Committee, therefore, recommends that the Ministry 
of Health and Family Welfare must actively pursue a time -bound resolution with the 
Ministry of Housing and Urban Affairs regarding the proposed amendments to the 
National Building Code (NBC) guidelines for vertical expans ion of hospital buildings  in 
AIIMS-like initiatives, wherever required. 
 
3.43 SHORTAGE OF FACULTY AND NON FACULTY STAFF 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.43.1 The Committee appreciates that the Ministry is also considering common recruitment 
process for the  non-faculty of all AIIMS by clubbing together all the vacancies. The 
Committee concurs with the view of the Ministry that one AIIMS can hold recruitment of a 
particular category for all the other AIIMS like Institutions. The Committee is of the view that 
the Ministry should take this proposal with the Central Institute Body (CIB) and take 
necessary steps to execute the same on ground level. The Committee expects that CIB would 
play a pro-active role in this matter. 
(Para 9.34 of the report) 
 
3.43.2 The Committee strongly recommends for a centralised recruitment process for all non-
faculty staff preferably monitored by AIIMS, Delhi or through a centralized reputed agency, 
so that the irregularities in the recruitment process are minimized and the issue of st aff 
shortage is addressed through a nationwide selection process. The issues related to shortage of 
faculty staff have been dealt in respective AIIMS Chapter.  
(Para 9.35 of the report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.43.3 On the basis of the Parliamentary Standing Commit tee observations, the Central 
Institute Body (CIB) in its 3 rd meeting held on 24.01.2019 advised all AIIMS to conduct 
recruitments for Non -faculty (Technical) cadres on centralized basis by applying the 
following measures:  
 
 AIIMS Delhi will conduct centra lized recruitment for all AIIMS in case of Nursing 
Cadres. 
 For rest of technical cadres (19), the six new AIIMS will do the centralized 
recruitment by suitably dividing the cadres amongst themselves.

3.44 DIALYSIS FACILITIES 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
3.44.1 Attention of the Committee has also been drawn to the procurement of Dialysis 
machine at a much higher price in the new AIIMS. The Committee observes that same 
dialysis machines with the same specifications have been procured by State Government at a 
much lower  price. Moreover, the same dialysis machines have been procured under the 
MPLAD scheme at one third of the price paid by new AIIMS. The Committee takes this as a 
serious irregularity in the procurement policy and process. The Committee, therefore, strongly 
recommends for instituting an enquiry into the matter and accordingly apprises the Committee 
about the reasons and compelling circumstances under which the dialysis machines were 
purchased at such higher prices in the new AIIMS. 
(Para 9.55 of the report) 
Action Taken 
 
3.44.2 Observations / Recommendations of the Committee have been noted for compliance.  
It is submitted that an Enquiry committee has also been set up for submitting the report by 
30.04.2019. The committee constituted to look into the issue ha d submitted its report. 
However, on examination it was observed that the committee had not considered all relevant 
aspects. The committee has been advised to exam ine the matter further, and re -submit their 
report. AIIMS are providing round the clock hemodialysis services to the patients. 
 
Further Recommendation 
 
3.44.3 The Committee expresses concern over the incomplete assessment by the initial 
enquiry committee constituted to examine the issue of procurement of Dialysis machine 
at a much higher price in t he new AIIMS , and in view thereof, the Committee 

196 
 
recommends that the Ministry expedite the re -examination and ensure the resubmission 
of a comprehensive and conclusive report on the procurement of dialysis machines at 
inflated costs. The revised enquiry must include a comparative cost analysis with similar 
procurements by State Governments and under MPLAD schemes, evaluation of 
procurement procedures followed, and identification of any lapses or accountability 
issues. The Committee also stresses that measur es be instituted to strengthen 
transparency, cost-efficiency, and oversight in future procurements across all AIIMS to 
prevent recurrence of any irregularities.

AIIMS PATNA 
 
4.1 OPERATION THEATRES 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
4.1.1 From the data submitted by AIIMS Patna, the Committee observes with deep concern 
that AIIMS, Patna has conducted on an avera ge 10 surgeries per day in its 13 Operation 
Theatres. Moreover, only 506 emergency surgeries have been conducted. The Committee 
finds that these 13 OTs remain highly underutilized although AIIMS Patna cited limited 
availability of OTs as the reason behind the waiting time in surgical procedures. The 
Committee also recommends to AIIMS, Patna for planning higher number of surgeries to be 
carried out in a day so that one OT can be used for more than one surgery in a day, thus, 
resulting in optimum utilisation of the Operation Theatre. 
(Para 5.60 of the Report) 
Action Taken 
 
4.1.2 The observation / recommendation of the Hon‘ble Committee has been noted  for 
future guidance. Many of the surgeries carried out in the OT‘s are major surgeries and take 
more time than normal routine surgeries. Moreover, many minor surgeries are performed in 
OPD‘s, the number of which is not taken into account while submitting the data. It is also to 
submit that with increase in no of OT‘s and ward, the number of surgeries will increase.  The 
use of O.T. will also be optimized.  As present there are 28 OTs functional at AIIMS Patna 
and 17 Minor OTs. No. of surgeries performed have also increased now which shall be clear 
from the following table:- 
Month Major Minor Total 
Sept 21 984 159 1143 
Oct 21 900 116 1016 
Nov 21 928 106 1034 
 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
4.1.3 As per the information submitted by the Ministry, AIIMS Patna has conducted 4224 
major and minor surgeries till September, 2018, the Committee is displeased with such huge 
variation in the data and would like AIIMS Patna to submit precise data with respect to 
surgical procedures (patient wise and Department wise) till date, since inception of the 
Institute, within three months of presentation of this Report. 
 (Para 5.61 of the Report) 

198 
 
Action Taken 
 
4.1.4 As directed by the Committee, a detailed report will be submitted soon. Regarding the 
number of procedures per OT, it is pertinent to point out that number of cases  being done in 
an operation theatre is dependent on the nature  and complexity of cases with more complex 
cases taking a much longer time. However, it is informed that total 6271 surgical procedures 
were performed at AIIMS Patna in 2018.

make extra efforts to build itself into a world class institute as envisioned while establishing 
the institute. 
(Para 5.78 of the Report)

AIIMS RAIPUR 
4.3 STATUS OF CONSTRUCTION 
Recommendations/Observations  
 
4.3.1 The Committee observes that the AIIMS, Raipur has failed in evaluating the 
requirement for the residential accommodation wh ile planning the Phase II expansion 
program. The planning process for expansion started in March, 2015 and continued till May, 
2018 with the submission of detailed drawings of the project. By then, it was realized that 
there is a need to revive the entire plan. The Committee is appalled at the laid back approach 
of the Institute in execution of the project. The changes in the design at such a later stage 
would have a negative impact on the expansion program, in terms of additional resources, 
cost and extended project duration. It seems to the Committee that obviously the Institute had 
not conducted any proper study before planning the expansion of phase II residential 
complex. The preparation of revised drawing, revised detailed estimates and tender 
documents would require additional time resulting in cost and time overruns. The Committee, 
therefore, underlines the need for proper planning and evaluation by engaging reputed 
agencies before undertaking any construction projects. 
 (Para 6.16 of the Report) 
 
 

199
