RECOMMENDATIONS/OBSERVATIONS  - AT   A  GLANCE 
 
STATUS OF FACULTY/NON FACULTY STRENGTH 
1. The Committee notes the progress made by filling up of 232 posts out of 305 
sanctioned faculty posts in AIIMS Bhopal, but the Committee expresses conc ern that 73 
vacancies still persist and that some appointments (e.g. Nursing Principal) are delayed 
due to procedural hurdles. In order to secure full, sustained staffing, the Commit tee 
recommends that AIIMS Bhopal, in coordination with the Ministry should further 
undertake followings actions for strengthening faculty and non - faculty of AIMS 
Bhopal: (a) set a deadline for completing all remaining regular recruitments and 
declaring results of pending cases; (b) convert existing contractual and one -level-below 
appointments into permanent posts as soon as eligibility is met; (c) institute a joint 
quarterly review mechanism to monitor progress against recruitments targets; and (d) 
actively partner with premier medical institutes to broaden the candidate pool, thereby 
ensuring uninterrupted teaching, research and patient-care functions. 
(Para 3.1.5 of the ATR) 
2. The Committee observe lackadaisical approach towards filling up of non -faculty 
posts at AIIMS Bhopal, particularly in the nursing cadre. Accordingly, the Committee 
remains deeply concerned towards the persistent large number of vacancies and the 
over-reliance of the institute on outsourcing. Since the 3776 sanctioned non -faculty posts 
are not filled up through regular recruitment, core institutional functions are being 
compromised. The Committee, therefore, reiterates its recommendation for the Ministry 
to operationalize a robust, time-bound recruitment mechanism for all technical and non-
technical non -faculty posts, ensuring uniformity and transparency across AIIMS. The 
Committee further recommends that posts proposed to be surrendered be reviewed with 
caution to avoid under -staffing in critical areas, and that all direct recruitment  and 
deputation-based appointments currently under process be concluded, at the earliest. 
(Para 3.1.10 of the ATR) 
SPECIALITY/SUPER SPECIALITY DEPARTMENTS 
 
3. The Committee acknowledges the Ministry‟s ongoing recruitment efforts and 
policy interventions ai med at addressing faculty shortages across AIIMS institutions, 
including AIIMS Bhopal. However, the Committee remains concerned that despite these 
measures, critical gaps persist in the Specialty and Super Specialty Departments, many 
of which continue to f unction with only one faculty member. The Committee, therefore, 
recommends that AIIMS Bhopal, in close coordination with the Ministry, implement a 
targeted recruitment plan specifically for Super Specialty Departments, with defined 
timelines and deliverabl es. The Committee also urges that the previously suggested 
measures such as offering special incentives, permitting lateral entry of experienced 
specialists from leading institutes be operationalized without delay. 
 (Para 3.2.5 of the ATR) 

203 
 
MEDICAL EDUCATION 
 
4. The Committee notes significant progress in increasing student intake across 
various courses at AIIMS Bhopal, including MBBS, B.Sc Nursing, MD/MS, and the 
successful initiation of numerous new PDCC, DM, M.Ch, and PhD programs. This 
demonstrates a proactive approach to expanding educational and healthcare capacities. 
However, the continued vacancy in certain super -specialty departments like Cardiology, 
Nephrology, and Surgical Oncology remains a concern for the Committee. While the 
Ministry attributes this to an all-India phenomenon, it is crucial to further analyses and 
make detailed assessment by the concerned authority and take appropriate step for 
fulfilling up vacant post in various departments. Moreover, if any specific factors at 
AIIMS Bhopal con tribute to these vacancies, in order to develop targeted strategies in 
coordination with AIIMS Delhi, AIIMS Bhopal must address these gaps, ensuring 
timely recruitment of sanctioned seats and optimal utilization of resources, particularly 
in high-demand and specialized departments. 
(Para 3.3.5 of the ATR) 
 
5. The Committee appreciates positive steps taken to address the Committee‟s 
concerns regarding inadequate clinical exposure and faculty shortages, notably through 
a substantial increase in faculty number s (from 53 in 2017 to 249 as of 12/02/25), 
expansion of MD/MS courses to 25 departments, and the commencement of PDCC, DM, 
M.Ch, and PhD programs. The improved National Institutional Ranking Framework 
(NIRF) ranking to 31st in 2024 further supports the cla im of enhanced educational 
quality. While these are commendable achievements demonstrating a commitment to 
providing a "well-rounded medical education," the Committee further recommends that 
the Ministry should provide specific metrics, such as patient -to-student ratios, bed 
occupancy rates, and a breakdown of surgical cases or specialized procedures students 
are exposed to across various departments. Such data would offer concrete evidence of 
adequate clinical material and validate the claim of "extensive clinical exposure," 
ensuring that the qualitative improvements translate into demonstrably high levels of 
practical skills for all medical and nursing graduates. 
(Para 3.3.10 of the ATR) 
6. The Committee commends AIIMS Bhopal for initiating paramedical (Al lied 
Health Sciences) courses and expanding nursing education programmes, including the 
launch of B.Sc., M.Sc., and Ph.D. level courses. This marks a significant step toward 
addressing the shortage of trained technical manpower and optimizing the use of 
available infrastructure. However, the Committee recommends that the Ministry and 
the Institute should further strengthen these efforts by expanding the range of Allied 
Health Science  disciplines offered, increasing seat capacity based on projected 
healthcare needs, and ensuring timely faculty recruitment for these programmes. The 
Committee also recommends the establishment of a dedicated Allied Health Sciences 
faculty or school within AIIMS Bhopal for better academic oversight, curriculum 
development, and in tegration with clinical services. A long -term roadmap for Allied 

204 
 
Health Science education expansion should be taken up on fast track mode to produce 
timely and adequately competent technical work force in various clinical fields. Such 
steps would make the hospital infrastructure capable of providing world class 
healthcare besides opening opportunity for employment -generation through expansion 
of Medical field. 
(Para 3.3.14 of the ATR) 
OPERATION THEATRES 
7. The Committee acknowledges the Ministry‟s clarifica tion that all 27 Major OTs 
(including 24 Modular OTs) and 8 Minor OTs at AIIMS Bhopal are now fully 
functional, and appreciates the substantial volume of surgeries being conducted 
annually. However, the Committee is worried that despite the operational rea diness of 
OTs, long waiting periods for surgical procedures persist due to high patient load. The 
Committee, therefore, recommends that AIIMS Bhopal undertake a comprehensive 
audit of OT utilization, manpower adequacy, and scheduling efficiency to identify  
systemic bottlenecks to overcome the issue of long waiting time for surgeries at AIIMS 
Bhopal. In this regard, the Institute should consider extending OT hours, recruiting 
additional surgical staff, and streamlining pre -operative processes to improve outp ut. 
The Committee also urges the Ministry to explore the feasibility of establishing satellite 
surgical centres or partnerships with nearby government hospitals to offload elective 
surgeries and reduce waiting times. 
(Para 3.4.3 of the ATR) 
STATUS OF INTENSIVE CARE UNITS AND TRAUMA/EMERGENCY FACILITIES 
8. The Committee notes the significant progress in enhancing trauma and 
emergency services at AIIMS Bhopal, particularly with the increase to 12 faculty 
members and 78 functional beds, including 20 ventilator -supported beds. The overall 
Intensive Care Unit (ICU) bed count has increased to 137. A breakdown of these beds 
into specialized units like Trauma, Cardiology, ENT, Neurology, Urology, etc.,  address 
the targeted functionality and the specific need for sp ecialized critical care. This 
specialization optimizes patient care by providing tailored environments and expertise 
for complex medical conditions. The Committee further recommends that the separate 
ICUs should be expanded to other remaining specialisatio ns also and their numbers in 
Urology and Trauma should be adequately increased for taking specialised critical 
healthcare. 
(Para 3.5.3 of the ATR) 
DIALYSIS FACILITIES 
9. The Committee appreciates the efforts made by AIIMS Bhopal to strengthen its 
Nephrology services, including the increase in the number of functional dialysis 
machines from two to eleven across Nephrology, Paediatrics, and ICU. However, the 
Committee emphasizes that enhancing equipment capacity must be matched with 

205 
 
adequate and specialized h uman resources to ensure quality service delivery. In its 
reply, the Ministry stated that the faculty posts have been advertised in Department of 
Nephrology in 2019, however, the status of recruitment through that advertisement and 
the present status of va cant posts should have been informed to the Committee. The 
Committee further recommends that the Institute should expedite all pending 
recruitment of qualified nephrologists, dialysis technicians, and nursing staff dedicated 
to renal care. It also urges the Ministry to support the development of a dedicated Renal 
Care Unit with integrated diagnostic, therapeutic, and training facilities. 
(Para 3.6.3 of the ATR)  
 
MERGER OF BHOPAL MEMORIAL HOSPITAL & RESEARCH CENTRE 
(BMHRC) AND AIIMS, BHOPAL 
 
10. The Committ ee recapitulates the positive response of the Ministry w.r.t. the 
merger of BMHRC with AIIMS Bhopal before the Committee during its discussion on 
the subject. The Committee, therefore, reiterates its strong conviction that the merger of 
BMHRC with AIIMS Bh opal is in larger public interest and would ensure optimum 
utilization of resources, better faculty retention, and expanded training and teaching 
opportunities, particularly while continuing to cater to the needs of gas tragedy victims. 
While noting that t he Department of Health Research has decided against the merger, 
the Committee urges the Department of Health and Family Welfare to revisit this 
decision in consultation with all stakeholders, keeping in view the long -term benefits for 
patients, medical fa culty, and students. The Committee, therefore, recommends the 
Department of Health and Family Welfare to devise a concrete roadmap and time -
bound plan for resolving administrative hurdles and ensuring that the merger, along 
with protection of service condi tions of existing BMHRC doctors during the transition, 
in the interest of public health and medical education. 
(Para 3.7.4 of the ATR) 
STATUS OF FACULTY/NON FACULTY STRENGTH 
11. The Committee acknowledges the efforts made by AIIMS Rishikesh and the 
Ministry to enhance faculty recruitment, including the steps initiated through the 
Central Institute Body (CIB) and the ongoing policy interventions. However, the 
Committee expresses the concern that despite repeated advertisements, approximately 
one-third of the  sanctioned faculty posts remain vacant, and recent recruitments were 
delayed due to the revision of the reservation roster. The Committee, therefore, 
recommends that AIIMS Rishikesh must urgently finalize and operationalize the revised 
reservation roster and ensure that the pending faculty recruitment advertisement is 
issued without further delay. To avoid recurring procedural setbacks, the Committee 
further advises instituting a streamlined and time -bound recruitment protocol. Regular 
biannual recruitment cycles, as envisaged by CIB, must be strictly adhered to, and the 
progress should be reviewed quarterly by the Ministry. Further, the Committee would 
like to have a status report on efforts taken by  AIIMS Rishikesh and the Ministry 
regarding the operation alization of revised reservation roster and latest recruitment 
advertisement issued towards filling up of sanctioned posts of faculties. 
(Para 3.8.4 of the ATR) 

206 
 
 
12. The Committee takes note of the Ministry‟s and AIIMS Rishikesh‟s ongoing 
efforts to strengthen the Specialty and Super Specialty Departments, including progress 
in departments such as Gastroenterology, Transfusion Medicine, and Biochemistry. 
However, the Committee is concerned that critical vacancies still persist in key 
departments such as Phy sical Medicine & Rehabilitation (PMR) and Biochemistry, and 
that faculty recruitment overall continues to lag despite repeated advertisements. The 
Committee, therefore, reiterates its recommendation that AIIMS Rishikesh urgently 
prioritize the filling up o f all senior faculty positions, especially Professors, through 
time-bound recruitment drives, while exploring targeted strategies such as inter -
institutional deputation, lateral entry of experienced specialists, and incentivized 
appointments from leading i nstitutes like AIIMS Delhi, JIPMER, and PGIMER. 
Further, the Committee emphasizes that any plan for institutional expansion must be 
accompanied by demonstrable improvements in faculty strength, infrastructure 
utilization, and patient load. 
(Para 3.8.8 of the ATR) 
MEDICAL EDUCATION 
13. The Ministry's response, stating that the Committee's observations have been 
"noted for future guidance" and that the Institute has been "sensitized" and is "trying 
to implement all suggestions," lacks specific details or a co ncrete action plan to address 
the Committee's serious concerns regarding the potential dilution of medical education 
standards at AIIMS Rishikesh. The Committee's core apprehension centres on the 
perceived misinterpretation and misuse of AIIMS Act autonomy  to justify not adhering 
to MCI /NMC benchmarks, thereby raising questions about the quality of learning and 
clinical experience. Therefore, the Committee strongly recommends that the Ministry 
provide a detailed, time -bound action plan outlining how AIIMS Rishikesh will be 
brought into full compliance with MCI/NMC norms for faculty -student ratios, 
infrastructure, and clinical exposure, particularly for postgraduate seats, without 
compromising the autonomy under the AIIMS Act, but rather enhancing, the quali ty of 
medical education. 
(Para 3.9.3 of the ATR) 
OPERATION THEATERS (OTS) AND TRAUMA & EMERGENCY CENTER 
14. The Committee appreciates the progress made by AIIMS Rishikesh in 
operationalizing a total of 54 Operation Theatres, including 25 Major and 29 Minor  
OTs, which marks a significant improvement in surgical infrastructure. However, the 
Committee emphasizes that full functionality must translate into tangible reductions in 
patient waiting times across all surgical departments. The Committee, therefore, 
reiterates that AIIMS Rishikesh conduct and submit, without delay, a detailed 
comparative analysis with AIIMS Delhi on OT utilization efficiency, including the 
number of surgeries (major and minor) performed per OT per day. The Committee 
further recommends t hat a performance audit be undertaken to assess scheduling 

207 
 
practices, manpower deployment, and procedural turnaround times to ensure optimum 
utilization of available OT infrastructure. The Committee reiterates that any future 
expansion plans must be substa ntiated by due justification evident with supporting data 
showing improved usage of existing facilities and reduced surgical backlogs. 
(Para 3.10.3 of the ATR) 
 
15. The Ministry's response details significant progress in addressing the 
Committee's concerns  regarding disaster preparedness at AIIMS Rishikesh, with the 
Trauma Building now fully functional with 50 beds, the helipad operational, and the 
commencement of Heli Emergency Medical Services (HEMS). This proactive approach 
to establishing critical infra structure and services for trauma and disaster management 
is commendable, especially given Uttarakhand's susceptibility to natural disasters. The 
Committee, however, recommends that the Ministry of Health and Family Welfare, in 
tandem with Ministry of Home  Affairs, must now focus on conducting regular, large -
scale multi -agency disaster drills and simulations involving local and state emergency 
services, as well as other relevant stakeholders. The Committee also desires that the 
Ministry should make introspe ction to specifically test the hospital's capacity in 
managing "huge numbers of casualties" in recent disaster in the states and accordingly 
refine coordination protocols to further enhance preparedness and response capabilities 
for future contingencies. 
(Para 3.10.6 of the ATR) 
16. The Committee takes note of the steps undertaken by AIIMS Jodhpur to ensure 
transparency in procurement, including the use of e -tendering and publication of 
tenders on the CPPP portal, as well as adherence to relevant provisions  of the General 
Financial Rules (GFRs) in cases where single bid procurement was undertaken. 
However, the Committee is of the view that instances of procurement through single 
bids should have been avoided and the institute must adhere to multiple tenderin g 
attempts. The Committee feels the need for more proactive vendor outreach and 
competitive specification design to reduce cost of procurement of components. The 
Committee, therefore, recommends that AIIMS Jodhpur should also develop a 
structured vendor en gagement strategy, including pre -bid meetings, to increase 
participation. Moreover, the Committee desires that all single bid and proprietary 
procurements should be independently reviewed on a half -yearly basis by an internal 
audit committee, and the summa ry of such reviews, including justification and cost 
comparisons with peer institutions, should be submitted to the Ministry. A 
comprehensive status report on the findings of the earlier probe into single bid 
procurements, as previously recommended, should  also be submitted for consideration 
of Committee. 
(Para 3.11.3 of the ATR)

208 
 
drives and the introduction of combined contractual recruitment through AIIMS New

and guidance is critical to institutional performance and academic quality. The

be set with quarterly progress monitoring. Further, the policy of appointing candidates 
under contract & one level below the sanctioned post should be only a temporary 
stopgap measure/management, and not a substitute for attracti ng high -calibre talent. 
The Committee, in this regard, recommends the creation of a centralized digital 
recruitment dashboard across all AIIMS to track applications, selections, rejections 
(with reasons), and origin of candidates (including those from AIIM S 
Delhi/JIPMER/PGI). This would not only enhance transparency but also help refine

with reasons for their rejection to be submitted in Action Taken Notes of present Report 
to the Committee for consideration.

PROCUREMENT OF MEDICAL EQUIPMENT 
23. The Committee takes note of the updated status indicating progress in the 
delivery and installation of most of the previously undelivered and uninstalled medical 
equipment at AIIMS Patn a. The Committee, however, expresses serious concerns over 
the fact that some critical equipment remains undelivered or uninstalled due to 
unresolved issues between the Procurement Support Agency (HITES) and vendors 
reflects persistent systemic inefficienc ies. The Committee, therefore, recommends that 
the Ministry should monitor and co -ordinate in a time -bound manner for resolution of 
issues pertaining to procurement and installation delays, indicating defined 
accountability for both the Institute and the P SA. A periodic review system should be 
institutionalized, involving all stakeholders, to ensure proactive site readiness, 
infrastructure support, and timely vendor coordination. The Ministry should consider 
developing a centralized dashboard for real -time tracking of equipment procurement 
and installation across all AIIMS. 
(Para 3.17.4 of the ATR) 
24. The Committee notes the efforts being made to impress upon HITES for early 
delivery and installation, along with the imposition of penalties for delays at AII MS 
Patna. Furthermore, the provision of skilled manpower for installation and the coverage 
of equipment under 5 -year warranty and subsequent 5 -year Comprehensive 
Maintenance Agreements are positive steps towards ensuring operation functionality 
and profici ency. However, the reply does not explicitly address the Committee‟s 
recommendation for a technical inspection by a technical inspector before acceptance of 
equipment, nor does it details a comprehensive contingency plan in case of equipment 
breakdown to p revent adverse effects on diagnostic services. Therefore, the Committee 
reiterates that the Ministry, in addition to existing measures, establish a mandatory 
protocol for independent technical inspection and certification of all medical equipment 
prior to acceptance to ensure quality and functionality at the time of delivery. The 
Committee reiterates that a detailed contingency plan for critical equipment 
breakdowns should be developed at AIIMS Patna outlining alternative arrangements to 
minimize disruption to patient diagnostic and treatment services. 
(Para 3.17.7 of the ATR)

211 
 
inclusive procurement policy across all AIIMS and mandate that procurement

foreign certifications. 
(Para 3.17.10 of the ATR) 
 
26. In view of the action taken by the Ministry and the ongoing efforts by the Bureau

However, to ensure that these efforts translate into real -time procurement reform, the 
Committee recommends that AIIMS Patna and all other similar institutions strictly 
discontinue the practice of mandating US FDA certification where BIS or C E standards 
are available. Additionally, the Committee recommends monitoring of procurement 
patterns across AIIMS institutions to ensure preference for indigenous and BIS-certified 
products.

academic environment, and geographical location. Based on these findings, the Ministry 
should develop and implement targeted strategies, which could include enhanced 
incentives, improved institutional support for research and academic pursuits, or 
specialized outreach programs, to at tract and retain experienced medical professionals 
to AIIMS Raipur. 
(Para 3.24.3 of the ATR) 
42. The Committee notes that the Ministry acknowledges the Committee's concern 
regarding the high reliance on contractual and outsourced non -faculty staff at AIIMS  
Raipur. A significant number of candidates have joined through direct recruitment in 
2024 across Group A, B, and C, and that 1627 nursing staff are already on permanent 
roles. The Ministry explicitly states that attempts are made to fill up unfilled posts  on 
contractual basis to run the services and strengthen department," indicating that 
contractual arrangements remain a necessity due to persistent vacancies. While 
acknowledging the operational imperative, nevertheless the Committee is of the view 
that the Ministry should develop a comprehensive, time-bound strategy to systematically 
reduce the reliance on contractual and outsourced non -faculty staff at AIIMS Raipur 
and ultimately to do away with such methods of ad -hoc recruitment. Rather the strategy 
should be more focussed on frequent direct recruitment drives for all non -faculty cadres 
and addressing the underlying reasons for permanent positions remaining vacant, such 
as potentially uncompetitive salaries or benefits for specific roles, to ensure long -term 
job security and foster a sense of belongingness among the workforce, thereby 
enhancing the overall stability, quality and professional proficiency of healthcare 
services. 
(Para 3.24.6 of the ATR) 
SPECIALTY AND SUPER SPECIALTY SERVICES 
43. The Committe e notes that the Ministry, in its response to the critical faculty 
vacancies, particularly, in specialty and super -specialty departments at AIIMS Raipur, 
largely reiterates the ongoing efforts viz. including continuous recruitment drives, 
contractual appoi ntments, and the implementation of a rolling advertisement system. 
While the completion of recruitment for 169 faculty posts and the ongoing process for 
129 more (with 34 selections) are positive indicators of effort, nevertheless, the persistent 
high vaca ncy rates, especially in departments being run by a single faculty, pin -point 
that current strategies deployed by AIIMS management are not fully addressing the 
underlying challenges of attracting and retaining specialized medical professionals. The 
Committee, therefore, further recommends that besides normal recruitment efforts, the 
Ministry, in conjunction with AIIMS Raipur management must undertake a targeted, 

217 
 
department-specific analysis for 16 departments with over 50% vacancies, particularly 
those operated by a single faculty. 
(Para 3.25.3 of the ATR) 
 
 
44. The Committee acknowledges the efforts made by AIIMS Raipur to 
operationalize crucial Super Specialty Departments, with the result  that Department of 
Nephrology, Surgical Gastroenterology, Medical On cology & Haematology are now 
functional. The ongoing recruitment drives, including the preference given to Super -
Specialty departments in interviews and initiatives like the Visiting Faculty Scheme, 
demonstrate a commitment to adequate staffing. However, t he reply highlights the 
persistent challenge of making Surgical Oncology fully functional due to a lack of 
suitable applicants or candidates joining. The Committee further recommends that the 
Ministry must direct AIIMS Raipur management to conduct a target ed analysis to 
understand the specific deterrents for super -specialists in Surgical Oncology. The 
Ministry should develop a tailored, aggressive recruitment strategy for Surgical 
Oncology, which could include offering enhanced incentives, dedicated researc h grants, 
specialized training opportunities, to ensure that this vital department becomes fully 
functional with adequate specialized faculty without further delay, thereby ensuring 
comprehensive cancer care. 
(Para 3.25.6 of the ATR) 
PROCUREMENT OF EQUIPMENT 
45. The Committee has been informed that AIIMS Raipur generally adheres to best 
practices by purchasing equipment only when infrastructure and manpower are 
available, and by ensuring warranty periods commence post -installation, with 
provisions for liqui dated damages. This is a positive step towards prudent equipment 
management.  However, the submission that "few of the equipment could not be 
installed where the designated area of the departments concerned was not handed over 
timely by the project contrac tors" underscores a critical gap in coordination between 
procurement and infrastructure development. The Committee, therefore, further 
recommends that the Ministry implement a more robust, inter -departmental 
coordination mechanism between the procurement w ing and the project/construction 
department at AIIMS Raipur. Such mechanism should include mandatory, pre -
procurement verification of infrastructure readiness and a staggered procurement 
schedule explicitly linked to the confirmed handover dates of designa ted departmental 
areas, thereby preventing future instances where costly equipment remains uninstalled 
due to construction delays. 
(Para 3.26.3 of the ATR) 
 
46. The Committee has been apprised that the Committee‟s concern over delays in 
equipment delivery and installation has been addressed by imposing liquidated damages 
on errant suppliers and that AIIMS Raipur generally ensures infrastructure and 
manpower are available before procurement. The Committee, however, recommends 
that the Ministry, beside imposi ng penalties must establish a more integrated and 
proactive system to ensure seamless coordination between procurement, infrastructure 

218 
 
development, and departmental readiness at AIIMS Raipur. This system should 
mandate a pre -procurement checklist and real -time monitoring of project completion 
milestones, enabling a "just-in-time" equipment delivery and installation strategy.  
(Para 3.26.6 of the ATR) 
MEDICAL EDUCATION 
47. The Committee acknowledges the Ministry‟s explanation that the persistent 
vacancies in  pre-clinical and para -clinical departments are largely due to candidates‟ 
preference for clinical specialties offering wider job opportunities. The Committee, 
while noting the submission of the Ministry, emphasizes that sustained underutilization 
of sanct ioned PG seats defeats the very objective of expanding medical education 
capacity. The Committee, therefore, recommends that the Ministry, in consultation with 
the National Medical Commission (NMC), explore remedial measures such as offering 
structured career pathways, research incentives, fellowships, or enhanced academic and 
institutional opportunities in pre -clinical and para -clinical disciplines to make them 
more attractive to aspirants. The Committee, therefore, recommends the Ministry for 
periodic ass essment of demand trends across disciplines and corrective action so that 
sanctioned seats are optimally utilized and do not remain vacant year after year. 
(Para 3.27.3 of the ATR) 
AYUSH 
48. The Committee appreciates the progress made by AIIMS Patna in exp anding 
AYUSH services, including the initiation of OPD and IPD services and the appointment 
of permanent Medical Officers in various AYUSH disciplines. However, the Committee 
notes with concern that Unani services remain non -operational due to a vacancy, a nd 
the Department overall still lacks the full sanctioned strength needed for optimal 
functioning. Given the increasing demand for holistic and cost -effective healthcare 
solutions, the Committee reiterates the importance of ensuring all five AYUSH systems 
are fully operational. Therefore, the Committee further recommends that AIIMS 
Raipur expedite the recruitment process for the vacant Unani Medical Officer post and 
ensure that all sanctioned posts under AYUSH are filled on a regular basis. 
(Para 3.28.4 of the ATR) 
BUDGETARY ALLOCATION 
49. The Committee takes note of the significant improvement in fund utilization at 
AIIMS Bhubaneswar in recent years, particularly the near full utilization of capital 
grants in 2018-19, 2019-20, 2020-21, and 2023-24. However, the Committee observes that 
in 2021 -22 and 2022 -23, utilization again dipped to below 75 percent, which points 
towards persisting gaps in financial planning and project execution. While the 
implementation of TSA has addressed the issue of surplus funds ly ing idle, the 
Committee recommends that the Ministry ensure a robust mechanism for real -time 
monitoring of fund flow, project progress, and timely corrective action, and 
institutionalize periodic financial audits and  capacity -building of its financial 

219 
 
management systems to prevent recurrence of underutilization and consistency in fiscal 
discipline. 
(Para 3.29.3 of the ATR) 
50. The Committee notes with satisfaction that regular financial audits of AIIMS 
Bhubaneswar are now being conducted by the CAG and tha t the audit reports from 
2018-19 to 2023 -24 have been received and incorporated in the Annual Reports. While 
this is a welcome step towards ensuring financial accountability, the Committee 
recommends that the Ministry ensure timely completion and disclosur e of audits every 
financial year without delay. The Committee also stresses that audit observations, if any, 
must be acted upon promptly with a clear compliance mechanism and periodic 
reporting to avoid recurrence of lapses. 
(Para 3.29.6 of the ATR) 
STATUS OF CONSTRUCTION WORK 
51. The Committee takes into account the updated status of construction packages at 
AIIMS Bhubaneswar and acknowledges that most of the packages have been completed, 
though with significant delays and cost overruns. However, the pende ncy in Package II, 
particularly the sub -judice matter related to IBMS (Integrated Building Management 
System), remains a matter of concern as it continues to hold back full and optimal 
operationalization of the facilities. The Committee, therefore, reitera tes that the 
Ministry must take expeditious steps to resolve the pending issues, including legal 
hurdles, and ensure that all components of Package II are completed at the earliest. 
Further, the Committee emphasizes the need for fixing accountability on no n-
performing agencies to prevent recurrence of such delays and for instituting a robust 
monitoring and penalty mechanism in future projects to minimize both time and cost 
overruns. 
(Para 3.30.3 of the ATR) 
STATUS OF FACULTY AND NON-FACULTY STRENGTH 
52. The Committee acknowledges the proactive efforts undertaken by AIIMS 
Bhubaneswar to address faculty and non -faculty shortages through repeated 
advertisements and the use of centralized recruitment mechanisms like NORCET. The 
Committee, however, expresses deep  concern over the continuing high vacancies at 
various levels, particularly, with 78 vacancies out of 315 faculty posts, 222 vacancies out 
of 748 sanctioned posts at non -faculty level (SRs & JRs) and 865 vacancies out of total 
sanctioned strength of 3082 p osts of non -faculty (excluding SRs & JRs) still remains to 
be filled up in AIIMS Bhubaneswar. The Committee emphasizes that persistent gaps in 
critical human resources, especially senior faculty and Group A positions, can severely 
undermine institutional p erformance, training quality, and patient care. Therefore, the 
Committee further recommends that AIIMS Bhubaneswar devise a time -bound 
recruitment strategy with specific targets to fill up all key vacancies existing at (78) 
faculty, (222) non -faculty (SRs & JRs),  and (865) non -faculty (excluding SRs & JRs) 

220 
 
levels including alternative mechanisms such as lateral entry or contractual 
appointments as the interim measure. Additionally, the Institute should explore 
incentives to attract talent, strengthen feeder cadres for promotional posts. 
(Para 3.31.4 of the ATR)   
SPECIALITY/SUPER SPECIALITY DEPARTMENTS 
53. The Committee appreciates that all 18 Specialty and 17 Super -Specialty 
departments at AIIMS Bhubaneswar are currently functional, however, the Committee 
remains to be apprised of faculty status, particularly in critical departments such as 
Nephrology, Endocrinology, Surgical Oncology, and Plastic Surgery. The Committee is 
of the considered view that functionality without adequate staffing attracts risks of  
compromising the quality of care, overburdening existing personnel, and affecting the 
academic environment. The Committee, therefore, further recommends that the 
Institute undertake an urgent, department-wise assessment to identify high-burden units 
operating with suboptimal staffing. Priority recruitment drives must be conducted for 
these departments, alongside strategic faculty deployment, visiting consultant 
arrangements, and collaborations with other AIIMS or institutions to ensure 
uninterrupted services and academic training. 
(Para 3.32.3 of the ATR)  
STATUS OF OPERATION THEATRES AND WARDS/BEDS 
54. The Committee appreciates the significant progress made by AIIMS 
Bhubaneswar in operationalizing all 25 sanctioned Modular Operation Theatres (MoTs) 
and ens uring the installation of the Medical Gas Pipeline System (MGPS), thereby 
addressing a critical infrastructure gap. However, the Committee notes that despite the 
sanction of 112 ICU beds in the main hospital, 86 beds in the Trauma Centre, and 36 
beds in the Burn Centre, only a total of 130 ICU beds (112 + 12 trauma centre beds + 06 
burn centre beds) are currently functional and 30 burn centre beds remains to be 
installed. This indicates a considerable underutilization of sanctioned critical care 
infrastructure. The Committee, therefore, further recommends that the Institute 
expedite the process of making all remaining ICU beds functional by addressing any 
outstanding manpower, equipment, or logistical constraints. The Committee also desires 
that the Institut e may furnish details of occupancy rate of ICU beds in various 
wards/departments. 
(Para 3.33.3 of the ATR) 
AYUSH FACILITIES 
55. The Committee notes with satisfaction that the Institute has taken steps to fill up 
the sanctioned posts in the AYUSH Department , with five out of six posts now occupied, 
thereby reflecting progress in addressing the earlier manpower shortfall. However, the 
Committee also emphasizes that mere availability of posts is not sufficient unless backed 
by regular appointments, appropriate  infrastructure, and full operationalization of 

221 
 
services. Therefore, the Committee desires that the remaining vacancy be filled up on a 
priority basis through regular recruitment. Additionally, the Institute should ensure 
that all appointed personnel are provided with necessary facilities and support to deliver 
quality AYUSH services. The Committee also urges the Institute to develop a roadmap 
for expanding AYUSH services beyond OPD to include IPD and academic functions, 
aligning with the Ministry‟s broader  vision of integrated and regionally adapted 
traditional healthcare delivery. 
(Para 3.34.4 of the ATR) 
 
 
UPGRADATION OF GOVERNMENT MEDICAL COLLEGES/ INSTITUTIONS  
 
BUDGETARY ALLOCATION AND FUNDING PATTERN 
 
56. The Committee acknowledges the progress made by the Ministry in ensuring that 
almost all State Governments, barring Andhra Pradesh, have released their share of 
funds under Phase III of the PMSSY upgradation projects. However, the continued 
delay by the Government of Andhra Pradesh in releasing its sh are for the GMCs at 
Anantapur and Vijayawada remains a matter of concern. The Committee, therefore, 
recommends that the Ministry intensify its efforts by engaging at the highest 
administrative and political levels with the Government of Andhra Pradesh to e xpedite 
the release of the pending State share. Additionally, the Committee reiterates its earlier 
recommendation that a formal mechanism be institutionalized to monitor and report 
fund releases on a quarterly basis, including reasons for delays and follow -up actions 
taken, so as to ensure timely implementation of upgradation works and enable proactive 
intervention wherever needed. 
(Para 3.35.3 of the ATR) 
 
SITE SELECTION AND STATUS OF CONSTRUCTION ACTIVITIES IN GMCS 
 
57. The Committee appreciates the conce rted efforts made by the Ministry in 
coordinating with State Governments and relevant stakeholders to address the multiple 
causes of delays in construction activities at various Government Medical Colleges 
(GMCs), resulting in the completion of 71 out of 7 5 projects. However, the Committee 
expresses concern over the continued delays in the remaining four projects viz. PMCH, 
GMC (Surat) and GTBH (Delhi), particularly due to pending State share commitments 
and GMC (Cuttack) due to delayed submission of Detail ed Project Reports (DPRs). The 
Committee, therefore, strongly recommends that the Ministry should establish a fixed 
timeline in coordination with the concerned State Governments for resolving the 
outstanding issues in these four projects. A robust escalati on mechanism should also be 
instituted for projects facing chronic delays, including structured fortnightly reviews 
and submission of status updates to the Committee. Furthermore, resilient follow -up 
action is needed in the case of the Guru Teg Bahadur Hos pital project in Delhi, where 
financial commitments are still pending, to prevent further delay in project completion. 
(Para 3.36.6 of the ATR) 
 

222 
 
58. The Committee takes note of the reasons cited such as issues related to land 
availability and statutory cle arances as being largely under the purview of the State 
Governments and implementing agencies. The Committee, therefore, recommends that 
for future projects, the Ministry must ensure timely pre -approval protocols, including 
mandatory submission of encumbra nce-free land certificates and statutory clearances 
before initiating tendering or construction. 
(Para 3.36.7 of the ATR) 
PROCUREMENT OF EQUIPMENT 
59. The Ministry's Action Taken Notes, while acknowledging the equipment shortage 
at GMCs under PMSSY, primarily focuses on the current procurement status for Phase 
III, indicating that orders for 48 out of 141 pending items have been placed and the rest 
are under procurement. This response, however, completely omits addressing the 
Committee's serious concern ove r the wastage of public funds because of the fact that a 
total of 75 numbers of equipment worth Rs. 3.26 crore remained unaccounted for by 
both HLL and the GMCs concerned, as highlighted by the CAG. It also fails to address 
the underlying issues of delays in order placement, changes in equipment models, and 
lack of follow -up, or to outline any measures for fixing accountability for these lapses. 
Therefore, the Committee strongly reiterates its recommendation for a comprehensive 
and urgent remedial action pl an to recover or account for the 75 missing equipments as 
identified by the CAG, along with a detailed report on the investigation conducted to fix 
accountability for such lapse in public fund management. Furthermore, the Ministry 
must chalk out a concrete  strategy, with strict timelines and clear performance metrics, 
and streamline the entire procurement -to-installation process, establishing robust 
inventory management systems ensuring rigorous oversight and follow -up action with 
PSAs and GMCs to prevent future occurrences of such mismanagement. 
(Para 3.37.3 of the ATR) 
 
60. The Committee notes that its recommendation regarding avoidance of single bid 
procurement to encourage competitive pricing has been acknowledged by the Ministry. 
However, in view of the  significant financial outlay involved in medical equipment 
procurement and the need for transparency and efficiency in public procurement 
processes, the Committee recommends that the Ministry should issue detailed guidelines 
to all implementing agencies a nd procurement units under its purview, explicitly 
discouraging single bid procurement except in exceptional and justifiable circumstances. 
These exceptions should be subject to prior approval by a higher authority and duly 
documented. Additionally, the Co mmittee recommends that the Ministry must establish 
a monitoring mechanism to regularly review procurement cases, especially those 
finalized on a single bid basis. 
(Para 3.37.6 of the ATR) 
61. The Committee acknowledges that the Ministry has taken note of its 
recommendations and is reviewing the progress of equipment procurement alongside 

223 
 
construction activities with respect to all GMCs. The Committee, in this regard, 
recommends that a centralized digital dashboard be developed for real -time tracking of 
tender issuance, approval, delivery, installation, and operationalization of equipment 
across all GMCs. The effective monitoring mechanism must ensure that there should be 
no delay in procurement of medical equipments in any GMC. 
(Para 3.37.9 of the ATR) 
ENHANCEMENT OF PG SEATS 
62. The Committee appreciates the efforts in securing commitments from State 
Governments through MOUs for creating and filling up required faculty and non -
faculty posts, and its continued engagement with States through review meetings. 
However, the Committee expects that the Ministry should have furnished the 
development/progress made and outcome achieved of various steps taken, in this regard, 
during September 2019 to May 2025. Given that enhancement of PG seats without 
proportionate fa culty undermines the quality of medical education, the Committee 
recommends that the Ministry develop a structured timeline with performance 
indicators for faculty recruitment aligned with the increase in PG seats. Further, the 
Ministry should regularly mo nitor compliance by States and the status of faculty -
student ratios across GMCs on a quarterly basis. 
(Para 3.38.3 of the ATR) 
63. The Committee desires that the Ministry should have furnished the details of 
outcome of the efforts so made to collaborate wi th State Governments through MoUs 
and its continued engagement via review meetings to enhance PG seats in GMCs, 
especially during the period September 2019 to May 2025. The Committee, furthermore, 
recommends that a time -bound and target -driven roadmap be f inalized and 
implemented for each GMC, detailing the proposed increase in PG seats aligned with 
available infrastructure and faculty strength. The Committee further urges the Ministry 
to institute a central monitoring cell to track the progress of PG seat augmentation 
across all GMCs along with status of professional proficiency generated due to 
enhancement of PG seats in all GMCs. The Committee understands that GMCs are the 
gateway for delivery of adequate, accessible and affordable healthcare services at grass 
root level imparting medical education and clinical exposure at doors, and effective 
means of removing regional imbalances and critical medical education gap at source. 
The Ministry, in tandem with State Government, should chalk out „Action Plan‟ to 
achieve the said objectives. 
(Para 3.38.6 of the ATR) 
SUPER SPECIALTY BLOCKS 
 
64. The Committee takes note of the creation of 18 Cath Labs under Phase III in 
consultation with GMCs, Institutes, and State Governments. However, the Committee 
expresses the concern that due to budgetary limits, not all required Cath Labs could be 

224 
 
established, which may compromise the availability of critical cardiac care services in 
certain regions. The Committee, therefore, recommends that the Ministry, in 
coordination with the State Governments, should devise a phased plan to ensure that all 
medical colleges and institutions with super -specialty services are equipped with 
functional Cath Labs at the earliest. Additional financial support, either through 
enhanced central alloca tion, phased funding, Corporate Social Responsibility or 
convergence with other healthcare schemes, should be explored so that budgetary 
constraints do not hinder the provision of life-saving cardiac care infrastructure. 
(Para 3.39.3 of the ATR) 
GENERATION OF EMPLOYMENT 
65. The Committee acknowledges the communication with State Governments 
regarding manpower requirements, however, the Committee expresses concern that the 
Ministry has not furnished updated response covering details of progress made during 
September 2019 to May 2025 in compliance of recommendation for exploring huge 
employment generation opportunities in all GMCs identified for up -gradation under 
PMSSY. The Committee, therefore, recommends that a structured manpower audit be 
conducted for each GMC upgraded under PMSSY to assess current staffing gaps across 
faculty, non -faculty, healthcare allied and professional services, and support services. 
The Ministry should also set up a robust follow -up mechanism to ensure time -bound 
action by State Gov ernments on the creation and filling of posts, at various levels so as 
to open employment generation opportunities and ensuring that all departments of 
GMCs are made functional. 
(Para 3.40.3 of the ATR) 
STUDY VISIT OF THE COMMITTEE 
 
66. The Committee takes into account the progress made in strengthening healthcare 
infrastructure in Bihar through the establishment of new medical colleges at Purnia, 
Saran, and Samastipur as well as the initiation of AIIMS Darbhanga.  
(Para 3.41.6 of the ATR) 
67. The Committee, however, emphasizes that the Koshi/ Simanchal region and other 
underserved districts such as Saharsha, Madhepura, Katihar, Kishanganj, Araria and 
Farbishganj continue to remain deprived of adequate tertiary healthcare facilities. The 
Committee, therefore , recommends that the Ministry, in collaboration with the State 
Government, should prepare a comprehensive roadmap to ensure equitable distribution 
of tertiary healthcare services across all backward and flood -prone Koshi/Simanchal 
region of Bihar. The Com mittee understands that site selection for any AIIMS like 
Institution requires a detailed assessment of the geographical location and the 
demographic distribution. Easy accessibility and transportation availability is also the 
other criteria being taken in to consideration for setting up of AIIMS like institution.  
Moreover, air connectivity to the place has been one of the major basis for site selection 
for AIIMS. The Committee takes into consideration the poor human development index 

225 
 
in the Simanchal region  and poor ratio of doctor against the national average 
necessitates the setting up of AIIMS like institution in the Simanchal region.  The 
proposed expressway from Patna to Purnia and commencement of air connectivity to 
and fro Purnia has brought the Koshi region on national platform that fulfils the criteria 
of easy accessibility and transportation for setting up of AIIMS like institution. Further, 
being a backward and underserved region, Koshi/Simanchal region is the suitable site 
for establishment of AIIM S like institution to bridge the regional imbalance in 
healthcare access. The Committee, therefore, urges the Government for setting up of 
clear timelines for exploring the feasibility of setting up additional tertiary healthcare 
facilities through AIIMS like institution in the Koshi/Simanchal region. 
(Para 3.41.7 of the ATR) 
OBSERVATIONS/RECOMMENDATIONS ON COMMON ISSUES 
EXPANSION PROGRAMME OF AIIMS 
68. The Committee has been informed that the recommendation for excluding 
AIIMS-like institutions from or ame nding NBC guidelines to allow vertical expansion 
has been "noted for future guidance," and the matter has been taken up with the 
Ministry of Urban Development and NBCC. The mention of increased FAR for AIIMS 
Nagpur and Bilaspur is a positive sign, suggesti ng some progress in facilitating vertical 
growth. However, the communication with the Ministry of Housing and Urban Affairs 
dates back to March 2019, and the response lacks concrete timelines or definitive 
outcomes from the action taken so far, particularl y regarding a broader policy change 
that would benefit all AIIMS. The Committee, therefore, recommends that the Ministry 
of Health and Family Welfare must actively pursue a time -bound resolution with the 
Ministry of Housing and Urban Affairs regarding the proposed amendments to the 
National Building Code (NBC) guidelines for vertical expansion of hospital buildings in 
AIIMS-like initiatives, wherever required. 
(Para 3.42.3 of the ATR) 
SHORTAGE OF FACULTY AND NON FACULTY STAFF 
69. The Committee appreciates t he action taken by the Ministry and the CIB‟s 
endorsement of a centralized recruitment mechanism for non -faculty staff across all 
AIIMS. The Committee recommends that such centralized process be institutionalized 
with clear timelines, standard operating pr ocedures, and accountability mechanisms to 
ensure timely filling up of vacancies. While the delegation of specific technical cadres to 
individual AIIMS is a step forward, the Committee urges the Ministry to establish a 
unified digital recruitment platform under the supervision of AIIMS Delhi or a 
designated national agency to oversee coordination, monitoring, and transparency in 
recruitments. The Committee would like to be apprised of current filled up positions of 
non-faculty in all six AIIMS. The Ministry  should have furnished the details in its 
Action Taken Notes. 
(Para 3.43.4 of the ATR) 

226 
 
 
70. The Committee has been informed about the recruitment of faculty from premier 
medical institutions and the current vacancy status. In view thereof, the Committee 
recommends that a targeted and sustained strategy be formulated to attract and retain 
high-quality faculty from institutions like AIIMS Delhi, PGIMER, JIPMER, and others. 
The Committee further urges that each AIIMS -like institute regularly publish and 
update detailed recruitment data, including reasons for ineligibility and status of 
representations, on their websites in a transparent and standardized format. 
Additionally, the Ministry should explore incentive -based mechanisms such as 
accelerated career progr ession, housing support, or research funding to make positions 
in newer AIIMS institutions more attractive to faculty from premier institutes. 
(Para 3.43.8 of the ATR) 
DIALYSIS FACILITIES 
71. The Committee expresses concern over the incomplete assessment b y the initial 
enquiry committee constituted to examine the issue of procurement of Dialysis machine 
at a much higher price in the new AIIMS , and in view thereof, the Committee 
recommends that the Ministry expedite the re-examination and ensure the resubmission 
of a comprehensive and conclusive report on the procurement of dialysis machines at 
inflated costs. The revised enquiry must include a comparative cost analysis with similar 
procurements by State Go vernments and under MPLAD schemes, evaluation of 
procurement procedures followed, and identification of any lapses or accountability 
issues. The Committee also stresses that measures be instituted to strengthen 
transparency, cost-efficiency, and oversight in future procurements across all AIIMS to 
prevent recurrence of any irregularities. 
(Para 3.44.3 of the ATR)
